Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 9 
 Introduction 
The primary objective of this Concept of Operations (ConOps) is to establish a robust framework for 
the management and maintenance procedures essential to ensuring the sustainability, safety, and 
reliability of Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA). This framework integrates various systems, 
facilities, and services critical to seamless airport operations. By defining the roles and responsibilities 
of stakeholders and outlining procedures for both regular and emergency scenarios, the ConOps 
ensures optimal functionality under all conditions. 
Emphasizing both preventive and corrective maintenance, the ConOps aims to minimize operational 
disruptions, extend the lifespan of airport assets, and deliver a safe and pleasant experience for all 
users. It also encompasses relevant policies, regulations, and clearly defined roles and responsibilities. 
As a comprehensive guideline for stakeholders, this ConOps facilitates the establishment of effective 
management and maintenance procedures. It further identifies key performance indicators (KPIs) to 
evaluate the efficiency and effectiveness of maintenance activities. 
Ultimately, this ConOps is designed to enable LTIA to sustain its peak capacity, delivering reliable 
and safe services to passengers and stakeholders alike. 
1.1 Purpose 
This process provides a structured approach to maintenance, ensuring compliance with standards, 
detailed planning, and performance measurement to maintain system efficiency and reliability. 
 Management of Airport Facilities and Systems at LTIA 
This summary outlines the core elements and operational strategies for managing facilities and 
systems at Long Thanh International Airport, emphasizing its advanced, sustainable, and efficient 
management approach. 
 
 Definition and Importance of Airport Management 
A. Airport Management refers to the safe and efficient operation of airport facilities and the 
coordination of all activities related to air transportation. 
B. Airports play a significant role in regional economic development, serving as hubs for 
people, goods, and information. 

=== PDF PAGE 11 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 10 
C. To meet the needs of local industries and customers, airports must be maintained and 
operated effectively. 
D. Ensuring all elements of the airport meet safety standards through proper maintenance is 
essential. 
 
 Key Aspects of Airport Management 
A. Infrastructure Management 
 Airside: Runways, taxiways, and aprons. 
 Landside: Passenger terminals, cargo terminals, and parking facilities. 
 Other Facilities: Firefighting facilities, perimeter fences, internal roads, environmental 
protection facilities, power and water supply, and drainage systems. 
B. Operations Management 
 Air Traffic Control (ATC) : Safe management of aircraft takeoffs, landings, and 
movements within the airport. 
 Terminal Operations : Passenger processing, baggage handling, and management of 
commercial facilities. 
 Ground Services: Aircraft fueling, ground support equipment (GSE) management, and 
aircraft cleaning and maintenance. 
 Emergency Response : Development and execution of plans for accidents, fires, and 
medical emergencies. 
C. Service Provision 
 Aviation Security: Maintaining security and addressing threats within the airport. 
 Customer Service: Providing a comfortable and safe experience for passengers. 
 Commercial Services: Managing duty-free shops, food and beverage outlets, and other 
commercial facilities. 
D. Technology Management 
 Automation Systems: Enhancing operational efficiency at the airport. 
 IoT (Internet of Things) : Connecting and managing all equipment and systems within 
the airport. 
 Information Systems: AODB (Airport Operational Database), FIDS (Flight Information 
Display Systems), CUPPS (Common Use Passenger Processing Systems), and more.

 Stakeholder Collaboration: Working closely with airlines, ground service providers,

 Local Authorities: Collaborating on regional development and management around the 
airport.

 Management Features of Long Thanh International Airport 
A. Integrated Management System 
 All airport operations are centrally managed by the APOC (Airport Operation Center). 
 APOC is responsible for real -time information sharing, problem -solving, security 
management, and emergency response. 
 The ACMC (Airport Crisis Management Center)  acts as a backup to APOC, 
addressing crisis situations. 
B. Smart Airport 
 Utilizes advanced technologies to maximize operational efficiency and ensure a secure 
airport environment. 
C. Green Airport 
 Adheres to environmental protection standards and pursues sustainable development 
goals.

 Definition and Functions of APOC 
 
Figure 2 APOC Location 
 
This concept for Long Thanh Airport’s APOC outlines its critical roles in coordination, monitoring, 
and maintenance to ensure efficient and secure airport operations. 
A. APOC (Airport Operations Center)  serves as the central hub for coordinating airport 
operations and processes. It operates 24/7 as a single point of contact, monitoring all airport 
systems and analyzing data. 
B. APOC oversees the overall airport situation, relays real -time information to strategic 
departments, and coordinates all airport operations and authorities. 
C. It is a critical facility for comprehensive airport management and works alongside the A -
CDM (Airport Collaborative Decision Making) system to enhance operational efficiency. 
D. Key functions include flight flow planning, reducing security and transfer times, managing 
public announcements, visually monitoring terminal buildings and associated facilities via 
CCTV, and analyzing meteorological data. 


=== PDF PAGE 15 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 14 
E. APOC is also a pivotal facility for managing emergency situations, such as terrorism and 
natural disasters. 
 
Figure 3 APOC Features 
 
 Configuration of APOC 
A. Airport APOC is divided into three main zones: 
 Integrated APOC: Located in front of the APOC video wall, it hosts APOC coordinators 
responsible for monitoring and process management. 
 APOC Backup: Located near the Integrated APOC and staffed by operators responsible 
for process management. 
 Crisis Management Room: Used during crisis management situations. 
B. The APOC Support Area is an open office space where APOC coordinators and process 
operators provide analysis and support.

 Maintenance Concept for Long Thanh International Airport 
 Ensuring safety and efficient operations: Maintenance is essential for guaranteeing the 
safety and efficiency of airport facilities. 
 Meeting aviation safety standards: Proper maintenance ensures compliance with safety 
standards and provides passengers with a comfortable and secure experience. 
 Extending infrastructure and equipment lifespan : Contributes to prolonged use of 
airport infrastructure and equipment while reducing operational costs. 
 Sustainability and environmental responsibility : Maintenance supports sustainable 
operations and minimizes the environmental footprint of the airport. 
 
 Key Maintenance Activities 
A. Regular Inspections 
 Routine checks to identify issues early and address them proactively. 
 Activities are scheduled based on intervals, including daily, monthly, and annual 
inspections. 
B. Preventive Maintenance 
 Proactive measures to prevent equipment failure and facility damage. 
 Activities include lubrication, replacement of worn parts, and system adjustments. 
C. Repair and Restoration 
 Immediate and planned repairs of damaged facilities and faulty equipment. 
 Emergency repairs and scheduled restorations ensure minimal disruptions to airport  
operations. 
D. Improvements and Upgrades 
 Upgrading outdated facilities and integrating modern technologies. 
 Enhancing operational efficiency and safety through infrastructure modernization.

 Maintenance Goals for Long Thanh International Airport 
To ensure the efficient and sustainable management of airport facilities, Long Thanh International 
Airport adheres to the highest standards and implements best practices across several critical areas: 
A. Compliance with Standards 
 Adherence to both international and domestic safety regulations to maintain operational 
integrity. 
 Compliance with environmental management standards, such as ISO 14001:2015 , to 
ensure environmentally responsible operations. 
B. Safety Measures 
 Implementation of robust safety protocols during maintenance activities to prevent 
accidents. 
 Prioritization of the safety of maintenance workers and passengers at all times. 
C. Collaboration with Stakeholders 
 Coordination with key stakeholders, including: 
o Airport Operations Teams : For synchronized maintenance and operational 
activities. 
o Air Traffic Control (ATC): To ensure safety during airside maintenance activities. 
o Airlines and Ground Service Providers : For seamless integration of maintenance 
with ground operations. 
 Active collaboration with external service providers to enhance overall efficiency. 
D. Technology Adoption 
 Introducing Monitoring and Control system  to improve maintenance efficiency and 
reduce downtime. 
 Utilization of preventive maintenance to forecast equipment failures and schedule 
proactive repairs. 
E. Sustainability Practices 
 Adoption of eco-friendly maintenance methods to minimize environmental impact. 
 Implementation of energy-efficient technologies and the use of sustainable materials 
for repairs and upgrades. 
 Focus on reducing waste and improving resource utilization to align with global 
sustainability goals.

F. Resource Management 
 Efficient allocation of personnel, tools, and materials to maximize productivity and 
ensure timely maintenance. 
 Strategic planning of maintenance tasks to align with budget constraints and operational 
needs. 
 Development of contingency plans to address resource shortages or emergency scenarios. 
 
1.2 Regulation and Policies 
The Regulation and Policies  section defines the regulatory framework and guiding principles for 
maintaining operational excellence and compliance at Long Thanh International Airport. Key points 
include. 
 Regulations 
This document provides detailed guidelines and regulatory references, integrating both international 
and Vietnamese standards to optimize operational effectiveness. From structural maintenance and 
civil works to advanced systems like baggage handling, elec trical, and navigation facilities, each 
component is designed to meet rigorous safety and quality benchmarks. By adopting global best 
practices and local compliance measures, the airport aims to establish itself as a world-class facility. 
This introduction sets the stage for a detailed exploration of the critical systems and their management 
strategies. 
 
 Construction Law (Law No. 50/2014/QH13 dated 18 June 2014 on Construction, as 
amended by Law No. 62/2020/QH14 dated 28 June 2020 (Law on Amendments to the 
Construction Law). 
Maintenance Standards: The Construction Law establishes the overarching regulatory framework 
for the quality management and maintenance of public and private buildings in Viet Nam. 
Key Points:  
A. Regular inspections and maintenance procedures are mandated to ensure the structural 
safety of works. 

=== PDF PAGE 21 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 20 
B. Legal requirements for materials and construction methods safeguard structural integrity 
throughout the life cycle of facilities. 
C. The Law emphasizes post‑construction maintenance, providing clear legal grounds for 
maintenance planning and execution. 
It must particularly comply with the following provisions: 
A. Article 126 of the Construction Law No. 50/2014/QH13 on Maintenance of Construction 
Works, 
B. Clause 47, Article 1 of the Law No. 62/2020/QH14 on Amendments to the Construction 
Law. 
o The maintenance of construction works must meet the following requirements:  
Once in use, construction works and their components must be maintained. 
o A maintenance plan must be prepared and approved by the project owner before the 
facility is put into use; and the plan must be appropriate to the use purpose, type, 
grade, and installed equipment. 
o Maintenance activities must ensure the safety of people, property, and the structure 
itself. 
 The owner or user/manager of the facility is responsible for maintaining the structure and 
any installed equipment. 
 Maintenance must be conducted in accordance with the approved plan and procedures. 
 Large-scale structures, technically complex facilities, or those significantly affecting 
public safety and interest must undergo periodic safety assessments during operation. 
 The Government shall provide detailed regulations on maintenance, periodic safety 
assessment, and the responsibility for declaring expired or decommissioned structures. 
 
 Decree on Quality Management and Construction Maintenance (Decree No. 
06/2021/NĐ-CP) 
Maintenance Standards : This decree regulates the quality management and maintenance 
procedures for construction works, establishing the necessary procedures and standards for regular 
maintenance and quality control. 
Article 1 – Scope and Subjects of Application 

=== PDF PAGE 22 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 21 
A. This Decree elaborates on several regulations regarding the quality management, 
construction and maintenance of construction works. 
B. It applies to all domestic and foreign organizations and individuals involved in the quality 
management, construction or maintenance of such works. 
 
Key Points:  
A. Maintenance Work : Highlights the importance of regular inspections and preventive 
maintenance to ensure safety and longevity of the facility.  
B. Reporting and Certification : Maintenance activities must comply with regulations and 
standards, and completed work must undergo periodic inspections and certifications. 
C. This Decree elaborates on several regulations regarding the quality management, 
construction and maintenance of construction works. 
D. This Decree applies to domestic and foreign entities, organizations and individuals related 
to the quality management, construction and maintenance of construction works. 
 
 Policies 
For Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA) to maintain safe and efficient operations, it is crucial 
to establish a comprehensive maintenance policy for all facilities and systems. The goal is to ensure 
the overall health of the infrastructure through regu lar inspections, manage consumables effectively 
to prevent breakdowns, and prepare for any unforeseen failures with a robust response system, 
ensuring that airport operations remain uninterrupted. 
 
A. Comprehensive Inspection of Facilities and Systems 
 Ensuring Structural Integrity : Regular and systematic inspections of all airport 
facilities, including buildings, structures, roads, and landscaping, ensure the structural 
integrity of the airport. Inspections help detect early signs of damage, such as cracks or 
corrosion, and allow for timely repairs. 
 System Status Monitoring : Each key system (power supply, HVAC, security, 
wastewater treatment) should be routinely monitored to ensure proper functionality. 
These systems should be checked not only through daily operational checks but also 

=== PDF PAGE 23 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 22 
through more detailed, periodic performance assessments to detect potential issues before 
they arise. 
 
B. Preventive Maintenance 
 Consumable Management and Replacement: Effective management of consumables, 
such as batteries, filters, light bulbs, and other perishable components, is a crucial part of 
preventive maintenance. Consumables should be replaced based on their usage cycles, 
and regular checks should be conducted  to prevent breakdowns and extend the life of 
critical systems. 
 Scheduled Maintenance: Preventive maintenance tasks, such as regular inspections, 
diagnostics, and minor repairs, should be performed according to a predefined schedule. 
These activities ensure the long -term health of the facilities and systems. For example, 
HVAC systems should undergo quarterly checks, while power supply systems should be 
tested annually. 
 Predictive Maintenance: In addition to scheduled preventive maintenance, predictive 
models should be established to anticipate wear and tear on critical components. Using 
operational data, maintenance teams can forecast potential failures and replace or repair 
parts before they fail, ensuring continuous operation. 
 
C. Preparation for Unforeseen Failures  
 Emergency Response Procedures: Even with thorough preventive measures in place, 
unforeseen failures may occur. A comprehensive emergency response procedure should 
be established to ensure rapid recovery and minimize downtime. This system should 
include predefined emergency protocols for each type of failure, with clear roles and 
responsibilities for maintenance teams. 
 Maintenance Department : In the event of an unexpected failure, on -demand 
maintenance teams should be dispatched immediately to restore normal operations. These 
teams should be trained to handle urgent repairs and should have quick access to spare 
parts and necessary tools for immediate action. 
 
D. Non-Disruptive Airport Operations 

=== PDF PAGE 24 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 23 
 Continuous Monitoring for Uninterrupted Operations: Real-time monitoring systems 
should be in place for all key systems to detect and address issues before they disrupt 
operations. These systems should provide alerts for any abnormal activity or degradation 
in system performance. 
 Data-Driven Maintenance: Operational data from various systems should be analyzed 
to predict failures and inform maintenance schedules. By monitoring system performance 
in real time, potential problems can be identified early, and maintenance can be scheduled 
proactively to avoid unexpected downtime. 
 Regulatory Compliance : All maintenance activities must comply with relevant 
Vietnamese regulations and international standards. This ensures that the airport meets 
legal requirements while maintaining safety, quality, and operational efficiency. 
 
1.3 Work and Responsibilities 
Effective maintenance of the facilities and systems at Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA) 
requires an integrated approach that ensures the smooth and continuous operation of all airport 
services and infrastructure. This approach should be focused on t he coordinated efforts of various 
teams and stakeholders, who are responsible for maintaining the operational efficiency, safety, and 
reliability of all systems within the airport. 
 
 Facility/System Handover  
To ensure stable and effective maintenance, it is essential that facilities and systems transition 
smoothly and efficiently from the construction phase to the operational phase, which requires 
thorough performance verification during the handover process. Accordingly, the roles and 
responsibilities of relevant departments shall be clearly defined, along with a concise description of 
the handover procedures. 
 
Roles and Responsibilities of Key Stakeholders 
Stakeholder Responsibilities 
Construction 
Department 
- Conduct individual equipment tests according to standards and document results.

results.

Handover Process Flowchart 
 
Figure 5 Hand over flow chart 


=== PDF PAGE 26 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 25 
 Equipment Individual Test 
o Individual equipment performance tests must be conducted according to the 
manufacturer's procedures and standards, and all equipment must pass these tests. 
o Test results must be documented and submitted to the Operations Department. 
 Integrated System Test 
o An integrated system test must be completed to verify that individual facilities and 
systems operate in conjunction with each other. 
o Integrated system test results must be comprehensively recorded and reported to the 
Operations Department. 
 Regulatory Approvals and Permits 
o All necessary legal and administrative approvals and permits for facilities and 
systems must be obtained from the relevant authorities. 
o Approval details and supporting documents must be provided to the Operations 
Department as part of the handover. 
 Documentation for Handover 
o A Handover documentation confirming the completion of all the above conditions 
must be prepared and presented for final signature and confirmation by the 
Operations Department. 
o The handover documentation must include Standard Operati on Procedures 
(SOP), Irregular Operation Procedures (IOP), and Emergency Operati on 
Procedures (EOP) documents and must be approved by the Operations Department. 
 
Through the structured handover process described above, the Operations Department verifies the 
prerequisites for systematic management of facilities. To ensure uninterrupted operations, 
maintenance activities are systematically conducted under regular ins pections, preventive 
maintenance, and corrective maintenance concepts. 
 
 Comprehensive System Inspection 
Work 

=== PDF PAGE 27 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 26 
A. Perform routine and detailed inspections of all airport systems and facilities. This includes, 
but is not limited to: 
 Building infrastructure 
 Power supply systems 
 HVAC (Heating, Ventilation, and Air Conditioning) systems 
 Security equipment, including surveillance and access control systems 
 Baggage handling systems 
 Water and drainage systems 
 Fire safety systems 
 Communication and IT infrastructure 
 Electrical systems, including backup power and emergency systems 
B. Continuously monitor the overall condition of the airport’s systems and infrastructure, 
ensuring their operational integrity, functionality, and safety in compliance with both 
domestic and international standards. 
C. Identify any potential weaknesses or issues that may affect the operation of the airport and 
plan for corrective or preventive actions. 
Responsibilities: 
A. Maintenance Department: Conduct daily, weekly, and quarterly inspections of all critical 
systems and facilities, including structural, electrical, mechanical, and technological 
systems. Each team member is responsible for monitoring the performance of their 
assigned system and ensuring it meets operational standards. 
B. Contractors: Engage in construction, equipment supply and installation, and provide 
consulting services as needed to support the maintenance and inspection processes. They 
assist in specialized inspections and offer technical expertise when required. 
C. Government Agencies: Oversee and regulate the inspection process. Relevant authorities 
such as the Civil Aviation Authority of Vietnam (CAAV), the Airport Authority, and the 
Inspection Agency are responsible for ensuring compliance with national regulations and 
aviation standards. 
 
 Preventive Maintenance 
Work 

=== PDF PAGE 28 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 27 
A. Implement a preventive maintenance program designed to minimize system failures and 
disruptions by performing timely inspections, cleaning, lubrication, and calibration of all 
systems. 
B. Regularly replace consumables such as filters, batteries, and light bulbs based on 
predetermined schedules to prevent unexpected breakdowns. 
C. Perform tasks that keep the systems in optimal working condition, enhancing their 
reliability and extending their lifecycle. 
Responsibilities: 
A. Maintenance Department: Develop and enforce preventive maintenance schedules for 
each system based on their operational requirements and criticality. The team is also 
responsible for tracking and ensuring adherence to preventive maintenance timelines. 
Carry out inspections and preventive maintenance tasks for specific systems such as HVAC, 
power supply, water supply, security, and baggage handling systems. 
B. Procurement Department : Ensure adequate stock of required consumables and spare 
parts is maintained for seamless replacement and repairs. 
C. Contractors: Work with the Maintenance Management Team to provide materials, 
equipment, and expertise for preventive maintenance.  
 
 Corrective Maintenance 
Work 
A. Address unexpected failures or breakdowns in any system or facility promptly to minimize 
operational downtime and ensure that the airport continues to function without 
interruptions. 
B. Perform repairs and system restoration on equipment or infrastructure that have 
malfunctioned, ensuring that the system is returned to normal working condition. 
 
Responsibilities: 
A. Operation Department: This department is responsible for immediate emergency 
response in the event of unexpected system failures to ensure uninterrupted operations. 
They request necessary actions from the Maintenance Department as needed. 

=== PDF PAGE 29 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 28 
B. Maintenance Department: Technicians perform diagnostics, repair, and restoration of 
systems to restore normal functionality. They are responsible for troubleshooting and 
resolving issues related to specific airport systems. 
C. Relevant department: Coordinate with departments ( e.g. Air  Traffic Control, Ground 
Service Companies) to ensure repairs do not disrupt operations. 
D. Contractors: Contractors provide emergency repairs and offer technical solutions for 
complex failures. They are called upon for systems that require specialized skills or tools. 
E. Government Agencies: Ensure that corrective actions comply with regulations and ensure 
proper documentation for compliance during major repairs. (If necessary) 
 
 
 Overall Responsibilities of Key Stakeholders 
A. Airport Operations Management : Oversee the overall maintenance strategy and 
coordination of all maintenance activities. Ensure alignment with operational goals and 
regulatory standards. 
B. Maintenance Department : Manage the day -to-day maintenance operations, including 
inspection, preventive maintenance, corrective maintenance, and emergency repairs. 
Ensure that all work is performed safely, efficiently, and according to schedules. 
C. Safety and Compliance Department : Ensure adherence to safety protocols and legal 
requirements for all maintenance activities. 
D. Contractors: Provide specialized expertise for complex or non-routine maintenance tasks 
and offer additional support for specific systems or technologies.

Structural safety pertains to the foundational and load -bearing elements of the building, such as 
reinforced concrete (RC), steel structures 
A. Ensuring Building Stability: Proper maintenance of structural elements prevents potential 
hazards, such as collapses or structural deformations, which could endanger the safety of 
passengers and staff. 
B. Extending Lifespan: Regular inspection and repair of cracks, corrosion(rust), and other 
structural vulnerabilities prolong the life of the building and reduce long-term costs. 
C. Compliance with Standards: Adhering to Vietnamese regulations such as Decree 
06/2021/ND-CP ensures that the building meets national safety requirements.

 Type: Rigid concrete pavement (concrete slab) to withstand the high loads exerted by

 Pavement: These roads primarily support GSE vehicles (excluding aircraft towing

 Drainage: Roads are elevated to ensure smooth water flow and connection. 
C. Emergency Access Roads 
 Emergency access roads are critical for rapid response to aircraft incidents, fires, or 
medical emergencies. These roads allow access between parked aircraft and GSE zones 
without obstructing normal operations.

D. GSE Tunnel 
 The GSE tunnel facilitates underground movement of support vehicles, separating them 
from aircraft taxi paths and enhancing airside traffic safety. The tunnel also links to key 
apron areas. 
 
Figure 20 GSE Tunnel 
 Scale: 4 lanes, the cross-section of the 2 connecting tunnel branches is designed with 2 
lanes. The width of each lane is 4.5m including the equipment width of 3.4m, the 
clearance on each side is 0.5m and the rounding tolerance is 0.1m. 
 Design:  
o Typical longitudinal slope: < 1% 
o Maximum slope: 5% (only at constrained elevation differences) 
 Pavement: Two pavement types (Type 5 and Type 6) are applied in this zone, concrete 
slab pavement surface (provides durability under frequent heavy loading by fuel trucks 
and cargo tractors.) 
 Drainage: Tunnel slope allows natural drainage toward culvert connections.

 Drainage: Designed with consistent surface slope and elevation alignment to match the 
apron. The average surface slope ranges from 0.3% to 1.0%, ensuring positive drainage

G. Utility Tunnel 
 Designed to integrate key infrastructure, such as electrical, communication, water 
supply, wastewater, and heating pipelines, into an underground space. 
 The tunnel includes access manholes, inspection pits, cable trays, lighting, and 
ventilation ducts, ensuring ease of inspection and maintenance. 
 This tunnel is aligned beneath the service roads or apron and connects key facilities such 
as the Energy Center, Passenger Terminal, Pump Station, and Operations Building. 
 Design  
o Typical cross -section of the tunnel is designed with an effective internal height 
ranging from approximately 2.5 to 3.5 meters, and a width of 3 to 5 meters. 
o Internally, a double-layer pipe rack, maintenance walkway, and ducts for electrical 
and communication cables are arranged in parallel. 
o The roof and walls are made of reinforced concrete, while the floor is composed of 
finished concrete or water-resistant concrete.

 Water Supply and Drainage Systems 
  Water supply System 
The water supply system at Long Thanh International Airport (Phase 1) is designed around three 
central pumping facilities: Pumping Station 1, Pumping Station 2, and the Energy Center.

A. Pumping Station 1 
Located north of the central railway, this station serves the cargo terminal, customs, airport 
maintenance units, border police, landscaping services, training centers, animal and plant 
quarantine, aircraft cleaning zones, and the Airport Administration Building. 
B. Pumping Station 2 
Positioned south of the railway, this facility supplies water to freight forwarding warehouses 
(1–8), the Medical Center, three airline offices, the Power Receiving Station, and the Data & 
Telecom Center. 
C. Energy Center 
The Energy Center provides water to the Passenger Terminal Building (PTB), the Air Traffic 
Control Tower, and supports systems like cooling tower makeup water, in-flight catering, and 
staff areas. 
 
Each station is equipped with underground water storage tanks, allowing for up to 8 hours of supply 
based on maximum daily demand. This setup enhances energy efficiency and avoids obstruction of 
flight operations. 


=== PDF PAGE 56 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 55 
 
D.  Tank Capacities 
 Pumping Station 1: Approx. 1,300 m³ 
 Pumping Station 2: Approx. 1,000 m³ 
 Energy Center: Approx. 5,350 m³ 
E. Distribution Network 
Water is pumped through underground utility tunnels (UUTs) using main pipelines ranging 
from Φ200 to Φ500 mm. Recycled water pipelines (Φ250 mm) are also provided for 
landscaping and sanitation usage. Each terminal or building is connected through a metere d 
boundary distribution unit. 
 
 
  Drainage System 
The rainwater drainage system for Phase 1 of Long Thanh International Airport is designed to 
efficiently manage stormwater runoff generated across the entire airport site, including runways, 
taxiways, aprons, internal roads, and building facilities. The sy stem is structured around three 
primary components. 
 
A. Airport Internal Drainage System 
The internal drainage system utilizes natural slopes and open concrete channels to guide 
stormwater away from critical infrastructure. Runoff from runways, taxiways, aprons, and 
buildings is collected through U -shaped open drains and linear slits integrate d into the 
pavement. 
 
Figure 32 Drainage system of Runway (For example) 
 
 


=== PDF PAGE 57 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 56 
B. Internal Road & Access Road Drainage 
Drainage for internal roads and main access roads is provided via side ditches ranging from 
600 mm to 1,000 mm in width. Fire lanes and temporary parking zones are equipped with 
Kanivo-type channels to ensure rapid collection. Drain inlets are spaced approximately every 
17 to 21 meters to prevent surface ponding. 
 
C. Catchment Division & Regulating Pond Design 
The airport site is divided into six major drainage basins, each connected to a designated 
regulating pond. Catchment areas were defined based on slope direction and proposed surface 
runoff pathways. The division ensures that runoff from paved surfaces, ro ads, and roofs is 
properly balanced across each basin. 
 
Figure 33 Overview of the Catchment Division 
 
 Basin & Pond Overview 
o Basin areas range from 244 to 763 ha 
o Ponds are located at the downstream end of each basin to maximize gravitational 
flow.

2.3 HVAC System 
The HVAC system at Long Thanh International Airport is designed to meet the operational and 
comfort needs of the  Passenger Terminal Building . It is a robust system that ensures effective 
temperature, humidity, and air quality control, contributing to both passenger comfort and operational 
efficiency.

 Reclaim Water 
Water Supply and Distribution System 
Category Details 
Scope of Work 
Reclaim water is supplied from the sewage treatment plant (STP) and stored in 
the PTB plantrooms. Distributed through DN250 pipes routed in the tunnel 
from STP to PTB. 
Water Supply System 
Reclaim water is supplied via pressurized pumps with pressure -reducing 
valves. The distribution system maintains outlet pressure from 2.5 bar to 4.5 
bar. Booster pumps consist of water supply pumps, pressure tanks, and VSD 
control panels. Each package includes two duty pumps and one standby pump 
for alternating operation. 
 
Water Quality Conditions 
Category Details 
Water Quality 
Reclaim water must comply with QCVN 01-1:2018/BYT and meet water 
quality standards for toilet flushing. The water is treated before use and must 
meet specific parameters (refer to domestic water quality table). 
 
Reclaim Water Consumption and Capacity 
Category Details 
Water Consumption Total consumption: 2,000 m³/day (1,391,040 L for water closets and 563,040 
L for urinals). 
Buffer Tank Capacity 50% of reclaim water is stored in buffer tanks located at West and East 
plantrooms. Each buffer tank holds 500 m³ (total: 1,000 m³).

 Sewage drainage and vent system 
Sewage Drainage and Vent System 
Category Details 
Scope of Work 
Collects wastewater from PTB toilets, urinals, basins, and floor drains. 
Directs wastewater to central STP for treatment. 
Includes a vent system to maintain water seals in P-traps and prevent odors. 
Connects to Civil Drainage system. 
System Design 
Uses a "three pipes system" for soil, waste, and vent pipes. 
Soil pipes handle wastewater from toilets and urinals. 
Waste pipes handle wastewater from sinks, basins, and floor drains. 
Grease waste from kitchens is separated and diverted to a grease trap. 
Water Consumption 
Total sewage water consumption: 3,600 m³/day. 
Domestic water consumption: 1,485 m³/day. 
Reclaim water consumption: 2,000 m³/day. 
Aircraft wastewater: 96 m³/day. 
 
Sewage Pump Station and Drainage System 
Category Details 
Sewage Pump Station 
Provides pumping for toilets in central PTB areas where gravity discharge is 
not feasible. 
Collects and pumps wastewater to civil manholes. 
PIT Level Drainage 
Uses trenches and sumps to collect wastewater at the PIT level. 
Submersible pumps transfer wastewater to civil manholes.

 Rainwater Drainage System 
Rainwater Drainage System 
Category Details 
Scope of Work 
Collects rainwater from the roof catchment areas of PTB, FLB. 
Discharges rainwater to civil drainage system. 
Includes calculations for flowrate and sizing for gutters and downpipes based 
on rainfall density in Long Thanh – Dong Nai. 
System Design 
Rainwater drainage will be distributed via a DN250 pipe routed from PTB to 
Civil Drainage. 
Separate drainage for wastewater, grease, and stormwater. 
Water Quality Follows QCVN 01-1:2018/BYT standards. 
Material Specifications 
Above-ground pipes up to 300 mm: Galvanized steel. 
Underground pipes (300 mm and above): HDPE (for 300 mm) and reinforced 
concrete (for pipes larger than 400 mm). 
Rainfall Calculation 
Rainfall intensity from Ho Chi Minh City used for system sizing: 496 L/s.ha. 
Rainwater drainage system design is based on this rainfall intensity data. 
 
Rain Recycling Water System 

=== PDF PAGE 73 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 72 
Category Details 
Scope of Work 
Reuses rainwater from roof catchment areas, stored in recycling tanks for 
landscape irrigation and roof cooling sprinklers. 
Includes filtration to maintain water quality and pH (6.5-7.5). 
System Design 
Water is distributed from the storage tanks to landscape and roof cooling areas. 
Includes thermal sensors to control the activation of the system based on roof 
temperature. 
Filtration System Includes sand filter and pH control system with dosing pump. 
Water Quality 
Conditions Water quality meets specifications for landscape irrigation and roof cooling. 
Material Specifications 
Above ground: PPR PN10. 
Exposed pipes: HDPE PN10 with UV protection. 
Pump Specifications 
Two booster pump packages, each with two pumps for redundancy. 
Pump flowrate: 7.0 L/s, with a head of 8.2 bar.

 Liquified Petroleum Gas (LPG) Supply System 
LPG Supply System Overview 
Category Details 
Scope of Work 
LPG stored in 2 cylinder storage rooms located outside PTB. 
LPG supply pipeline runs underground to PTB and goes up outside the façade 
to reach F&B and restaurant kitchens. 
Gas meter and isolation valve at each F&B/restaurant kitchen for future 
connection. 
Gas leakage detector connected to fire alarm system for safety. 
System Design 
LPG is used only in F&B restaurant kitchens. 
Gas leakage alarm and shut-off valve installed in supply pipes. 
Gas pipeline routed in trench or exposed façade before entering building. 
Gas Metering Gas meter installed in each kitchen for consumption monitoring. 
Safety Gas leakage detection and shut -off system integrated with the fire alarm 
system. 
LPG Consumption 
Calculation 
LPG consumption calculation based on restaurant area coefficient and usage. 
Consumption calculated as 160 kg/hr. 
Required cylinders calculated as 44 cylinders (22 per storage). 
 
LPG Consumption and Storage 
Category Details 
LPG Consumption 
Calculation 
Formula: Q = F x C, where F = area of retail, C = restaurant area coefficient. 
Assumed LPG type: Propane. 
LPG consumption rate: 160 kg/hr. 
Cylinder Quantity 
Total required cylinders: 44 cylinders (each 45 kg). 
Storage: 2 cylinders per storage with 22 cylinders each. 
LPG Cylinder Storage 
Each storage holds 990 kg (22 x 45 kg cylinders). 
Storage located outside PTB. 
LPG Control Panel 
Emergency shut-off valve and system status monitoring. 
Gas leakage alarm and control functionalities included. 
 
LPG Supply System Pipe Material 
Category Details 
Pipe Material External pipes: HDPE PN10.

2.6 Aircraft stand equipment system 
The Aircraft Stand Equipment (ASE) system at Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA) is designed 
to provide critical utility and operational support for aircraft during turnaround. The system comprises 
key equipment including Ground Power Units (GPU), Pre -Conditioned Air (PCA) units, Passenger 
Boarding Bridge (PBB) related Power and Water Supply (PWS), a nd Visual Docking Guidance 
System (VDGS). ASE is not an isolated system but is integrated under the Master Systems Integration 
(MSI) framework, ensuring connectivity and interoperability with other airport systems. 
 
 Aircraft stand equipment system (VDGS, PBB, PCA, PWS, IASS, GPU) 
System Key Capabilities 
VDGS 
(Visual Docking Guidance 
System) 
- Provides precise visual guidance for aircraft docking operations 
- Enhances safety and efficiency during aircraft arrival 
- Interfaces with AODB and IASS for seamless operational 
synchronization 
PBB 
(Passenger Boarding Bridge) 
- Enables safe and efficient passenger boarding and disembarkation 
- Integrates electrical and hydraulic systems for bridge operation 
- Includes emergency systems to support safety and reliability during 
use 
PCA 
(Pre-Conditioned Air) 
- Supplies temperature-controlled air to parked aircraft 
- Maintains cabin air quality and comfort during ground handling 
- Reduces APU usage, supporting green airport initiatives 
PWS 
(Potable Water Supply) 
- Provides potable water to aircraft through fixed connection points 
- Maintains hygiene standards through filtration and pressure control 
- Supports reliable and efficient water distribution on stands 
IASS 
(Integrated Aircraft Stand 
System) 
- Integrates stand equipment systems with central operational platforms 
(RMS, DNE, SCS) 
- Supports remote control, monitoring, and fault detection 
- Synchronizes operations via Master Clock System (MCS) 
GPU 
(Ground Power Unit) 
- Delivers stable ground electrical power to parked aircraft 
- Reduces fuel consumption and emissions by replacing APU use 
- Equipped with backup power systems for operational reliability 
 
 

=== PDF PAGE 83 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 82 
2.7 Aircraft fuel supply system 
The refuelling facilities at Long Thanh International Airport ensure safe and efficient fuel handling, 
storage, and distribution. Key features include advanced pumps, separators, and tanks with a total 
capacity of 36,000 m³, meeting international safety and quality stan dards. These systems support 
seamless operations and high fuel reliability for aircraft. 
 
 Receiving Facility 
The Receiving Facility at Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA) is designed to efficiently and 
safely handle the reception of Jet A -1 aviation fuel. This facility ensures compliance with 
international standards for fuel quality and operational safety. Th e primary components of the 
Receiving Facility are as follows: 
Category Component Type Flow Rate Number of Units 
Receiving Pumps Centrifugal, horizontal, self-priming 60 m³/h (264 GPM) 10 
Filter Separators Vertical 60 m³/h (264 GPM) 10 
Unloading Hoses Flexible hose with dry break 
couplings 60 m³/h (264 GPM) 10 
Air Separators Carbon steel or stainless steel with 
epoxy 60 m³/h (264 GPM) 10 
A. Receiving Pumps : These centrifugal, horizontal, self -priming pumps are capable of 
delivering a flow rate of 60 m³/h (264 GPM) each. With 10 units in operation, they provide 
reliable fuel transfer from incoming transport vehicles to storage tanks while maintaining 
consistent pressure and flow. 
B. Filter Separators: Designed in a vertical configuration, these separators remove water and 
particulate contaminants from the fuel, ensuring compliance with EI 1581 standards. Each 
of the 10 units operates at a flow rate of 60 m³/h (264 GPM), maintaining the purity of 
aviation fuel. 
C. Unloading Hoses : Equipped with dry break couplings to prevent spills, the flexible 
unloading hoses facilitate secure and efficient fuel transfer. The facility operates with 10 
hoses, each capable of handling 60 m³/h (264 GPM), ensuring seamless unloading 
operations. 

=== PDF PAGE 84 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 83 
D. Air Separators : Made from corrosion -resistant carbon steel or stainless steel with an 
epoxy coating, the air separators ensure the removal of entrapped air from the fuel system. 
With a flow rate of 60 m³/h (264 GPM) per unit and 10 units in total, they enhance 
operational safety and prevent equipment damage caused by air entrainment. 
 
 Storage Facility 
The Storage Facility  at Long Thanh International Airport is designed to ensure the secure and 
efficient storage of aviation fuel. Its components are engineered to comply with international 
standards, ensuring operational safety, environmental protection, and reliable fuel man agement. 
Below is a detailed description of the facility's key elements: 
Category Details 
Fuel Storage 
Tanks 
Capacity per Tank: 6,000 m³ (nominal), 6,470 m³ (working), 7,764 m³ (maximum) 
Number of Tanks: 6 units 
Design: Vertical, column-supported cone roof tanks (API 650-compliant) 
Material: Carbon steel with internal epoxy coating and external painting for durability 
Accessories

Breather Valves & Flame Arresters

A. Fuel Storage Tanks 
 The facility includes six vertical, column -supported cone roof tanks with a working 
capacity of 6,470 m³ per tank, offering a total steady fuel stock of 36,000 m³. 
 The tanks are built with carbon steel and feature internal epoxy coatings for corrosion 
resistance and external painting for durability under environmental conditions. 
 These tanks meet API 650 standards , ensuring high reliability and compliance with 
international best practices. 
B. Floating Suction Pipes 

=== PDF PAGE 85 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 84 
 Each tank is equipped with 20 -inch floating suction pipes with swivel joints, ensuring 
efficient extraction of clean fuel from the upper layers, even when sedimentation occurs 
at the bottom. 
C. Breather Valves & Flame Arresters 
 Installed on each tank to regulate internal pressure and prevent ignition risks. These 
valves ensure safe operations by maintaining tank pressure within safe limits during fuel 
filling or withdrawal. 
D. Level Gauges 
 High-precision gauges are installed on all tanks to monitor fuel levels accurately, enabling 
operators to track inventory and detect anomalies quickly. 
E. Emergency Venting 
 Each tank is equipped with emergency venting mechanisms to release pressure during 
abnormal conditions, preventing tank failure due to overpressure. 
F. Instrumentation 
 High-level and low-level switches are integrated into the system to prevent overfilling 
or underfilling, ensuring safe and efficient operations. 
 Motor-operated inlet and outlet valves  are interlocked to prevent simultaneous 
operations, reducing the risk of operational errors. 
 Pumping & Hydrant Facility 
The Pumping & Hydrant Facility at Long Thanh International Airport is a vital part of the refueling 
system, ensuring the efficient and reliable transfer of aviation fuel to aircraft. This facility features 
advanced pumping systems, filtration units, and hydrant infrastructure, all desi gned to meet the 
highest industry standards.

A. Hydrant Pumps 
 The facility includes four centrifugal, horizontal pumps with VFD (Variable Frequency 
Drive), each delivering a flow rate of 454 m³/h (2,000 GPM). 
 These pumps are designed for high efficiency and meet API 610 standards , ensuring 
reliability and safety under demanding operational conditions. 
 The pumps operate at a design pressure of ≥175 m, providing sufficient force to deliver 
fuel to hydrants across the airport. 
B. Hydrant Filter Separators 
 Four vertical filter separators, each with a flow rate of 454 m³/h (2,000 GPM) , ensure 
fuel quality by removing water and particulate contaminants. 
 Constructed from carbon steel with internal epoxy coating , these separators comply 
with EI 1581 standards for fuel filtration, guaranteeing clean fuel delivery to aircraft. 
C. Fueling Hydrants 
 The hydrant system includes 176 two -way hydrants with 4 -inch valves , capable of 
delivering fuel at a flow rate of 227 m³/h (1,000 GPM) per hydrant. 
 These hydrants are compliant with EI 1584 or JIG 2 standards , ensuring safe and 
efficient refueling operations for various aircraft types. 
D. Fueling Hoses 
 Each hydrant pit is equipped with two flexible hoses with dry break couplings, each 
delivering fuel at a flow rate of 100 m³/h (440 GPM). 
 These hoses are designed to prevent spills and ensure secure connections during refueling 
 
 Transfer & Recovery Facility

movement of fuel between storage tanks and operational areas, as well as the recovery and handling 
of unused or contaminated fuel. This facility is crucial for maintaining operational flexibil ity, 
minimizing waste, and ensuring the efficient use of aviation fuel resources.

 Fire Water Tank: A single tank with a capacity of 1,500 m³ stores sufficient water for

 Fire Water Pumps: Two pumps (electric and diesel -driven), each with a flow rate of

 Air Foam Chambers : The facility includes 12 units with a flow rate of 1,100 L/min 
per unit, enabling effective fire suppression for critical areas. 
 
2.8 GSE Fuel Supply Facility 
The GSE Fuel Supply Facility at Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA) is designed to provide 
safe and efficient fueling for ground support equipment (GSE). This facility consists of several key 
systems including manual fuel receiving, underground fuel storage, controlled dispensing , 
environmental drainage, and auxiliary infrastructure. The following sections describe the capabilities 
of each system based solely on the confirmed content from the translated technical specifications. 
 Fuel Receiving System 
The Fuel Receiving System facilitates the transfer of fuel from tanker trucks to the underground 
storage tank using a manual unloading method. Hoses and valves are used for controlled transfer, 
ensuring basic safety and operational simplicity.

 Fuel is manually unloaded from tanker trucks into the underground tank. 
 The unloading process uses flexible hoses connected directly to the inlet pipe. 
B. Valves 
 Manual shut-off valves are installed at the receiving point to control the fuel flow and 
prevent accidental spillage.

2.9 Sewage Treatment Plant (STP) 
The Sewage Treatment Plant (STP) at Long Thanh International Airport is designed to efficiently 
handle the wastewater generated by the terminal, surrounding facilities, and operational systems. It 
ensures that all wastewater is treated to meet local environmental standards and provides an integrated 
solution for managing wastewater within the airport's infrastructure.  The plant employs advanced 
treatment technologies, including green technology solutions that reduce energy consumption and 
enhance efficiency.  
 
 Primary Treatment System 
The Primary Treatment System at Long Thanh International Airport’s Sewage Treatment Plant (STP) 
is designed to efficiently remove large solids, debris, and heavy inorganic materials from the 
incoming wastewater. This first stage is essential for protecting downstream biological treatment 
processes and maintaining the operational efficiency of the entire plant. The system employs 
mechanical screening and aerated grit removal technologies to ensure effective preliminary treatment. 
Category Component Type Flow Rate Number of Units 
Screening Units Coarse and Fine Mechanical Screens 5,660 m³/day 2 
Grit Removal 
Units Aerated Grit Removal Tank 235.8 m³/hr 1 

=== PDF PAGE 93 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 92 
A. Screening Units:  
The screening system at the primary treatment stage consists of two mechanical screens: a 
coarse screen followed by a fine screen. 
 The coarse screen removes large debris such as plastics, cloths, and sticks, with a bar 
spacing of approximately 20 mm. 
 The fine screen captures smaller floating solids larger than 0.75 mm in size to protect 
downstream processes. 
Each screening unit is designed to handle the full design average daily flow rate of 5,660 
m³/day. The system includes automatic raking and cleaning devices to maintain continuous 
operation with minimal manual intervention, ensuring optimal hydraulic performance and 
preventing clogging of subsequent treatment facilities. 
 
B. Grit Removal Units:  
After screening, the wastewater flows into an aerated grit removal tank. This unit is designed 
to remove inorganic particles such as sand, gravel, and silt, which could cause wear and 
damage to pumps and downstream equipment. An aeration system installed at the bottom of 
the tank promotes the separation of lighter organic mater ials from heavier inorganic matter, 
enhancing grit removal efficiency. 
The grit removal unit is designed to handle an hourly flow rate of approximately 235.8 m³/h, 
consistent with the plant's design capacity. It also includes a grit washing system to clean the 
separated grit prior to disposal, reducing organic content in the removed material and 
minimizing odor issues.  This step is critical for ensuring the long -term reliability and 
efficiency of the STP’s mechanical and biological treatment processes. 
 
 Secondary Treatment System 
The Secondary Treatment System at Long Thanh International Airport’s Sewage Treatment Plant 
(STP) is designed to biologically treat the organic and nutrient pollutants remaining after primary 
treatment. Utilizing advanced Green Technology, the system integrates fixed biofilm and suspended 
biomass processes to optimize the biologic al removal of biochemical oxygen demand (BOD), 
chemical oxygen demand (COD), nitrogen, and phosphorus, ensuring that the effluent meets stringent 
environmental discharge standards. 

=== PDF PAGE 94 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 93 
Category Component Type Flow Rate Number of Units 
Biological

(reactors per line) 
Biofilm Carriers Fixed Biofilm Media - Installed in all 
aerobic reactors 
Anoxic and 
Aerobic Zones 
Alternating Anoxic/Aerobic 
Chambers - Provided 
A. Biological Reactors (Green Technology Bioreactors): 
The biological treatment process is conducted in modular Green Technology bioreactors 
designed with alternating anoxic and aerobic conditions.  Each treatment line consists of six 
sequential reactors: 
 Reactor 1 and 4: Anoxic reactors (805 m³ per reactor) 
 Reactor 2, 3, 5, and 6: Aerobic reactors (595 m³ per reactor) 
The bioreactors are operated with a target mixed liquor suspended solids (MLSS) 
concentration of 2,000 mg/L in the liquid phase. Biofilm carriers are installed within the 
aerobic reactors to enhance microbial activity and increase treatment capacity without 
significantly enlarging the footprint of the facility. This integrated approach maximizes the 
breakdown of organic pollutants and the biological removal of nitrogen through nitrification 
and denitrification processes. 
The modular arrangement allows for flexible operation and efficient load management, 
ensuring stable performance even under varying influent conditions. 
 
B. Biofilm Carriers (Fixed Biofilm Media): 
Fixed biofilm media are deployed extensively in the aerobic reactors.  The carriers are 
designed to simulate natural plant root structures, offering a large surface area (~500 m²/m³) 
for microbial growth. Key features include: 
 Biofilm thickness: 20–30 mm 
 Biomass density: 14–18 kg/m³ 
 High resistance to hydraulic shock loads 
 Enhanced sludge reduction through natural predatory food chains within the biofilm 
ecosystem 

=== PDF PAGE 95 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 94 
These features contribute to significantly improved pollutant removal efficiency and energy 
savings compared to conventional activated sludge processes. 
 
C. Anoxic and Aerobic:  
The treatment process alternates between anoxic (oxygen -free) and aerobic (oxygen -rich) 
conditions to achieve efficient nitrogen removal: 
 Anoxic Reactors: Facilitate denitrification, reducing total nitrogen levels. 
 Aerobic Reactors: Support biological oxidation of organic matter and nitrification of 
ammonia. 
This staged environment allows for optimized microbial community development, better 
adaptability to influent variability, and overall enhanced treatment efficiency.  Real-time 
monitoring and control of dissolved oxygen (DO) concentrations are implemented to optimize 
aeration rates and minimize energy consumption. 
 
 Tertiary Treatment System 
The Tertiary Treatment System at Long Thanh International Airport’s Sewage Treatment Plant (STP) 
is designed to further polish the effluent following secondary biological treatment. This stage ensures 
that the treated wastewater achieves the strictest wate r quality standards required for environmental 
discharge or potential reuse within the airport facilities. The tertiary treatment mainly involves 
chemical disinfection and fine filtration to ensure the complete removal of pathogens and suspended 
solids. 
Category Component Type Flow Rate 
Disinfection System Chlorine Disinfection (Sodium Hypochlorite 
NaOCl Dosing) 5,660 m³/day 
Fine Filtration Units Automatic Self-cleaning Filters (50-micron 
filtration) 5,660 m³/day 
A. Disinfection System (Chemical Chlorination):  
The disinfection system uses chemical chlorination with sodium hypochlorite (NaOCl) to 
eliminate pathogenic microorganisms from the treated effluent.  Treated water from the 
secondary clarifiers is dosed with chlorine in a dedicated contact tank designed for a retention 
time of approximately 30 minutes, ensuring effective microbial inactivation. 

=== PDF PAGE 96 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 95 
Key features: 
 Sodium Hypochlorite Dosing System (NaOCl solution injection via dosing pumps) 
 Target residual chlorine maintained to comply with QCVN 40:2011/BTNMT Column A 
discharge standards 
This disinfection process is critical for ensuring that the effluent is safe for discharge into the 
receiving environment and for any non-potable reuse applications. 
 
B. Fine Filtration Units: 
After chemical disinfection, the treated water is passed through automatic self-cleaning filters 
with a 50 -micron mesh size.  These filtration units are designed to remove fine suspended 
solids and any remaining debris that could impact the quality of the effluent or damage 
downstream reuse systems. 
Key features: 
 Protects downstream reuse systems (e.g., irrigation, cooling) 
 Enhances effluent clarity and quality 
The fine filtration step ensures that the reclaimed water meets reuse standards, supporting 
sustainable water management practices within the airport. 
 
 Sludge Treatment & Disposal 
The Sludge Treatment & Disposal system at Long Thanh International Airport’s Sewage Treatment 
Plant (STP) is designed to manage the sludge produced from the biological treatment and secondary 
clarification processes. The system aims to minimize sludge volu me, stabilize organic matter, and 
prepare the sludge for safe disposal in accordance with environmental regulations. The treatment 
process includes sludge thickening, mechanical dewatering, and final disposal.

Sludge Disposal Off-site Transport for Disposal - - 
A. Sludge Thickening:  
The aerated sludge storage tank temporarily stores excess sludge from the secondary clarifiers. 
Air diffusers installed at the bottom of the tank continuously mix and aerate the sludge to 
prevent sedimentation, promote partial digestion, and maintain sludge quality before 
dewatering. 
Key features: 
 Effective sludge homogenization 
 Prevention of anaerobic conditions and odor generation 
 Supports consistent feeding to the dewatering units 
The design storage capacity allows for up to 45 days of sludge accumulation (approximately 
425 m³ total volume). 
 
B. Sludge Dewatering (Belt Press Dewatering Units):  
Sludge from the storage tank is pumped to belt press dewatering units.  These machines 
remove excess water from the sludge, significantly reducing its volume and weight, thus 
facilitating easier handling and transportation. 
Key features: 
 Capacity of 5.0–9.0 m³/h per unit 
 Target dry solids content: 20–30% 
 Polymer-assisted dewatering for higher efficiency 
 Fully automated operation with continuous sludge feed 
The use of two dewatering units ensures operational flexibility and redundancy. 
 
C. Polymer Dosing System:  
To enhance the dewatering performance, a polymer preparation and dosing system is 
employed. The system automatically prepares and injects a precise amount of polymer 
solution into the sludge stream to promote flocculation, improving water separation during 
the dewatering process. 
Key features: 
 Daily polymer consumption: approximately 10 kg/day 

=== PDF PAGE 98 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 97 
 Automated mixing and dosing system 
 Reduces chemical consumption through optimized dosing control 
 
D. Sludge Disposal:  
The dewatered sludge is collected in storage containers and transported off -site for final 
treatment or reuse. Sludge handling complies with applicable environmental regulations, and 
final disposal or recovery will be determined in accordance with legal requirements.  
Key points: 
 Dewatered sludge is transported off-site   
 Disposal or reuse follows Vietnamese regulatory guidelines 
 
 Control & Monitoring Systems 
The Control & Monitoring Systems at Long Thanh International Airport’s Sewage Treatment Plant 
(STP) are designed to ensure safe, efficient, and reliable operation across all treatment processes. The 
systems enable real-time monitoring, automated control, d ata logging, and remote access, ensuring 
continuous compliance with operational and environmental standards. The automation and control 
architecture are based on an integrated PC –PLC platform combined with SCADA (Supervisory 
Control and Data Acquisition) for centralized management.

A. Centralized Control System:  
The STP employs a programmable logic controller (PLC) -based centralized control system 
that automates the operation of pumps, blowers, mixers, valves, and chemical dosing systems 
across the plant.  The system is programmed to respond dynamically to changing process 
conditions, optimize energy consumption, and maintain operational stability. 
Key features: 
 Fully automated process control 
 Integrated with all field instrumentation 
 High flexibility to accommodate process modifications 
 
B. Flow Meters:  
Electromagnetic and ultrasonic flow meters are installed at critical points across the influent, 
effluent, sludge, and chemical dosing lines.  These meters ensure precise flow monitoring, 
enabling efficient process control and early detection of abnormal conditions such as 
blockages, leaks, or overflows. 
Key features: 
 Continuous flow rate measurement 
 Data integration with SCADA for real-time tracking 
 
C. Level Sensors: 
Level sensors, utilizing ultrasonic and pressure-based technologies, are deployed in all major 
tanks and basins to monitor water levels. Accurate level monitoring allows the control system 
to maintain proper hydraulic balances, prevent overflows, and optimize pump operations. 
Key features: 
 Real-time water level tracking 
 Alarm activation in case of abnormal high or low levels 
 
D. DO & Chemical Sensors:

The STP incorporates various dissolved oxygen (DO) sensors, pH analyzers, and chemical 
concentration meters. These sensors are critical for maintaining optimal biological treatment 
conditions, ensuring that aeration and chemical dosing are adjusted based on real-time process 
demands. 
Key features: 
 DO control in biological reactors for energy-efficient aeration 
 pH control in chemical dosing systems 
 Continuous online monitoring and automated control adjustments 
 
E. SCADA System:  
A Supervisory Control and Data Acquisition (SCADA) system provides centralized 
visualization, data collection, and remote operation capabilities for the entire STP.  The 
SCADA interface displays process status, performance trends, alarms, and historical data, 
enabling operators to make informed operational decisions. 
Key features: 
 Real-time process visualization 
 Data storage for operational history and regulatory reporting 
 Advanced alarm management and event logging 
 
F. Alarm & Notification System: 
A comprehensive alarm and notification system alerts operators to process anomalies, 
equipment failures, and safety risks through visual indicators and audible alarms at the control 
center. 
Key features: 
 Automatic alert generation for operational incidents 
 Critical alarms for immediate operator response 
 Event logging for root cause analysis

2.11 Baggage Handling System 
The Baggage Handling System (BHS) at Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA) is a state -of-the-
art solution designed to handle up to 25 million passengers annually. Built to meet efficiency, 
security, and scalability requirements, the system ensures seamless baggage operations for inbound, 
outbound, and transfer baggage with an integrated central layout and advanced technologies. 
 
 Key Features 
 
Figure 357 Layout of BHS at LTIA 
 
 Centralized Layout and Automation 
A. Spanning multiple floors of the Passenger Terminal Building (PTB), the BHS layout 
minimizes transfer times between processing points. 
B. Includes Individual Carrier Systems (ICS) for precise baggage identification and routing. 


=== PDF PAGE 109 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 108 
C. Automated Tag Readers (ATRs) provide 360 -degree scanning for accurate tracking and 
sorting. 
 
 Integrated Security 
A. Equipped with Explosive Detection Systems (EDS) compliant with ECAC Standard 3 and 
TSA certifications. 
B. Security screening processes include Level 1 -4 screenings and reconciliation rooms for 
flagged baggage. 
C. Dedicated Customs inspection zones for international  and domestic baggage ensure 
compliance with regulatory standards. 
 
 Modular and Expandable Design 
A. The modular system design supports future expansion to meet growing passenger volumes. 
B. Dedicated facilities for Out -of-Gauge (OOG) and Super Out -of-Gauge (SOOG) baggage 
streamline special handling processes. 
 
 Efficient Resource Management 
A. Advanced monitoring through SCADA systems ensures real-time operational oversight. 
B. Redundancy features enable uninterrupted operations during equipment failures or 
maintenance.

2.12 Electrical systems 
 This section provides an overview of the electrical systems designed for Long Thanh International 
Airport, focusing on compliance with Vietnamese regulations and international standards to ensure 
operational efficiency and safety. 
 
 Electrical Systems Overview 
This section outlines the key components, guidelines, and specifications for the electrical systems, 
ensuring proper design, installation, and maintenance. 
 
Figure 37 Electrical Systems Overview 
 
This section outlines the key components, guidelines, and specifications for the electrical systems, 
ensuring proper design, installation, and maintenance. 


=== PDF PAGE 113 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 112 
 
Figure 40 Electrical Power Supply System Diagram at LTIA 
 
 Diverse Substation Types 
Long Thanh International Airport requires multiple types of substations to provide stable and reliable 
power across its vast facilities. 
A. Main Substation (110/22kV) 
 Constructed in compliance with Long Thanh TCTCHKVN (ACV) standards. 
 Equipped with 2x40MVA transformers operating in parallel to ensure redundancy and 
uninterrupted supply during maintenance. 
B. Backup Substation (22/0.4kV) 
 Supplies backup power to maintain airport operations during outages. 
C. Airfield Ground Lighting (AGL) Substation 
 Comprises transformer rooms and medium voltage panels, and contain diesel generators. 
 
 Medium Voltage (MV) Systems 
The system is designed to handle a 22kV distribution voltage per Vietnamese regulations. 
All MV equipment, including cables, switchgear, and transformers, must meet 22kV specifications. 
Key Requirements: 


=== PDF PAGE 114 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 113 
A. The system uses the TN earthing system as per IEC standards, with detailed connection 
methods from transformers to equipment. 
B. Voltage drop is maintained within specified limits, adhering to acceptable thresholds. 
C. Total Harmonic Distortion (THD) is regulated under Circular 30/2019/TT-BCT. 
 
 Power Supply and Distribution 
Five substations distribute load efficiently, reducing cable length and cost. 
Transformers operate in parallel to ensure redundancy and uninterrupted supply during maintenance. 
System Features: 
A. Main LV boards installed in substations deliver power to EPS rooms with a 10% power 
reserve and 20% space reserve for future expansion. 
B. LV panels are of a draw-out type for ease of operation and maintenance. 
C. SCADA controls power distribution and lighting while monitoring most M&E equipment. 
 
 Redundancy and Future Expansion 
Main LV boards in substations feature a 10% power reserve and 20% space reserve for future 
expansion. 
LV panels are of a draw-out type for streamlined operation and maintenance. 
Fire Safety Integration 
Power systems are integrated with fire prevention systems for rapid emergency response. 
Strategic Substation Placement 
Substations are strategically located to optimize power distribution and meet the airport’s overall 
energy demands. 
 
 Emergency Power and Protection Systems Overview 
The emergency power system is monitored and managed through the SCADA system.  The 
emergency power system at Long Thanh International Airport is essential for maintaining core 
operational functions during main power supply interruptions.  It primarily consists of diesel 

=== PDF PAGE 115 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 114 
generators and uninterruptible power supply (UPS) units.  These systems provide power to critical 
facilities across the airport to minimize operational disruptions. 
Backup power is supplied through the 22/0.4kV substation, which can support the entire load. 
During emergencies, power is prioritized for critical facilities, including: 
A. Runway lighting systems (AGS). 
B. Communication systems. 
C. Data centers. 
D. Firefighting systems. 
The Airfield Ground Lighting Station (AGS)  ensures operational lighting for runways during 
emergencies. 
Emergency power is efficiently distributed based on priorities via the SCADA system. 
Diesel generators automatically activate during outages to supply power to key equipment, while UPS 
units handle short-term interruptions to prevent system failures. 
The UPS ensures continuous power to critical systems like servers and control systems, protecting 
data and maintaining operational continuity.

 Control systems.

 Protection system

 Key Features of Major Facilities 
 Power Receiving Station (PWR) 
A. Main Role: Serves as the central facility distributing power across the airport. 
B. Control Scope: 
 Airport-Wide Supply: Distributes electricity to all facilities at Long Thanh International 
Airport. 
 Substation Connection: Receives power from the Dong Nai regional substation. 
 Emergency Power: Equipped with diesel generators to maintain power supply during 
outages. 
 Low Voltage Distribution Boards (MCC) : Distribute power while protecting against

 Outdoor Lighting System 
The Outdoor Lighting System forms part of LTIA’s critical infrastructure, covering the landside zone 
and associated access roads. The design package includes luminaires, poles, foundations, accessories, 
underground cabling, and an integrated monitoring -and-control interface with the airport BMS. 
Airfield guidance lighting and architectural façade lighting are expressly excluded from this scope.

fixtures.

2.13 Security systems 
 Security Screening Equipment (SSE) 
Airport security screening equipment is essential for ensuring aviation security and passenger safety. 
This equipment includes metal detectors, X-ray scanners, explosive detection systems, and full-body 
scanners, which are used to thoroughly inspect passen gers, baggage, and cargo. These tools play a 
critical role in preventing the entry of weapons, explosives, and hazardous materials into the airport 
premises. Advanced technology improves the speed and accuracy of inspections while being designed 
to maximize passenger convenience. Airport security screening equipment operates in compliance 
with national regulations and international aviation security standards. 
Figure 381 Standard Check-Point

 Airport Perimeter 
 Gate House 
Gate House is  strategically positioned facilities designed to enhance security and control access to 
the airport premises. These checkpoints are established at three key locations along the airport's main 
entry routes and access points, enabling thorough screening of v ehicles and individuals entering the 
area. Equipped with security screening equipment and staffed by trained personnel, each checkpoint 
is designed to detect and address potential threats proactively. This setup prevents unauthorized 
access to the airport and strengthens the overall security level. All vehicles and personnel passing 
through the Guard Post must have a valid badge, and all items brought in must be searched. 
Three gate houses will be constructed at Long Thanh Airport. Each gate house will be configured as 
follows depending on its location: 
 Gate House 1: 3 check lanes / Gate House 2: 2 check lanes / Gate House 3: 3 check lanes 
 
X-RAY and WTMD are placed in each inspection lane, and CCTV is installed in the post to monitor 
the entry and exit 24 hours a day.  In addition, the vehicle screening lane is equipped with an Under 
Vehicle Scanner to make it easy to search for hidden items.

VIP Check-Point for INT 1

 Perimeter Fence and Perimeter Intrusion Detection System (PIDS) 
Airport Perimeter Fences are critical structures designed to enhance the physical security of the 
airport boundary. These walls are robustly constructed around the airport to prevent external intrusion 
and effectively block unauthorized access. Made from durable materials, they a re built to withstand 
physical threats and ensure long -term stability. Additionally, the Fences are integrated with the 
airport's security surveillance systems, functioning alongside security cameras and sensors to 
strengthen the pe rimeter monitoring framework. These perimeter fences serve as essential security 
measures to protect airport facilities and ensure the secure airport operation. 
A. Objectives and Scope of the PIDS 
 Perimeter Protection: Secure the airport boundary by detecting unauthorized access 
attempts using advanced sensor technologies.  
 Intrusion Detection and Alerting: Identify security threats such as climbing, cutting, or 
breaching the fence, and trigger immediate alarms.  
 Event Logging and Tracking: Record intrusion events with time stamps and integrate with 
visual verification systems for investigation.  
 Centralized Monitoring: Operate through the Aviation Security Center ( ASC) for real -
time supervision, alarm handling, and system diagnostics. 
B. Key Functions 
 Continuous monitoring of perimeter fences using vibration, fiber optic, or microwave -
based Sensor Cable technologies. 
 Automatic alarm activation upon detection of climbing, cutting, or impact on the fence. 
 Real-time transmission of intrusion alerts to the Aviation Security Center (ASC). 
 Integration with CCTV systems for automatic camera focusing and event recording upon 
intrusion detection. 
C. Design Criteria 
 Detection systems must provide high accuracy with low false alarm rates under various 
weather and environmental conditions. 
 Sensor coverage shall be redundant in high-risk zones to ensure detection reliability. 
 Power backup and secure data transmission must be ensured for uninterrupted operation.

 Security-Related Facility 
 Aviation Security Center (ASC) & CCTV Room  
The Airport Security Center (ASC) serves as the central command and coordination hub for all 
security-related operations within the airport. Its primary mission is to ensure the safety and security 
of passengers, staff, infrastructure, and aircraft by impl ementing and enforcing aviation security 
regulations, protocols, and emergency response procedures  
 
Figure 403 Function of Airport Security Center 
 
At LTIA, the ASC is operated by the Immigration Police Department (A08), which functions under 
the authority of the Ministry of Public Security. This department holds full responsibility for 
supervising, executing, and managing all security matters within the airport premises. Its scope 
includes but is not limited to access control, passenger and baggage screening, surveillance, incident 
response, and coordination with intelligence and counterterrorism units

The CCTV Room is a secured and centralized facility dedicated to the real -time surveillance and 
monitoring of airport security operations, access control, and passenger areas. It plays a critical role 
in ensuring the safety and security of the airport by enabling continuous observation, incident 
detection, and rapid response coordination. 
 
Key functions include: 
A. Real-time Surveillance of key operational and restricted areas, including terminal 
interiors, access control points, baggage handling zones, and airside perimeters. 
B. Coordination with Airport Security Center (ASC)  to respond to potential threats or 
irregularities. 
C. Recording and storage  of video footage for post -incident review, evidence, and 
compliance with aviation security regulations.
