The CCTV System at Long Thanh International Airport is a state-of-the-art, digital, IP-based video 
surveillance system designed to: 
A. Monitor Airport Buildings: Provide real-time surveillance across public/restricted zones. 
B. Record and Store Video Data: Ensure video evidence is stored for at least 90 days. 
C. Support Access Control: Integrate with the ACS to trigger cameras in security events. 
D. Enhance Security Analysis: Utilize advanced video analytics for motion detection, facial 
recognition, object tracking, and people counting. 
System Components 
The LTIA CCTV system consists of the following key elements: 
A. CCTV camera: Security / Operational/ Maintenance cameras 
B. Video Management System (VMS): A virtualized system hosted on the DCH Server Farm 
for centralized video storage and management. Features include live monitoring, playback, 
and video export without interrupting ongoing recordings. 
C. Network Infrastructure: High -speed digital network over the DNE (Digital Network 
Environment) and SCS (Security Communication System). 
D. Storage System: Redundant SAN (Storage Area Network) infrastructure to store 90 days 
of footage at full resolution. Supports up to 9,000 cameras with scalable storage capacity. 
E. Monitoring Workstations: Located in key areas such as APOC, ASC, immigration rooms, 
customs rooms, and baggage handling areas. 
 
Key Function 
A. Surveillance Capabilities: 
 Public Areas: queuing zones, check-in counters, baggage claims, and transfer areas. 
 Restricted Zones: Coverage for data centers, control rooms, and technical facilities. 
 Aircraft and Apron Monitoring: boarding, fueling, and maintenance activities. 
B. Video Analytics: 
 Facial Recognition: For critical access points. 
 Object Detection: Identifying unattended objects or suspicious items. 
 People Counting: To track passenger flow and avoid congestion. 
C. Event Response: 
 Integration with ACS and FAS for automatic video triggering during abnormal events.

 Security Access Control System 
Objectives and Scope of the ACS 
The Access Control System (ACS) at Long Thanh International Airport is designed to enhance 
security and operational efficiency. The main objectives include: 
A. Restricted Area Access Control: Manage access to various zones (public and restricted) 
within the airport based on roles and permissions and real-time event logging 
B. Intrusion Detection: Detect security threats such as unauthorized entry or abnormal door 
openings and trigger alarms. 
C. Centralized Management: Integrate with the Airport Management System (AMS) for real-
time monitoring and event management. 
System Components 
A. Management System: Supports user registration, permission management, event logging. 
B. Workstations: Administration, Operational, Enrollment Workstations 
C. Access Control Devices: IP Card Readers and Controllers (CR), Contactless RFID Cards 
D. Door Equipment: Includes sensors, actuators, and emergency exit devices. Integrated with 
ACS to trigger alarms in abnormal door activities. IDS Sensors installed at doors and zones 
to detect unauthorized access. Sends real-time alerts to the control center in security events. 
Key Functions 
A. Access Authorization Management: Register users and assign access permissions. Enforce 
boundary control between public and restricted zones based on security levels. 
B. Real-Time Monitoring:  Monitor the status of doors and card readers in real-time. Trigger 
alarms and log events during unauthorized activities. 
C. Event Logging and Traceability: Maintain a comprehensive log of all access events to 
support security audits and investigations. Store event data for a minimum of 24 months. 
D. Integrated Management:  integrated with AMS and CCTV to trigger cameras during 
security incidents. Interfaces with FAS to automatically release emergency exit doors.

2.14 ICT systems  
The ICT systems at Long Thanh International Airport serve as the backbone for ensuring efficient 
and reliable airport operations. These systems provide a comprehensive framework for managing 
communications, data flow, and operational processes, enabling seamless interactions between 
passengers, staff, and various airport systems. The design integrates cutting -edge technology and 
complies with international standards such as ISO/IEC 27001 and Vietnamese regulatory 
requirements, ensuring security, reliability, and scalability. 
 
 ICT Systems Overview 
This chapter provides an overview of the major ICT systems implemented at Long Thanh 
International Airport, examining their functionalities and contributions to operational excellence.  
 
 FIDS (Flight Information Display System) 
The Flight Information Display System (FIDS) delivers real -time flight and resource data from the 
AODB to various display terminals, providing essential information to passengers, airlines, and 
airport stakeholders. It includes details on arrivals, departu res, delays, gates, check -ins, baggage 
handling, and operational updates tailored to different audiences. 
 
  
Fig 414 FIDS Schematic Diagram 


=== PDF PAGE 131 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 130 
 CUPPS (Common Use Passenger Processing System) 
The CUPPS serves as a core operational platform that enables airlines and service providers to share 
check-in counters and boarding gates efficiently. Its primary function is to support key passenger 
processing activities like check -in, boarding, and baggage handling by allowing each airline secure 
access to its own DCS (Departure Control System). Integrated with other systems, CUPPS ensures 
centralized control of passenger flow, contributing to improved operational coordination and service 
quality across the airport. 
 
 
 
 
 
 CUSS (Common Use Self Service) 
The CUSS enables passengers to independently complete various airport services, including check -
in, using shared kiosks. It features advanced equipment such as readers, and scanners for quick 
passenger identification and efficient processing. Additionally,  the system incorporates biometric 
recognition technology to expedite passport control and boarding procedures, significantly enhancing 
passenger experience and optimizing airport resource utilization.

 PAS (Public Address System) 
The public address system in passenger terminals delivers operational information, guidance, 
emergency alerts, and background music with clarity and proper sound quality. It supports regional 
manual broadcasts and consists of four main units: Input, Control, Amplification, and Output.

 PMR (Professional Mobile Radio) 
The Trunked Radio System (TRS), also known as TETRA  or PMR, enhances frequency efficiency 
by sharing designated channels among users, supporting modes like group, broadcast, individual, and 
emergency communication with PTT functionality. It also integrates with public networks (PSTN). 
Long Thanh Airport plans to implement the TETRA system in the 410–430 MHz band, establishing 
a private TRS network for efficient operations, seamless communication, and emergency 
management. Users include aviation security, control tower staff, ground handlers, and maintenance 
teams. Specifications may vary depending on the contractor.

 TOIP (Telephone Over Internet Protocol) System 
Airport voice telephony includes PBX systems for external and internal calls and emergency phones 
for critical situations. Traditional PBX systems are transitioning to IP PBX with VoIP technology. 
Long Thanh Airport plans to adopt an IP PBX system, featuring call processing, voicemail, and 
messaging functionalities through a central server and application software. 
 
 
 
Figure 46 TOIP Schematic Diagram 
 
 MCS (Master Clock System) 
The Master Clock system is operated to provide local standard time information to airport users, 
related organizations, and tenants, as well as to supply standard time to all facilities and systems for 
airport operations. Analog or digital clocks with high visibility are installed at key points in passenger 
terminals to display standard time information. Additionally, each system maintains synchroniz ed 
time using the NTP protocol.

 DNE (Data Network Equipment) 
The Data Network Equipment (DNE) in the airport primarily refers to the components that form the 
wired computer network based on the TCP/IP protocol. This Campus LAN (Local Area Network) is 
designed with routers, core switches, distribution switches, and access switches, ensuring efficient 
and reliable data transmission. The network has transitioned to an all-IP-based system to handle large 
volumes of multim edia data, including IPTV, IP PBX, and IP CCTV, reflecting the modern 
requirements of multimedia communication within the airport environment. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
Figure 51 DNE Schematic Diagram 
 


=== PDF PAGE 136 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 135 
 WDNE (Wireless Data Network Equipment) 
The airport's Wireless Data Network Equipment (WDNE) consists of a WLAN based on the IEEE 
802.11 standard, enabling local data transmission through access points. It uses a one -to-many 
communication method but experiences reduced speeds as connected device s increase and is 
vulnerable to security risks inherent to wireless communication. In airports, WDNE is essential not 
only for providing a dedicated WLAN for BRS devices used in baggage reconciliation, but also for 
supporting separate WLAN networks for avi ation-related processes on PPS devices, external units 
operating at the airport, and free Wi -Fi access for passengers . With the widespread use of 
smartphones, offering Free WiFi has become a key factor in ACI Airport Service Quality (ASQ) 
evaluations, enhancing passenger satisfaction and operational efficiency. 
 
 
 
 ETC (Electronic Toll Collection system) System 
The Long Thanh Airport ETC system is designed to support dedicated entry/exit lanes for cars as 
well as dedicated or mixed lanes for motorcycles. RFID readers and cameras installed at the 
entrance/exit lanes connecting major external roads to the internal terminal and parking areas identify

 Vehicle Entry 
o The ANPR camera scans the license plate; upon successful recognition, the barrier 
opens and the system logs the entry. 
o By viewing VMS updates, drivers can avoid fully occupied areas (full-lane signage) 
and seek alternative spaces. 
 Parking & Dwell Time 
o The central operation server automatically tracks how long each vehicle has been 
parked. 
o If certain areas approach full capacity, both A POC staff and VMS displays are 
updated instantly, allowing drivers to redirect to less crowded zones. 
 Vehicle Exit 
o When the vehicle approaches the exit, the barrier opens and exit time plus payment 
details are recorded in the system. 
o APOC personnel can monitor real-time revenue and remaining parking availability.

 SCS (Structured Cabling System) 
The Structured Cabling System (SCS) is designed to provide a robust physical infrastructure for all 
Long Thanh Airport ICT services within the facility. It ensures high-speed data transmission, reliable 
availability, and flexible future growth through a standardized, hierarchical cabling approach. By 
adhering to international norms and local regulations, the SCS underpins mission-critical operations, 
enhances maintenance efficiency, and minimizes service downtime.

 Cat6A to support 1GbE up to 10GbE in work areas

 Dedicated shafts, conduits, and trays designed to protect cables from physical damage

 Fire-rated materials, organized cable management, and adequate space for expansions

 Structured labeling compliant with TIA/EIA-606 (or equivalent)

 Data Center 
The Data Centers at Long Thanh International Airport comply with Tier -3 standards (TIA-942B) to 
ensure high reliability and security. Two mirrored Data Centers provide full redundancy for 
mechanical cooling, power, and network systems, guaranteeing operational continuity. 
The Data Center is responsible for: 
A. Failure reporting and system operation 
B. DNE, WDNE, Cybersecurity, DCH and entire traffic management 
C. Cybersecurity/network monitoring and access control 
D. Manage/monitor configuration and control changes in the Data center infrastructure 
E. Preventive inspections, failure handling, and configuration management 
 
 Data Center Operation 
A. System Monitoring 
 Collect and classify data from Element Management System (EMS) and Network 
Management System (NMS) 
 Identify system availability, failure status, and modification schedules 
 Notify and record unusual events for further analysis 
B. Reporting and dissemination & Reporting Procedures 
 Reporting and dissemination of failures during system operation 
 Initial Report (10 min): Failure details, impact assessment, and SMS notification 
 Intermediate Report (30 min): Progress updates and network team support 
 Final Report (1hr after resolution): Completion report and customer notification 
C. System Improvement  
 Develop improvement plans, get approvals, execute enhancements, and monitor results

 IPTV 
In the past, airport TVs were referred to as MATV (Master Antenna Television), which primarily 
consisted of a communal antenna and headend equipment, along with ancillary facilities, mainly used 
for watching terrestrial television. However, due to the natu re of airports, satellite broadcasting was 
required, and airports added a system called S(Satellite)MATV, which enabled satellite broadcasting 
reception. Currently, airports are transitioning to IPTV (Internet Protocol TV) to meet the diverse 
needs of passengers. 
 
 
 
 
Figure 56 IPTV Schematic Diagram  

=== PDF PAGE 146 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 145 
Major Applications of IPTV in Airports 
A. Providing Content in Waiting Areas 
 Providing necessary information for passengers such as flight schedules, duty -free shop 
information, exchange rates, etc. 
 Offering entertainment channels to enhance passenger satisfaction. 
B.  Gate/Lounge IPTV 
 Dynamically providing information on boarding order/gate changes, etc. 
 Offering premium content in VIP lounges, airline-specific lounges, etc. 
C. Advertising/Promotional Channels 
 Broadcasting advertisements for new services and promotions of the airport operating 
company or partner companies. 
 Also applicable for promoting various international events and tourist attractions. 
D.  Airport Operations Support 
 Internal broadcasts to support employee tasks (training, guidelines, announcements). 
 Transmitting response measures and real-time command systems in emergency situations. 
 
IPTV System Configuration 
A. Head-End Equipment 
 Content Ingest Server: Collects and manages broadcasting channels and VoD content. 
 Encoder & Transcoder: Converts external sources (digital, analog) into IP -based 
streaming signals. 
 DRM (Digital Rights Management) Server: Protects content and manages licenses. 
B. Middleware 
 The core of IPTV service operations, handling user management, channel scheduling, 
content metadata management, etc. 
 Provides subscriber authentication (including SSO integration), channel guides, and 
Electronic Program Guides (EPG). 
C. Network (Backbone & Access Layer) 
 Establishing a stable Gigabit/10-Gigabit Ethernet environment throughout the airport. 
 Requires L3 switches with IGMP snooping/proxy and QoS configuration for handling 
multicast traffic.

 Media Hardware (MH) 
The Airport Media Hardware System is an integrated solution that encompasses the Electronic 
Visual Information Display System (EVIDS) used at airports, along with various display devices and 
control units. 
The primary objective of this system is to provide real -time visualized information to airport users 
and operators. By doing so, it effectively delivers flight operation information, guidance messages, 
and advertising content, thereby enhancing passenger convenience and improving airport operational 
efficiency. Media hardware is deployed throughout the airport terminal and supports a wide range of 
services such as flight information display, airport guidance, advertisements, and promotional content 
through digital displays. It has become a core component that underpins the smart operation of 
modern airports.

2.15 Navigation systems 
Navigation facilities are essential for the safe and efficient operation of air traffic and must be 
maintained to ensure continuous 24-hour functionality. At Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA), 
the operation and maintenance of these facilities are divided between two main organizations: 
A. The Airports Corporation of Vietnam (ACV) is responsible for providing services and 
conducting maintenance for AGL facilities and ILS, DME systems.  
B. The Vietnam Air Traffic Management Corporation (VATM) oversees the operation and 
maintenance of a broader range of systems, including VOR, VHF, VCCS, ATIS, PSR/SSR, 
ARTS, SMR, MLAT, ADS-B, A-SMGCS, and meteorological facilities. 
In the LTIA construction project, the navigation facilities are developed as part of two key 
components: 
A. Component Project No.2 focuses on air traffic management facilities, including the Air 
Traffic Control Tower, ancillary facilities, and technical systems for information, 
navigation, surveillance, and meteorological services. This project also includes the  
VATM office and is managed by VATM. 
 
Figure 47 Air traffic management facilities 
 
B. Component Project No.3 encompasses the essential facilities at LTIA, particularly airfield 
systems such as AGL, ILS, and DME. This component is managed by ACV.

Through the structured division of responsibilities between ACV and VATM, the navigation facilities 
at LTIA are expected to meet the high operational and safety standards required for seamless air 
traffic management. 
 CNS facilities 
 
Figure 60 CNS facilities over view 
 
At Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA), CNS facilities play a crucial role in ensuring safe and 
efficient air traffic operations. These facilities provide essential navigation and control information, 
including azimuth, distance, position, and flight data, to assist aircraft entering or departing the airport. 
In addition to supporting general air traffic control, these navigation services are specifically designed 
to facilitate precision operations, such as CAT-II instrument landing, enabling aircraft to land safely 
under low -visibility conditions. By offering comprehensive navigation and control capabilities, 
LTIA’s CNS facilities ensure seamless and reliable support for all air traffic operations.

 Communication facility 
Facilitates the exchange of information between air traffic controllers, pilots, and other aviation 
entities, integrating data from meteorological systems to provide accurate airport weather information, 
ensuring seamless coordination and safety in air traffic operations. 
Key Facilities: 
A. VHF/UHF Radios: Enables voice communication between pilots and controllers over short 
and long distances. 
B. VCCS (Voice Communication Control System): Manages and integrates multiple 
communication channels, allowing seamless interaction between controllers and other 
stakeholders. 
C. AFTN (Aeronautical Fixed Telecommunication Network): Transmits flight plans, weather 
data, and NOTAMs efficiently across global aviation networks. 
D. ATIS (Automatic Terminal Information Service): Provides continuous broadcasts of non-
control information, including weather updates, runway conditions, and operational notices, 
reducing workload for controllers and improving situational awareness for pilots. 
 
 Navigation facility 
Provides aircraft with accurate positioning and guidance for safe and efficient flight operations, 
particularly during landing and en -route navigation. It supports precise routing and ensures safe 
landings under adverse weather or low-visibility conditions. 
Key Facilities: 
A. ILS (Instrument Landing System): Ensures precision guidance during approach and 
landing. 
B. VOR/DME (VHF Omnidirectional Range and Distance Measuring Equipment): Offers en-
route navigation and distance measurement. 
C. GNSS (Global Navigation Satellite System): Satellite -based navigation providing global 
positioning and routing. 
 

=== PDF PAGE 153 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 152 
 Surveillance facility 
Surveillance systems track and monitor aircraft positions of aircraft and vehicles within the 
maneuvering area in real -time, enhancing situational awareness, ensuring safety, and optimizing 
airspace utilization. It also enables the operation of airfield lighting systems, providing visual 
guidance for ground and air traffic. 
Key facilities 
A. PSR: Detects aircraft positions using reflected radar signals without transponders. 
B. SSR: Tracks aircraft with transponders, providing altitude and identification. 
C. ADS-B: Offers GPS-based real-time aircraft position data. 
D. MLAT: Triangulates aircraft and vehicle positions for precise tracking. 
E. A-SMGCS: Monitors and guides ground movements, controlling airfield lighting for safety. 
F. . 
 Aeronautical Lighting Systems 
  Basic Components of Aeronautical Lighting Systems 
Aeronautical lighting systems consist of key components that ensure reliable and efficient airport 
operations. They use various light sources, including incandescent bulbs, halogen lamps, fluorescent 
lights, and long -lasting LEDs. Lighting types include in -pavement and elevated lights, tailored for 
specific areas like runways, taxiways, and approaches. Power is supplied through grids, generators, 
and UPS, with constant current regulators (CCR) ensuring stable brightness. Advanced control 
systems like ALCS, A-SMGCS manage and automate lighting operations, while brightness levels can 
be adjusted in multiple stages to suit operational needs. 
 
 
Figure 61 Aeronautical Lighting Systems over view

Category Details 
Light Sources 
Incandescent Bulbs: Up to 500 hours. 
Halogen Lamps: Up to 1,500 hours. 
Fluorescent Lights: Up to 8,000 hours. 
LEDs: Up to 100,000 hours, offering superior longevity. 
Types of Lights 
In-Pavement Lights: Used for runways, taxiways, and stop bars; available in 
unidirectional, bidirectional, and omnidirectional types. 
8-inch Lights: Installed in touchdown zones (TDZ), runway centerlines, 
RETIL, and stop bars. 
12-inch Lights: Used for approach, runway thresholds, runway edges, and 
runway ends. 
Elevated Lights: Applied for approach, PAPI, runway thresholds, edges, ends, 
stop bars, runway guard lights, taxiway edges, obstacles, and guidance signs; 
available in unidirectional, bidirectional, and omnidirectional types. 
Power Supply 
Primary Power Sources: Includes power grids, generators, and UPS. 
Constant Current Regulators (CCR): Maintains consistent brightness levels 
using microprocessor control. 
Power Distribution: Includes circuit selectors, series circuit isolators, 
primary/secondary cables, isolation transformers, connector kits, and leads. 
Control Systems 
ALCS (Airfield Lighting Control System): Manages and monitors lighting 
systems. 
A-SMGCS (Advanced Surface Movement Guidance and Control System): 
Supports advanced ground movement guidance. 
AGLAS (Airfield Ground Lighting Automation System): Automates airfield 
lighting operations. 
Brightness Control 
Lighting brightness can be adjusted in five stages: 1%, 3%, 10%, 30%, and 
100%. 
Low-Intensity Systems: Provide three stages: 10%, 30%, and 100%. 
 
 
 Key Lighting Facilities 
Aeronautical lighting systems ensure safe aircraft navigation and operations, especially in low 
visibility. Runway lights guide takeoffs and landings, while taxiway lights direct aircraft between 
runways and terminals. Approach lights assist in aligning aircraft during landing. Special ized lights 
like PAPI, guard lights, and obstruction lights provide additional safety and visual cues, enhancing 
overall airport operations.

 Power Supply and Control 
The CCR supplies constant current to the series circuit, maintaining consistent brightness in all lights. 
The controller communicates with remote units to monitor the system's performance and identify 
faults. If a failure occurs (e.g., a lamp is open or malfunctioning), the system isolates the fault and 
alerts operators for maintenance. 

=== PDF PAGE 156 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 155 
 
Figure 62 Airfield Lighting System Configuration Diagram 
 
A. Constant Current Regulators (CCR) 
 CCRs supply steady current to lighting circuits, controlled via microprocessors. 
 Maximum voltage depends on circuit resistance and the number of connected lights. 
 Typical current ratings: 6.6A or 20A. 
B. Series Circuits 
 Most lighting systems use series circuits for consistent current control and reduced copper 
usage. 
C. Isolation Transformers 
 Separate high-voltage circuits from lighting units, enhancing safety and preventing full 
circuit shutdowns during faults. 
D. Low Harmonic CCR 
 Generates sine wave outputs, reducing harmonics for better performance under non-linear 
loads. 
 Utilizes IGBT technology for high efficiency and rapid switching. 
E. Remote Control Systems 
 Enable centralized control and monitoring of lighting systems.

3.2 Architecture maintenance  
Architecture maintenance is essential to ensuring the functionality, safety, and longevity of Long 
Thanh International Airport's architectural facilities. Through regular and ad -hoc inspections, 
diagnostics, and proactive measures, building maintenance achieves the following key goals: 
 
A. Safety and Compliance: Prevents structural failures, mitigates risks, and ensures 
adherence to Vietnamese regulations and international standards. 
B. Operational Efficiency: Reduces disruptions through proactive maintenance, ensuring 
seamless airport operations and optimizing resource usage. 
C. Asset Preservation and Sustainability: Protects and extends the lifespan of structural and 
architectural components while implementing strategies to reduce environmental impact 
and energy consumption. 
D. Aesthetic and Functional Quality: Preserves the iconic design inspired by the 
Vietnamese lotus while ensuring all facilities remain fully operational, visually appealing, 
and comfortable for users. 
 Inspection 
Introduction 
This section outlines the procedures and scope of inspection activities for architectural facilities at 
the airport, including regular, ad -hoc, and equipment -assisted inspections for both interior and 
exterior elements. 
 
Key Requirements 
Inspections must comply with Vietnamese construction regulations (e.g., Decree 06/2021/ND-CP), 
relevant QCVN standards, and international practices (e.g., ICAO Annex 14). The process shall 
cover structural integrity, material condition, water ingress, and visual safety indicators. Specialized 
inspections using drones, thermal cameras, or other technologies may be applied based on risk level. 
 
Outcome 
Timely detection of defects, enhanced preventive maintenance accuracy, and reliable records for 
future asset planning and renovation strategies. 
 

=== PDF PAGE 162 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 161 
Conclusion 
Systematic inspections ensure operational continuity, safety, and lifecycle efficiency of airport 
infrastructure, and form the basis for informed maintenance decision-making. 
 
 Types of Maintenance Activities 
Maintenance activities for architectural facilities are categorized into three major types, each 
defined by its purpose, execution timing, scope, and methodology. This classification ensures a 
proactive and systematic approach to sustaining the functionality, safety, and longevity o f airport 
buildings. 
A. Regular Inspection 
 Purpose: To monitor the general condition of architectural components and detect any 
obvious deterioration or wear. 
 Timing: Conducted at fixed intervals (e.g., monthly, quarterly, or annually), as specified 
in the maintenance schedule. 
 Scope: 
o Visual inspection of surfaces (e.g., walls, ceilings, floors, façades) 
o Identification of cracks, discoloration, water leakage, material detachment 
B. Preventive Inspection 
 Purpose: To detect early signs of hidden or developing damage before it becomes serious, 
and to prevent structural and functional degradation. 
 Timing: Conducted periodically with a focus on high -risk or critical zones (e.g., roof 
structures, expansion joints, exterior cladding). 
 Scope: 
o Areas prone to thermal expansion, corrosion, or vibration 
o Sealant joints, façade anchors, suspended ceilings, etc. 
C. Corrective Inspection 
 Purpose: To identify the root cause and extent of a detected defect or damage, and 
determine the appropriate corrective actions. 
 Timing: Triggered immediately upon detection of abnormalities or user-reported issues. 
 Scope: 
 
o Areas showing significant cracks, deformation, moisture ingress, detachment, etc.

o Investigation of structural or finish failures affecting safety or usability

 Exterior and Interior Finishes Maintenance 
 Exterior and Interior finishes play a key role in the functionality, aesthetics, and passenger experience 
at Long Thanh International Airport. Although not directly contributing to structural stability, their 
proper maintenance ensures a safe and visually appealing environment. 
 
A. Preserving Aesthetic Appeal: Well-maintained finishes uphold the airport’s iconic design 
and visual identity, reflecting its commitment to excellence. 
B. Protecting Underlying Structures: Proper maintenance of exterior finishes prevents 
moisture intrusion, which could damage structural components over time. 
C. Ensuring Safety: Preventing hazards such as loose tiles, cracked panels, or water leakage 
reduces the risk of accidents and injuries. 
D. Enhancing Passenger Experience: Clean, functional, and visually pleasing environments 
improve passenger comfort and satisfaction.

 Maintenance Procedures for Non-structural Element (Exterior and Interior 
Finishes) 
Exterior finishes play a vital role in protecting the building envelope from environmental factors 
while maintaining the visual identity of Long Thanh International Airport. Proper maintenance 
ensures durability, aesthetic appeal, and functional integrity, preventing premature deterioration and 
costly repairs. This section outlines the inspection protocols, common damage cau ses, and effective 
repair methods for exterior finishes. 
 
Causes of Damage to Non-structural 
A. Environmental Exposure: Prolonged exposure to UV radiation, rain, wind, and 
temperature fluctuations can cause discoloration, cracking, or peeling. 


=== PDF PAGE 167 ===
Long Thanh International Airport

B. Mechanical Damage: Impact from equipment or operational activities may result in dents,

C. Moisture Intrusion: Ineffective waterproofing or damaged seals can lead to seepage,

D. Aging and Weathering: Over time, materials such as stone cladding, metal panels, or 
glass may lose their original properties, requiring restoration or replacement.

 Inspection Protocols 
Regular inspections help identify and address potential issues in exterior finishes. 
Visual Inspection 
A. Assess surfaces for discoloration, cracks, or peeling paint. 
B. Inspect joints, seals, and flashing for signs of wear or failure. 
Adhesion Testing  
Test the bond strength of cladding and panels to ensure secure attachment. 
Moisture Testing 
Use moisture meters to detect water infiltration in walls and cladding systems. 
 
 Maintenance and Repair Methods 
Masonry Work 
A. Main Defects: Cracked mortar joints, efflorescence, dislodged bricks. 
B. Inspection Method: Visual inspection for cracks and white salt deposits; tap testing for 
hollowness. 
C. Repair Method: 
 Remove old or loose mortar with a chisel or scraper. 
 Clean joints and refill with mortar or epoxy filler. 
 Use neutral-pH cleaner to remove efflorescence. 
D. Repair Tools: Trowel, brush, chisel, water sprayer. 
Waterproofing 
A. Main Defects: Membrane detachment, water leakage, sealant cracks. 
B. Inspection Method: Water spray test, check for sealant failure and membrane lifting. 
C. Repair Method: 
 Remove damaged waterproofing layers and clean the base. 
 Apply primer and install new membrane or sealant. 
 Seal overlaps and terminations for watertightness. 
D. Repair Tools: Torch gun, sealant gun, scraper, moisture meter. 
Tiling 

=== PDF PAGE 169 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 168 
A. Main Defects: Loose or broken tiles, joint loss. 
B. Inspection Method: Tap test for hollowness, visual inspection of cracks and missing grout. 
C. Repair Method: 
 Remove loose or damaged tiles and clean the base. 
 Reattach with tile adhesive; level and press. 
 Apply grout and finish surface. 
D. Repair Tools: Tile cutter, rubber mallet, adhesive, grout tool. 
Stone Work 
A. Main Defects: Cracks, discoloration, panel detachment. 
B. Inspection Method: Visual and tap inspection; check for gaps and stains. 
C. Repair Method: 
 Re-anchor or re-bond loose panels using epoxy. 
 Clean surface stains with appropriate cleaner. 
 Seal joints if necessary to prevent water infiltration. 
D. Repair Tools: Anchor kit, epoxy gun, stone cleaner. 
Metal Work 
A. Main Defects: Corrosion, peeling, loose bolts or brackets. 
B. Inspection Method: Visual check for rust, touch test for loose fasteners. 
C. Repair Method: 
 Remove rust with wire brush or sandpaper. 
 Apply primer and corrosion-resistant paint. 
 Tighten or replace hardware. 
D. Repair Tools: Wrench, wire brush, anti-rust paint. 
Paint 
A. Main Defects: Peeling, fading, chalking. 
B. Inspection Method: Visual inspection and tape adhesion test. 
C. Repair Method: 
 Scrape off peeling or damaged paint. 
 Apply primer and finish coat. 
 Match color and texture with surrounding area. 
D. Repair Tools: Scraper, roller, brush, paint set.

Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 169 
Interior Finish 
A. Main Defects: Surface damage, peeling, mold stains. 
B. Inspection Method: Visual inspection for cracks, bubbles, and discoloration. 
C. Repair Method: 
 Remove damaged area and clean surface. 
 Refinish with putty or repaint. 
 Apply mold-resistant coating if needed.

3.3 Structural Safety 
Regularly inspecting and maintaining load -bearing elements like reinforced concrete and steel 
structures to ensure stability and safety. 
 
 Structural Safety Inspection 
Introduction 
This section covers the inspection of structural elements in airport buildings to ensure their stability, 
durability, and safety under both normal operating conditions and external impacts such as weather, 
vibration, and aging. It includes key structural members such as beams, slabs, columns, roof trusses, 
and load-bearing walls. 
Key Requirements 
Structural inspections shall be conducted in accordance with Vietnamese regulations (e.g., Decree 
06/2021/ND-CP, QCVN structural codes), and include visual inspections, crack and deformation 
monitoring, load condition checks, and non -destructive testing (NDT) when necessary. High -risk 
structures must be prioritized, and inspections must be documented and traceable. 
Outcome 
These inspections aim to prevent structural failures, ensure user safety, and maintain compliance 
with legal and operational standards. Data gathered will support maintenance planning, risk 
mitigation, and facility lifecycle decisions. 
Conclusion 
A structured approach to structural safety inspection helps maintain the integrity of airport 
infrastructure, reduces emergency repair costs, and reinforces public trust in facility safety. 
 
 Types of Structural Safety Inspection 
Regular Inspection 
Periodic inspections (e.g., annually or biennially) to visually assess structural components such as 
columns, beams, slabs, and joints for cracks, deflection, corrosion, or visible damage. 

=== PDF PAGE 175 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 174 
 
Preventive Maintenance 
Targeted inspections using technical tools to detect early-stage degradation in critical areas exposed 
to stress, weathering, or vibration.   
 
 
Corrective Maintenance 
Performed when a structural abnormality is reported or suspected (e.g., large cracks, tilting, leaks, 
or excessive settlement ). Involves crack gauges, laser scanners, moisture meters, or thermal 
imaging

Long Thanh International Airport

 Distinguishing Structural and Non-Structural Defects in Inspection Stages 
In the inspection stages of architectural facilities, distinguishing between structural and non-structural 
defects is of paramount importance. Structural defects, depending on their severity, can significantly 
impact the safety of users. Therefore, immedia te measures such as partial suspension of facility use 
or urgent reinforcement must be implemented to mitigate potential risk. 
 


=== PDF PAGE 178 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 177 
 Purpose and Importance of Structural Safety Maintenance 
Maintaining the structural safety of architectural facilities is vital for the uninterrupted and safe 
operation of Long Thanh International Airport. A robust inspection and maintenance program ensures 
the long -term stability and functionality of the airpor t, supports regulatory compliance, and 
safeguards the lives of all who use the facility. 
 
A. Ensuring Safety and Preventing Accidents: Timely identification of potential structural 
issues, such as cracks, deformation, or tilting, minimizes the risk of catastrophic failures 
that could endanger passengers, staff, and assets. 
B. Preserving Structural Integrity: Regular maintenance ensures that the load -bearing 
capacity and stability of critical components, including concrete and steel structures, are 
retained over time, even under dynamic operational conditions. 
C. Extending Facility Lifespan: Proactive maintenance reduces wear and tear, delays the 
need for major repairs or reconstruction, and ensures that facilities remain in optimal 
condition throughout their design life. 
D. Compliance with Regulations: Adherence to national and international safety standards, 
ensures the facility meets legal requirements, avoiding potential penalties or operational 
disruptions.

 Concrete Structure Safety 
Concrete structures form a critical component of Long Thanh International Airport's architectural 
facilities. Ensuring their durability and safety requires comprehensive maintenance procedures to 
prevent structural degradation and enhance operational relia bility. This section outlines the key 
inspection and maintenance activities, focusing on crack management, surface treatment, and 
reinforcement integrity. 
 
 Causes of Damage to Concrete Structures 
A. Environmental Factors: Prolonged exposure to moisture, temperature fluctuations, and 
chemical agents can lead to cracking, spalling, and carbonation of concrete. 
B. Structural Load: Excessive or uneven load distribution can cause deformation, cracking, 
or settlement. 
C. Corrosion of Reinforcement: Deterioration of embedded steel reinforcement due to 
chloride penetration or carbonation weakens the overall structural capacity. 
D. Construction Defects: Poor workmanship, insufficient curing, or improper material 
selection can result in voids, honeycombing, or weak bonding. 
 
 Concrete Structure Inspection Protocols 
Concrete structure inspections are conducted to identify and address potential risks early.  
En Visual Inspection 
A. Cracks, spalling, and discoloration. 
B. Signs of reinforcement corrosion or water seepage. 
 
Non-Destructive Testing (NDT) 
A. Ultrasonic pulse velocity testing to detect internal voids or cracks. 
B. Rebound hammer testing to assess surface strength. 
 
Core Sampling and Laboratory Testing 
A. Extraction of concrete cores for compressive strength analysis. 

=== PDF PAGE 180 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 179 
B. Carbonation depth measurement to evaluate the progression of concrete deterioration. 
 
  Concrete Structure Maintenance and Repair Methods 
Concrete repair methods should vary based on the severity of damage. The following guidelines 
outline specific repair techniques based on the extent of the damage. 
 
Crack Repair 
A. Hairline Cracks (<0.3mm ): Apply surface sealants or coatings to prevent moisture 
ingress. 
B. Moderate Cracks (0.3mm - 1mm): Use cementitious grout or polymer-modified mortars 
to fill and seal the cracks. 
C. Large Structural Cracks (>1mm ): Perform epoxy injection to restore load -bearing 
capacity and structural integrity. 
 
Spalling Repair 
A. Minor spalling: Patch the area using polymer-modified mortar 
B. Severe spalling with exposed reinforcement: Remove loose material, clean and treat the 
reinforcement with anti-corrosion agents, and apply high-strength repair mortar. 
 
Carbonation Mitigation 
A. Apply carbonation-resistant coatings to prevent further deterioration. 
B. Replace or repair sections where carbonation depth compromises reinforcement. 
Surface Treatment 
A. Apply hydrophobic sealers to enhance water resistance. 
B. Repaint or recoat damaged areas to restore protective and aesthetic finishes. 
 
Spalling Repair 
A. Re-coating of exposed reinforcement with anti-corrosion agents 
B. Removal of damaged concrete and application of patch repair mortar. 
 

=== PDF PAGE 181 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 180 
Carbonation Mitigation 
A. Application of protective coatings or sealers to reduce CO₂ penetration. 
B. Replacement of highly carbonated sections where necessary. 
 
 Steel Structure  
Steel structures are vital components of Long Thanh International Airport, providing critical support 
and stability to the overall architectural framework. Proper maintenance ensures the durability and 
safety of these structures, preventing corrosion, defo rmation, and other forms of structural 
degradation. This section outlines inspection protocols, common damage causes, and effective repair 
methods. 
 
 Causes of Damage to Steel Structures 
A. Corrosion: Exposure to moisture, salt, and pollutants accelerates rust formation, reducing 
the strength of steel components. 
B. Fatigue and Stress: Repeated load cycles cause micro -cracks and material fatigue, 
potentially leading to structural failure. 
C. Deformation: Excessive loading or improper installation may result in bending, buckling, 
or warping of steel elements. 
D. Connection Failures: Loose or damaged bolts, weld defects, and misaligned connections 
compromise structural integrity. 
 
 Inspection Protocols 
Steel structure inspections focus on identifying early signs of deterioration. Key inspection activities 
include 
Visual Inspection 
A. Check for rust, deformation, and damaged connections. 
B. Inspect welds, bolts, and joints for visible signs of wear or looseness. 
Non-Destructive Testing (NDT) 
A. Ultrasonic testing to detect internal flaws or cracks. 

=== PDF PAGE 182 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 181 
B. Magnetic particle inspection for surface and near-surface defects. 
Load Testing 
Conduct load tests to verify the structural capacity under expected service conditions. 
 
 Maintenance and Repair Methods 
Corrosion Control 
A. Cleaning and Coating:  Remove rust using mechanical or chemical methods and apply 
anti-corrosion coatings. 
B. Cathodic Protection : Install sacrificial anodes to protect steel from electrochemical 
corrosion. 
Structural Reinforcement 
A. Strengthen deformed or weakened components using steel plates or braces. 
B. Replace severely corroded or damaged sections to restore structural integrity. 
Connection Repairs 
A. Tighten or replace loose bolts. 
B. Repair welds using appropriate welding techniques and inspect for quality assurance. 
 
 Safety Measures During Maintenance 
A. Access Control: Secure the work area to prevent unauthorized entry and ensure worker 
safety. 
B. Load Management: Temporarily redistribute loads to reduce stress on affected 
components during repairs. 
C. Personal Protective Equipment (PPE ): Provide workers with appropriate safety gear, 
including gloves, helmets, and harnesses. 
 
 Documentation and Reporting 
A. Inspection Reports: Record findings, including photographic evidence and detailed 
descriptions of defects. 

=== PDF PAGE 183 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 182 
B. Repair Logs: Maintain a record of all maintenance and repair activities, including 
materials and techniques used. 
C. Follow-Up Plans: Schedule future inspections and preventive maintenance based on repair 
outcomes.

3.5 Water supply and drainage systems 
The water supply system at Long Thanh International Airport consists of a pumping station and 
energy center, which supply water to both the terminal and airport areas. The drainage system is 
designed to efficiently manage stormwater runoff across different  catchment areas using ground 
slopes and drainage channels. Maintenance activities are planned for each system’s facilities, divided 
into regular inspection, preventive maintenance, and corrective maintenance.

 Water supply system  
The operation and maintenance of the water supply systems aim to: 
A. Ensure an uninterrupted and consistent supply of water to the terminal, airport facilities, 
and surrounding areas to support daily operations and passenger services. 
B. Maintain water quality standards in line with national and international regulations, 
ensuring potable water is safe for consumption and meets all health and environmental 
requirements. 
C. Optimize energy usage in the water supply process, including the pumping station and 
energy center, to reduce energy consumption, lower operational costs, and contribute to 
the airport's sustainability goals. 
D. Implement preventive maintenance programs to minimize operational disruptions, reduce 
the risk of water supply interruptions, and optimize the lifespan of equipment and 
infrastructure, ensuring cost-efficient system operation.

A. APOC (Airport Operations Control Center) 
 Monitor system dashboards for flow rate, pressure, and quality indicators. 
 Detect abnormal consumption or potential leaks through SCADA/BMS systems. 
 Report early signs of malfunction or supply inconsistency to maintenance teams. 
B. Maintenance Personnel 
 Conduct physical inspections of pumps, valves, meters, backflow preventers, and tanks. 
 Check for signs of corrosion, leakage, vibration, or pressure loss. 
 Collect water samples for quality checks (e.g., chlorine levels, pH). 
 Record findings in maintenance logs with timestamp and location. 
C. Contractors 
 Provide specialized inspections for underground pipelines and reservoir structures. 
 Use CCTV, acoustic detection, or pressure tests when needed. 
 Deliver detailed inspection results and recommend further testing if required.

 Drainage system  
The operation and maintenance of the drainage systems aim to: 
A. Design and operate the drainage system to efficiently manage stormwater runoff, 
preventing flooding in the terminal, runways, and other critical areas, especially during 
heavy rainfall. 
B. Ensure that the drainage system complies with environmental regulations, effectively 
filtering and managing stormwater to prevent contamination of local water sources and 
surrounding ecosystems. 
C. Regularly inspect and maintain the drainage system to prevent blockages, leaks, or failures, 
ensuring the system’s continuous operation and minimizing disruptions to airport 
operations.

3.6 Equipment system 
The Equipment Systems  at Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA) are essential for ensuring 
smooth operations, safety, and efficiency across all airport functions. These systems cover a wide 
range of critical areas, including power supply system, air conditioning system, firefighting 
system, navigation system, ICT system and more. Each system must undergo thorough maintenance 
procedures to ensure continuous operation and compliance with relevant safety and performance 
standards. 
In order to ensure the optimal performance and longevity of the equipment systems at Long Thanh 
International Airport (LTIA), a structured approach to maintenance is essential. This approach 
involves three key types of activities: Inspection, Preventive Maintenance , and Corrective 
Maintenance. Each of these activities plays a vital role in the continuous operation of airport systems, 
ensuring safety, efficiency, and compliance with operational standards. 
 


=== PDF PAGE 205 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 204 
 
Inspection 
Inspection refers to the systematic evaluation of equipment and systems to assess their condition 
and compliance with safety and operational standards. Inspections are typically conducted by 
external experts or third-party inspectors and are often required to meet legal or regulatory 
standards. This process focuses on identifying any potential issues, ensuring that equipment is 
operating within its prescribed parameters, and ensuring compliance with local regulations. 
A. Purpose: To evaluate the condition of equipment and verify that it is functioning within 
the required safety and regulatory standards. 
B. Key Activities:  
 Testing operational performance to ensure compliance with established standards. 
 Reviewing documentation and operational logs to verify that equipment is being 
maintained properly. 
 Assessing compliance with legal and environmental regulations. 
 
Flowchart 
The workflow is structured as follows: 
 
 
A. Preparation Phase

ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance

 Focus: Planning and readiness for emergency maintenance to ensure swift response and 
minimal downtime. 
 Roles: 
o System Operators:  Monitor system data for anomalies, maintain an emergency 
contact list, and verify the readiness of emergency shutdown procedures. 
o Maintenance Personnel: Ensure the availability of critical spare parts, tools, and 
resources required for emergency repairs. 
o Contractors: Coordinate with the internal team to understand emergency protocols, 
prepare specialized equipment, and confirm availability for immediate mobilization. 
B. Execution Phase 
 Focus: Immediate implementation of emergency maintenance tasks to restore operational 
conditions. 
 Roles: 
o System Operators: Quickly isolate the affected area, provide access to the system, 
and support real-time troubleshooting. 
o Maintenance Personnel:  Identify and repair faulty components, such as 
compressors, fans, or pumps, and implement containment measures for leaks or 
failures. 
o Contractors: Perform complex repairs, ensure compliance with safety standards, 
and collaborate with the in-house team for seamless execution. 
C. Reporting Phase 
 Focus: Documenting and communicating the details of the emergency incident and 
actions taken. 
 Roles: 
o System Operators: Provide a log of system performance during the emergency and 
document deviations from normal operations. 
o Maintenance Personnel: Record the repairs and replacements conducted, including 
the downtime and resources utilized. 
o Contractors: Submit a detailed report of specialized tasks performed, including root 
cause analysis and future prevention recommendations.

Preventive Maintenance 
Preventive Maintenance  is the proactive maintenance of equipment designed to prevent 
malfunctions and extend the service life of assets. It includes regular checks, lubrication, cleaning, 
part replacements, and other maintenance tasks that aim to keep equipment running efficiently 
and avoid unexpected failures. This is typically carried out by Maintenance Department as part 
of a scheduled, ongoing effort to ensure the system remains in peak working condition. 
A. Purpose:  
To prevent unexpected equipment failures, reduce downtime, and extend the lifespan of 
systems by performing scheduled maintenance tasks. 
B. Key Activities:  
 Regular cleaning and lubrication of equipment. 
 Replacing worn-out or outdated parts before they fail. 
 Routine calibration and adjustments to keep systems operating efficiently. 
 Checking system performance and addressing minor issues before they become major 
problems.

Corrective Maintenance 
Corrective Maintenance  is performed after a failure or breakdown has occurred. This type of 
maintenance addresses issues that were not caught during the inspection or preventive maintenance 
stages, often requiring repairs or replacements of faulty components. Corrective Maintenance is 
typically carried out by Maintenance Department or contractor depending on the complexity of 
the issue.

A. Purpose: To restore the equipment to normal operating conditions after a failure, ensuring 
that operations can continue without significant delays. 
B. Key Activities:  
 Diagnosing and identifying the root cause of the failure. 
 Repairing or replacing malfunctioning parts or systems. 
 Testing and verifying equipment after repairs to ensure it is fully operational. 
 Updating maintenance logs and reporting the incident for future analysis and prevention.

 Electrical systems 
The Electrical Systems Management and Maintenance section of the Concept of Operations outlines 
comprehensive strategies to ensure safe, reliable, and efficient operation of all electrical systems at 
Long Thanh International Airport. It covers regular insp ections, energy efficiency measures, safety 
protocols, and personnel training, aligning with international standards like ISO 50001 and 
Vietnamese regulations. The section also details preventive and corrective maintenance workflows, 
emphasizing timely fault response, risk mitigation, and system optimization. By integrating advanced 
monitoring systems such as SCADA and BMS, the airport ensures seamless power distribution and 
operational continuity. These measures are designed to enhance system performance, minimize 
downtime, and support sustainable airport operations. 
 
Key Safety Measures 
Category Details 
1. Emergency Preparedness 
Regular Outage 
Recovery Drills 
Conduct routine drills to simulate power outages and recovery scenarios. 
Train personnel to execute emergency protocols efficiently during actual 
events. 
Equipment Testing 
Verify the functionality of backup systems, including UPS, generators, and 
ATS. 
Use simulated failures to ensure seamless power transfer and recovery. 
2. Adherence to Safety Protocols 
Personnel Safety 
Ensure all staff wear appropriate protective equipment (e.g., insulated gloves, 
safety helmets). 
Enforce lockout/tagout (LOTO) procedures to prevent accidental power 
activation during maintenance. 
Worksite Safety 
Maintain clear access to emergency exits, fire extinguishers, and safety 
stations. 
Ensure all high-voltage areas are marked with warning signs and restricted to 
authorized personnel. 
3. Task-Based Risk Assessment (TBRA) 
Pre-Task Risk 
Identification 
Assess potential hazards associated with each maintenance or operational task. 
Evaluate risks related to equipment failure, electrical shock, fire, or 
environmental factors.

 Routine Inspections 
 This section outlines the procedures for the regular inspection and performance assessment of 
electrical equipment, in compliance with Vietnamese regulations. These inspections ensure the safe, 
reliable, and efficient operation of all electrical systems at Long Thanh International Airport. 
A. Compliance with Vietnamese Regulations: 
 Adhere to TCVN and QCVN national standards. 
 Ensure 22kV distribution voltage meets Circular 30/2019/TT-BCT. 
B. Key Inspection Areas: 
 Voltage levels and total harmonic distortion (THD), ensuring compliance with Circular 
30/2019/TT-BCT. 
 Insulation resistance of cables and connections to prevent power leakage. 
 Performance and alignment of switchgear, circuit breakers, and protection devices. 
 
Key tasks during inspections include: 
A. Checking transformers for insulation damage, cooling fan operation, and terminal 
connections. 
B. Testing circuit breakers for trip conditions, manual/automatic operation, and contact 
integrity. 
C. Verifying ATS functionality, including seamless transfer between power sources. 
D. Monitoring UPS battery voltage, charging status, and bypass operation. 
E. Using BMS to monitor real-time system data, including alarms, energy consumption, and 
equipment performance.

 Corrective Maintenance 
 This section outlines the corrective maintenance procedures specific to key power facilities, 
including the Power Receiving Station (PWR) , Airfield Ground Lighting Station (AGS) , and 
Airport Support Facilities (AFS). These procedures are designed to ensure the timely restoration of 
functionality, minimize downtime, and maintain operational reliability. All activities are centrally 
coordinated through the Power Control center (PWR, AGS, AFS) 
 
Role of Control Centers (APOC, Energy Center, Power Control Center) 
 
The Airport Operations Center (APOC) , Energy Center, and Power Control Center  serve as 
centralized command units for coordinating corrective maintenance across critical power 
infrastructure at Long Thanh International Airport, including the Power Receiving Station (PWR), 
Airfield Ground Lighting Station (AGS), and Airport Support Facilities (AFS). 
A. Centralized Monitoring and Supervision 
 Continuously monitor equipment status through SCADA and BMS platforms. 
 Detect anomalies in real-time and relay precise alerts to the field teams with time, location, 
and system details. 
B. Initial Response Coordination and Resource Allocation 
 Analyze fault severity and determine operational priority. 
 Mobilize the necessary technical teams, spare parts, and tools for immediate dispatch. 
C. Integrated System Management 
 Coordinate the operation of interconnected infrastructure systems, including electrical, 
lighting, and utility components. 
 Ensure seamless data integration and command execution between SCADA, BMS, IASS, 
and other platforms. 
D. Post-Recovery Evaluation and Reporting

Role of Field Teams (On-Site Maintenance Units) 
 
Field teams are responsible for executing physical inspections, repairs, component replacements, and 
system testing at the equipment site. These teams consist of in-house technicians and certified third-
party maintenance contractors across electrical, mechanical, and airfield lighting disciplines. 
A. On-Site Diagnostics and Fault Identification 
 Use visual inspection and diagnostic tools to assess faulty components based on control 
center alerts. 
 Analyze failures in MCCs, transformers, lighting units, pumps, or other critical 
components. 
B. Corrective Repair and Component Replacement 
 Remove defective equipment and install functional replacements from inventory or 
emergency stock. 
 Ensure mechanical integrity, electrical safety, and compliance with installation standards. 
C. Functional Testing and System Integration Check 
 Perform full functionality tests to verify successful restoration. 
 Confirm real-time communication and operational status with SCADA, BMS, and IASS 
systems. 
D. Reporting and Documentation 
 Document repair activities, replaced parts, and test outcomes in standardized maintenance 
logs. 
 Submit detailed reports to the control centers for compliance and recordkeeping.

 HVAC 
This section outlines the guidelines for the operation, inspection, maintenance, and emergency 
management of the HVAC (Heating, Ventilation, and Air Conditioning) system at Long Thanh 
International Airport (LTIA). The HVAC system is vital for maintaining optimal environmental 
conditions throughout the airport, ensuring passenger comfort, operational efficiency, and safety. 
Effective HVAC operations con tribute to air quality, temperature control, and energy efficiency, 
which are essential for both the comfort of airport users and the functionality of critical systems. 
 Routine Inspection 
Routine inspection focuses on regularly scheduled checks to ensure the proper functioning of 
equipment and systems. This proactive approach helps identify potential issues early, ensuring 
operational continuity and reducing the likelihood of unexpected fai lures. Routine inspections form 
the foundation of a robust maintenance program, emphasizing preventive measures to maintain safety 
and efficiency.

Chiller System: 
A. Chillers: 
 Weekly Inspection:  Verify the chiller's cooling performance, ensuring it meets the 
design cooling capacity. Check the pressure and temperature at key points to confirm 
optimal operation. 
 Monthly Inspection: Inspect the refrigerant levels to ensure that there are no leaks and 
that they are within specified parameters. Clean condenser coils and inspect for any debris, 
dirt, or corrosion that could affect heat exchange efficiency. 
B. Condenser Coils: Inspect for physical damage, debris buildup, or corrosion that could 
impair heat transfer and system efficiency. This inspection should be performed monthly 
to avoid performance issues during high demand periods. 
Air Conditioning System: 
A. AC Units: 
 Weekly Inspection: Perform performance checks for cooling efficiency, verifying if the 
system is cooling at the expected rate. Check pressure levels within the system and ensure 
that the temperature setpoints are being maintained. 
 Monthly Inspection: Verify refrigerant levels to ensure there are no leaks. Inspect the 
temperature and pressure within the system to confirm that it remains within optimal 
operational ranges. Clean condenser coils to maintain cooling efficiency and prevent 
buildup that could cause system failure. 
B. Refrigerant Lines: Check refrigerant lines for any visible damage or leaks. This includes 
checking for pressure discrepancies that could indicate an issue with the refrigerant circuit. 
Regular monitoring of these parameters will help in identifying early signs of failure. 
Mechanical Ventilation: 
A. Air Handling Units (AHUs): 
 Weekly Inspection: Inspect AHUs for dust accumulation or other debris that might clog 
the filters or affect airflow. Check that the system is operating at the designed airflow 
capacity and that no blockages are present in any part of the air handling process.

Smoke Exhaust System:

 Monthly Inspection:  Verify sensor calibration to ensure accurate readings for 
temperature, humidity, and CO2 levels. Test response times of all sensors to ensure that 
they react as expected to changes in the system’s conditions. 
 Control Panels:  Check control panels for error codes or fault messages that could

control system that may require attention or repair.

 Preventive Maintenance 
Preventive maintenance uses advanced analytics and data trends to predict and prevent failures before 
they occur. By using sensors, monitoring tools, and historical data, maintenance can be scheduled 
based on the actual condition of the equipment rather than a set in terval. This approach reduces the 
likelihood of unplanned downtime and enhances the overall efficiency and longevity of HVAC 
systems.

Chiller System: 
A. Monitor compressor and pump vibration patterns : Regular vibration analysis of 
compressors and pumps is conducted to identify any irregularities or potential failures that 
could lead to system inefficiency or breakdown. 
B. Monitor refrigerant pressure and temperature : Continuous monitoring of refrigerant 
pressures and temperatures helps to identify early signs of leaks or inefficiencies in the 
cooling cycle. 
C. Use thermal imaging to inspect coils for blockages : Thermal imaging is used to detect 
any cold spots or blockages in the condenser and evaporator coils, which can reduce the 
efficiency of the cooling process. 
D. Monitor cooling tower performance: Assessing the performance of the cooling towers, 
including water flow rates and temperature differentials, ensures that heat rejection is 
optimal and that cooling towers are operating at full capacity. 
Air Conditioning System: 
A. Analyze compressor vibration patterns to predict wear : Vibration analysis of the 
compressors is performed to predict wear and tear, allowing for early intervention before 
failure. 
B. Monitor refrigerant pressures and temperatures : Regular checks ensure refrigerant 
levels and pressures are maintained within optimal parameters, preventing system 
inefficiencies or damage. 
C. Inspect condenser coils with infrared thermography : Infrared thermography detects 
temperature inconsistencies in the condenser coils, revealing issues like dirt buildup, which 
can impair performance. 
D. Preventive analysis of expansion valve performance: Expansion valves are analyzed to 
ensure they function efficiently, as any issues could affect the overall cooling capacity. 
Mechanical Ventilation:

A. Monitor AHU (Air Handling Unit) airflow for discrepancies: Airflow discrepancies are 
identified through regular monitoring, which can point to obstructions, filter issues, or 
malfunctioning components. 
B. Track motor and fan performance data: Motor and fan data is continuously reviewed to 
detect any degradation in performance, indicating potential motor failure or inefficient 
operation. 
C. Preventive analysis of filter efficiency: Air filters are analyzed based on airflow trends to 
predict their remaining lifespan and ensure they are replaced before causing significant 
airflow issues. 
D. Monitor energy consumption trends: Monitoring energy consumption helps identify any 
sudden increases in usage, which could be due to inefficiencies or failing components. 
Smoke Exhaust System: 
A. Monitor smoke exhaust fan performance : Regular checks ensure smoke exhaust fans 
are performing as expected and can evacuate smoke efficiently during emergencies. 
B. Analyze smoke exhaust duct integrity : Duct integrity is assessed to identify potential 
leaks or blockages, ensuring that smoke can be removed without any obstructions. 
C. Preventive analysis of damper performance : Dampers are analyzed to ensure they are 
functioning correctly to regulate airflow in the smoke exhaust system, especially in 
emergencies when ventilation is critical. 
Automation Control and Monitoring System: 
A. Assess sensor health and performance : Sensors are monitored for any signs of 
malfunction or drift, as these can affect the accuracy of system controls and monitoring. 
B. Monitor BMS (Building Management System) data for system performance trends : 
By reviewing BMS data, Preventive maintenance identifies any signs of degradation in 
system performance or potential failure points in HVAC systems. 
C. Perform Preventive diagnostics on control valves and actuators : Regular diagnostics 
ensure control valves and actuators are functioning correctly, preventing issues like 
pressure imbalances or unresponsive components. 
D. Monitor control system for communication faults: Communication faults in the control 
system are identified early to ensure seamless operation of the Building Management 
System (BMS) and connected components.

 Corrective Maintenance 
Corrective maintenance is performed in response to unexpected system failures or malfunctions, 
aimed at quickly restoring the system to normal operation. The goal is to reduce operational 
disruptions, enhance the longevity of equipment, and minimize the risk of future breakdowns. 
Corrective actions are initiated immediately when a failure occurs to ensure th e HVAC systems 
remain fully functional.

Chiller System: 
A. Chillers, Compressors, and Coils: 
 Repair/Replace: Immediate repairs or replacement of malfunctioning chillers, 
compressors, or evaporator coils are essential to restore cooling efficiency. This includes 
addressing issues like freezing, blockages, or mechanical failures that hinder performance. 
 Refrigerant Leaks: If refrigerant leaks are detected, the affected areas need to be 
promptly sealed to prevent further loss of refrigerant. Immediate attention is required to 
restore the cooling capacity and maintain optimal system function. 
 Refrigerant Levels: Once leaks are fixed, refrigerant levels should be inspected and 
topped off as necessary to maintain the proper charge. Incorrect levels can cause system 
inefficiency or failure. 
Air Conditioning System: 
A. Compressors and Evaporator Coils: 
 Repair/Replace: When compressors or evaporator coils malfunction (e.g., due to 
mechanical failure or clogging), immediate repair or replacement is necessary to restore 
the cooling capacity. This includes addressing any refrigerant flow issues or damage to 
coil fins that reduce heat exchange efficiency. 
B. Refrigerant Leaks or Expansion Valves: 
 Repair/Replace: Any refrigerant leaks should be located and sealed immediately. 
Malfunctioning expansion valves, which regulate refrigerant flow to the coils, need to be 
replaced quickly to ensure proper cooling and prevent system inefficiencies or 
overheating. 

=== PDF PAGE 228 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 227 
 Top Off Refrigerant Levels:  In cases where refrigerant levels are insufficient due to 
leakage or system inefficiency, refrigerant should be topped off to ensure that the system 
operates at its maximum cooling potential. 
Mechanical Ventilation: 
A. Air Handling Units (AHUs) and Fans: 
 Repair/Replace: If AHUs or fans break down, immediate replacement or repair is 
required. This could involve fixing broken fans, motors, or other components to ensure 
the proper circulation of air. A failure in these components could cause a disruption in 
airflow and overall system efficiency. 
B. Leaks or Blockages in Ductwork: 
 Repair: Leaks or blockages in the ductwork must be addressed immediately. These issues 
can lead to poor airflow, higher energy consumption, and potentially hazardous air quality. 
Ductwork should be inspected regularly, and any obstructions or leaks should be seal ed 
promptly to restore proper airflow. 
Smoke Exhaust System: 
A. Faulty Smoke Exhaust Fans or Dampers: 
 Replace/Repair: If smoke exhaust fans or dampers malfunction, they must be repaired 
or replaced immediately. These components are critical during an emergency for 
removing smoke and providing fresh air, so they must always be in working condition. 
Failure to address this can compromise safety in case of fire. 
B. Leaks or Blockages in Smoke Exhaust Ducts: 
 Repair: Any leaks or blockages in the smoke exhaust ducts should be fixed immediately. 
These ducts are vital for the safe extraction of smoke during emergencies. A compromised 
system will not only impede smoke extraction but could also lead to dangerous 
accumulations of toxic gases within the building. 
Automation Control and Monitoring System: 
A. Malfunctioning Sensors or Control Units: 
 Replace: If sensors or control units malfunction, they must be replaced immediately. 
These components are essential for monitoring system performance and ensuring that all 
HVAC systems operate within the desired parameters. Failure to fix them will result in 
inaccurate readings and loss of control over the system. 

=== PDF PAGE 229 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 228 
B. Circuit Boards or Wiring: 
 Repair/Replace: Any faults in the circuit boards or wiring within the Building 
Management System (BMS) must be repaired or replaced. These components allow for 
communication between different parts of the HVAC system and the BMS. If 
communication is disrupted, it can lea d to operational inefficiencies, system failures, or 
complete loss of control over certain systems. 
C. Control Panels or BMS Software: 
 Fix/Update: Malfunctioning control panels or BMS software must be repaired or updated 
to restore proper system control capabilities. This can involve reprogramming, replacing 
faulty software, or calibrating systems to ensure full functionality.

 Plumbing System

airport functions, including potable water, fire protection, drainage, and sewage management. Proper 
maintenance of the plumbing system is crucial to avoid d isruptions in services and ensure the safe, 
hygienic, and efficient operation of the airport’s facilities.

Routine inspections are essential for ensuring the operational efficiency and safety of the plumbing 
system. These inspections are carried out regularly to identify any potential issues early, prevent 
failures, and confirm compliance with established perfo rmance and safety standards. Through these 
proactive checks, potential problems can be detected and resolved before they impact system 
performance or result in costly repairs.

 Fire Fighting System 
This section describes the guidelines for the operation, inspection, maintenance, and emergency 
management of the firefighting system at Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA). The firefighting 
system is designed to protect the airport’s infrastructure, staff, passengers, and assets from fire 
hazards. This system includes fire hydrants, sprinklers, fire pumps, firewater storage tanks, and 
auxiliary firefighting equipment. Maintaining these syste ms is critical for ensuring the safety and 
compliance with loc al and international fire safety regulations, as well as minimizing the risks 
associated with fire emergencies. 
 
 Routine Inspection 
Routine inspection is critical for maintaining the operational reliability and safety of the firefighting 
system. This process involves scheduled checks to proactively identify issues early, ensuring the 
system is always ready for use during emergencies. It forms the foundation for an effective 
maintenance program, focusing on preventive measures to maintain system efficiency and safety.
