Fire Hydrant System: 
A. Weekly Inspection: 
 Hydrant Pillar Accessibility:  Ensure that all fire hydrant pillars are unobstructed and 
accessible, ensuring firefighters can quickly deploy them in an emergency. This includes 
confirming that the surrounding area is clear and that no objects or vegetation block the 
operation of the hydrant. 
 Discharge Flow and Pressure: Verify the flow rate and pressure at each hydrant nozzle 
to ensure it meets the specified requirements for firefighting. If the pressure is insufficient 
or the flow is low, it may indicate issues with the supply line or pump. 
B. Monthly Inspection: 
 Hose and Nozzle Condition: Inspect the fire hoses and nozzles for wear, cracks, or other 
damage. If hoses are frayed, punctured, or show signs of degradation, they should be 
replaced to ensure they perform correctly under high-pressure conditions. 
 Activation Mechanism:  Test the activation mechanism of the hydrants, ensuring the 
quick connect and other components are functioning smoothly. The system should be able 
to start delivering water as soon as the hydrant is turned on. 
Pipe System: 
A. Weekly Inspection: 

=== PDF PAGE 238 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 237 
 Leaks and Damage: Visually inspect the firefighting pipes for visible leaks, cracks, or 
any damage that may reduce water pressure or lead to contamination. Leaks can 
significantly affect the system’s effectiveness and must be addressed promptly. 
 Flow and Pressure:  Verify that water flow and pressure are consistent throughout the 
system. Any drops in pressure may indicate a blockage or a failure in the pump or valve 
system. 
B. Monthly Inspection: 
 Corrosion and Blockages: Check for any signs of corrosion, particularly in underground 
or hard -to-reach pipes, which may not be immediately visible during weekly checks. 
Blockages can also form over time and reduce system effectiveness, so it's crucial to 
ensure the pipes are clear. 
 System Integrity: Perform checks to ensure that the pipes are adequately secured and 
supported, especially in high-stress areas. Loose fittings or unsupported pipes can lead to 
system failures during high-pressure use. 
Sprinkler System: 
A. Weekly Inspection: 
 Sprinkler Head Check:  Confirm that all sprinkler heads are properly installed and 
unobstructed. Sprinkler heads that are blocked by debris, dirt, or other materials will not 
deploy correctly during a fire emergency. 
 Activation Functionality: Test the system to ensure sprinkler heads can be activated 
properly and the flow of water is sufficient to extinguish a fire. 
B. Monthly Inspection: 
 Flow and Pressure Test:  Ensure the sprinkler system provides adequate flow and 
pressure. A lack of sufficient water can impair the sprinkler’s ability to suppress fires and 
may result in an emergency situation being uncontrollable. 
 Sprinkler Head Activation:  Verify that each sprinkler head responds to heat and 
activates at the correct temperature. Any failure to activate properly could lead to delays 
in fire suppression. 
Portable Fire Extinguisher: 
A. Weekly Inspection: 

=== PDF PAGE 239 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 238 
 Pressure Check: Ensure that the extinguisher pressure gauge is within the operational 
range. A low-pressure reading means the extinguisher might not work when needed. 
 Accessibility: Confirm that the fire extinguishers are easily accessible and not obstructed 
by furniture, debris, or other obstacles. Proper placement and visibility are essential to 
ensure quick action in case of an emergency. 
B. Monthly Inspection: 
 Condition and Functionality: Inspect the fire extinguishers for signs of damage, wear, 
or expiration. Ensure the pin is secure and the tamper seal is intact. If any extinguisher is 
expired or damaged, it must be replaced or refilled. 
 Physical Inspection:  Ensure the weight and shape of the fire extinguisher remain 
consistent, which indicates it is fully charged. If any noticeable weight loss or distortion 
occurs, the unit should be recharged. 
Gaseous Fire Suppression System: 
A. Monthly Inspection: 
 Gas Purity: Ensure that the nitrogen and FM200 systems are filled to the correct pressure 
and purity levels. If gas purity falls below the required standard, the system's ability to 
suppress fire is compromised. 
 Detector Operation:  Test all smoke and heat detectors to confirm that they are 
functioning correctly and can activate the fire suppression system promptly. 
 Nozzle Condition: Inspect all discharge nozzles for cleanliness and ensure they are free 
from debris that could block the gas flow. Proper nozzle performance is essential for 
effective fire suppression. 
Water Tanks, Fire Pumps, and Valves: 
A. Weekly Inspection: 
 Water Tank Levels: Check the water levels in the firefighting tanks to ensure they are 
filled to the required level. Insufficient water in the tank can result in inadequate fire 
suppression capability. 
 Pump and Valve Operation:  Test the fire pumps and valves to confirm they are 
operational. This includes ensuring that the pumps start automatically when needed and 
that the valves open and close properly without obstruction. 
B. Monthly Inspection: 

=== PDF PAGE 240 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 239 
 Pump and Valve Performance:  Test the operation of the pumps under various 
conditions to ensure that they function correctly. Also, check the valves for any wear and 
ensure they seal properly when closed to maintain system integrity. 
 Tank Condition:  Inspect the interior of the fire water tanks for any buildup or 
degradation that could impair the water quality or storage capacity. 
Automation Control and Monitoring System: 
A. Weekly Inspection: 
 BMS Integration: Confirm that the Building Management System (BMS) is properly 
integrated with the firefighting systems, ensuring that the systems are controlled and 
monitored effectively. 
 System Diagnostics: Review the diagnostic reports for any issues or faults within the fire 
protection systems, such as failure alerts or maintenance warnings. 
B. Monthly Inspection: 
 Sensor Calibration: Verify that all sensors are accurately calibrated to detect smoke, 
heat, and other fire hazards. Test the response time of each sensor to ensure rapid 
detection in case of a fire emergency. 
 Control Panel Monitoring: Inspect all control panels for error codes, faults, or signs of 
malfunction that may impede the operation of the firefighting systems. Immediate 
attention should be given to any abnormalities. 
Interlock System: 
A. Semi-Annual Inspection: 
 Fire Scenario Simulation: Conduct a comprehensive simulation of fire within selected 
zones to verify that the entire interlock chain responds correctly. 
 Alarm and Suppression Activation : Confirm activation of the fire alarm system and 
automatic fire suppression systems (sprinkler, gas, hydrant). 
 Smoke Control and Pressurization: Verify proper activation of smoke extraction fans, 
pressurization fans, and associated dampers to ensure smoke management. 
 Door Control and Elevator Recall: Check automatic door release functions and elevator 
recall protocols per fire zone logic. 
 Electrical Cut-Off: Ensure proper disconnection of non -essential power circuits during 
fire mode.

 Public Announcement and Warning System: Validate activation of fire alarm speakers 
and flashing warning lights. 
 Monitoring Integration : Confirm all interlock responses are properly monitored and

This inspection must be performed at least every 6 months, in accordance with TCVN 
3890:2023. 
 
 Preventive Maintenance 
Preventive maintenance aims to anticipate potential issues before they occur by monitoring system 
performance and identifying early warning signs. This proactive approach helps to extend the lifespan 
of components and minimize unscheduled downtime.

Fire Hydrant System: 
A. Pressure Levels and Flow Rate Trends:  Use pressure sensors and flow rate monitoring 
devices to track the performance of the hydrant system. Any deviations from the normal 
pressure range can signal potential issues with the system, such as blockages or valve 
malfunctions. Monitoring flow trend s helps predict if maintenance is needed before a 
critical failure occurs. 
B. Hydrant Nozzles: Implement ultrasonic sensors to monitor the condition of the hydrant 
nozzles. These sensors can detect wear or damage early, allowing for timely replacement 
to prevent malfunction during an emergency. 
C. Hydrant Pillar Integrity:  Vibration analysis can help detect structural issues in the 
hydrant pillars. This analysis identifies any unusual vibrations that may indicate cracks or 
weaknesses in the pillar, preventing future failure. 
Pipe System: 
A. Water Pressure and Flow Monitoring:  Continuous monitoring of water pressure and 
flow rates helps detect leaks or blockages early. Any decrease in pressure or inconsistent 
flow may point to issues such as damaged pipes or valves, allowing for preventive repairs 
before a full system breakdown. 
B. Ultrasound for Pipe Condition:  Use ultrasound technology to assess the wall thickness 
and integrity of the pipes. This non -invasive method detects early signs of corrosion or 
damage, allowing repairs to be scheduled proactively. 

=== PDF PAGE 243 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 242 
C. Flow Sensors:  Installing flow sensors throughout the pipe system ensures that any 
irregularities in water distribution, such as sudden drops in flow, are detected early. This 
enables maintenance teams to address problems before they affect system performance. 
Sprinkler System: 
A. Sprinkler Head Performance:  By using Preventive analytics, the performance of 
sprinkler heads can be monitored to ensure they are functioning correctly. Regular checks 
on each head’s response time and activation pattern help in anticipating when maintenance 
will be required. 
B. Flow Rate and Pressure Monitoring:  Monitoring the flow rate and pressure across the 
system helps identify blockages, leaks, or malfunctions before they cause a system -wide 
failure. These readings are crucial for ensuring the system delivers the expected volume of 
water during a fire. 
C. Thermal Imaging for Heat Detection: Thermal imaging cameras can identify abnormal 
heat patterns or hot spots in the sprinkler system, which could indicate potential fire hazards 
or malfunctioning components. 
Portable Fire Extinguisher: 
A. Pressure Monitoring: Regularly monitor the pressure levels of fire extinguishers using 
remote sensors. This data can alert maintenance teams to potential pressure drops that 
might indicate an issue with the extinguisher, allowing for early refilling or replacement. 
B. Sensor-Based Monitoring for Tampering:  Install tamper-proof sensors that detect any 
unauthorized access or damage to the extinguisher. This ensures the extinguisher will 
function properly when needed and prevents false alarms. 
Gaseous Fire Suppression System: 
A. Gas Purity Monitoring:  Use gas purity sensors to detect any deviations in nitrogen or 
FM200 purity. A drop in purity could affect the system's fire suppression capability, and 
corrective actions can be taken before it compromises performance. 
B. Smoke and Heat Detection Trends:  Analyzing the data from smoke and heat detectors 
can provide insights into system performance. Any delays or failures in detection could 
indicate an issue with the system that requires immediate attention. 
C. Pressure Sensors for Leak Detection:  Pressure sensors throughout the suppression 
system can help detect any gradual leaks or drops in pressure that could lead to system 

=== PDF PAGE 244 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 243 
failure. These early warnings allow for timely maintenance to restore the system’s 
effectiveness. 
Water Tanks, Fire Pumps, and Valves: 
A. Pump Vibration and Temperature Monitoring:  Regular vibration analysis and 
temperature monitoring of fire pumps help detect mechanical wear, overheating, or 
misalignment in the pumps. Early detection allows for scheduled maintenance or part 
replacement before pump failure. 
B. Flow Rate Analysis: Monitoring the flow rates of water through the system ensures that 
all components, including pumps and valves, are operating within their optimal range. Any 
deviation could indicate a blockage or malfunction that requires maintenance. 
C. Pressure Sensor Monitoring for Valves:  Continuous monitoring of valve pressure 
sensors helps identify early signs of malfunction or wear. This data allows for Preventive 
maintenance to replace or repair valves before they compromise the water flow. 
Automation Control and Monitoring System: 
A. BMS Component Failure Prediction:  Analyzing system logs and diagnostics from the 
Building Management System (BMS) can predict potential failures in the fire protection 
systems. By tracking historical data and identifying trends, maintenance teams can 
schedule repairs before issues escalate. 
B. Sensor Performance Tracking:  Preventive algorithms can be used to monitor sensor 
performance over time, ensuring that sensors remain accurate and responsive. Any signs 
of degradation or failure can be addressed proactively, maintaining system integrity. 
C. Control Panel Monitoring:  Data from control panels provides insights into the overall 
health of the firefighting systems. By monitoring trends and identifying irregularities, 
potential failures can be predicted, and timely maintenance can be planned. 
 
 Corrective Maintenance 
Corrective maintenance focuses on the actions taken to restore the firefighting system to normal 
operating conditions after an issue or malfunction is identified. This ensures that all components 
perform as intended when needed.

Fire Hydrant System: 
A. Damaged Hydrant Pillars: If the fire hydrant pillar is damaged or obstructed, it must be 
replaced or repaired immediately to ensure that it is fully functional during an emergency. 
B. Leaks or Cracks in the System:  Any leaks or cracks in the hydrant system should be 
addressed promptly to prevent water wastage or loss of pressure, ensuring the system 
operates as intended. 

=== PDF PAGE 246 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 245 
C. Hose and Nozzle Replacement: If the hoses or nozzles are found to be worn out, damaged, 
or corroded, they should be replaced immediately. This ensures that the hydrants will 
provide optimal water flow during firefighting operations. 
Pipe System: 
A. Leaks or Cracks: Leaks or cracks in the firefighting pipes can significantly reduce system 
pressure and effectiveness. If any are identified, they must be sealed or replaced to restore 
full functionality. 
B. Corroded or Damaged Pipes:  Over time, pipes may corrode or become physically 
damaged, leading to potential failures. Replacing these pipes ensures continuous and safe 
water flow. 
C. Blockages: Blockages within the pipes can prevent the proper flow of water. Identifying 
and clearing these blockages is essential for maintaining an effective firefighting system. 
Sprinkler System: 
A. Malfunctioning Sprinkler Heads: If any sprinkler heads are found to be malfunctioning, 
they should be replaced immediately to maintain full coverage during a fire. 
B. Faulty Flow Switches or Valves:  Any issues with the flow switches or valves, such as 
blockages or mechanical failure, should be repaired or replaced to ensure that the sprinkler 
system operates efficiently and effectively. 
C. Activation Issues: If there are problems with sprinkler head activation, such as delayed or 
no activation in response to heat, repairs should be made to restore the system's reliability. 
Portable Fire Extinguisher: 
A. Expired or Faulty Extinguishers:  Fire extinguishers that are past their expiration date, 
have missing parts, or are not functioning correctly should be replaced to ensure they will 
work when needed most. 
B. Broken Equipment: If any fire extinguishers are physically damaged (e.g., broken handle, 
punctured cylinder), they should be repaired or replaced immediately. 
C. Low Pressure: If an extinguisher shows low pressure, it should be refilled or replaced to 
guarantee proper functioning in an emergency. 
Gaseous Fire Suppression System: 
A. Gas Cylinder Malfunctions: If the nitrogen or FM200 gas cylinders are malfunctioning, 
they should be replaced immediately. Any issues with pressure or purity should be 
addressed to maintain system effectiveness. 

=== PDF PAGE 247 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 246 
B. Detector Failure: If smoke or heat detectors fail to detect a fire properly, they should be 
repaired or replaced. These detectors are crucial for activating the fire suppression system 
in time. 
C. Clogged or Damaged Nozzles:  Clogged nozzles can prevent proper dispersion of the 
gaseous agent. They should be cleaned or replaced to ensure complete coverage during a 
fire event. 
Water Tanks, Fire Pumps, and Valves: 
A. Pump Failures:  Any fire pumps that fail to start or malfunction must be repaired or 
replaced to ensure water can be pumped at the required pressure during a fire. 
B. Valve Issues: If any valves are found to be stuck, leaking, or damaged, they should be 
repaired or replaced immediately to prevent water loss and maintain system pressure. 
C. Tank Leaks: A leaking water tank should be repaired to ensure sufficient water is available 
for firefighting. If repairs are not possible, the tank should be replaced. 
Automation Control and Monitoring System: 
A. Malfunctioning Sensors or Control Panels:  Sensors that fail to detect fire or 
environmental changes correctly should be replaced, and malfunctioning control panels 
should be repaired to restore proper operation of the system. 
B. Failed Communications: Communication cables or connections that fail to send signals 
to the control system should be repaired or replaced to maintain system integration. 
C. Control Logic Failures: Any issues with the control logic of the BMS, such as failure to 
respond to alarms or sensor signals, should be addressed to ensure the system operates 
efficiently and accurately.

 Navigation (CNS) facilities 
These guidelines ensure 24/7 operation and reliability of aeronautical navigation facilities at LTIA 
through preventive maintenance, real -time monitoring, and effective failure response, prioritizing 
safety, efficiency, and resilience. 
* Communication and surveillance facilities are maintained by VATM, and any internal VATM 
regulations will take precedence if applicable. 
 
 
 Routine Inspections 
Flight Inspection ensures the accuracy and performance of navigation aids (NAVAIDs), while 
Management Inspection focuses on assessing compliance, documentation, and operational efficiency 
of maintenance activities and overall system reliability. These processes collectively ensure safe and 
efficient air traffic operations in line with ICAO standards (Annex 10) and national regulations. 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 
 


=== PDF PAGE 249 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 248 
Flowchart 
 
Flight inspection involves pre-inspection preparation, system readiness checks, flight execution with 
data recording, and post -inspection actions including result analysis, training, and management to 
ensure navigation facilities' reliability and compliance. 
 
Description 
A. Definition: 
 A flight inspection is conducted by skilled flight inspectors aboard an aircraft to evaluate 
the performance of air navigation facilities. 
B. Purpose: 
 Measure signals from air navigation facilities in the air to verify their performance. 
 Provide baseline data for ground inspections. 
 Determine the facility's performance based on flight inspection standards. 
C. Conducting Authority: 
 The VIAT & ATTECH are responsible for carrying out flight inspections. 
D. Types of Flight Inspection 


=== PDF PAGE 250 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 249 
 
 
E. Flight Inspection Targets and Intervals: 
In Vietnam, flight inspections of air navigation facilities are conducted based on the standards 
recommended by the International Civil Aviation Organization (ICAO) and the internal 
regulations of the Civil Aviation Authority of Vietnam (CAAV). The inspection intervals vary 
depending on the type and operational purpose of the facility. These inspections are conducted 
regularly to ensure the performance of the facilities and to maintain aviation safety. 
 
Key Flight Inspection Intervals 
 For ILS, regular inspections are conducted every 6 months. Additionally, special 
inspections are carried out in cases of facility malfunctions, upgrades, or emergencies. 
 VOR and  DME require regular inspections every 12 months, with additional special 
inspections as needed after maintenance or upgrades. 
 Frequency and Communication Systems  require inspections every 12 months, with 
additional special inspections are conducted in case of malfunctions. 
 PSR/ASR and auxiliary equipment are typically inspected every 12 months to 2 years, 
depending on the facility's importance and operational conditions. 
 ADS-B systems are inspected every 12 months, with additional initial inspections 
conducted after new installations or operational changes. 
 For AGL regular inspections are conducted every 12 months depending on the 
characteristics and usage of the facility. 


=== PDF PAGE 251 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 250 
*  DME, PAPI regular inspection correspond to the related ILS, VOR equipment installed 
together (Clause 3, Article 27, Circular No.53/VBHN -BTVT dated November 3, 2023 
regulating the Management and assurance of flight operations). 
 
The intervals for flight inspections are determined based on the technical characteristics and 
operational significance of each facility, ICAO recommendations, and Vietnam's domestic 
regulations. Facilities with high usage frequency, such as those supporti ng aircraft approach 
and landing, are assigned shorter intervals to enhance reliability and safety. 
The CAAV oversees these regular inspections of air navigation facilities. Inspection results 
are used to renew operational certifications and to develop maintenance and improvement 
plans. This approach ensures the continuous safety and efficiency of aviation operations. 
 
Maintenance of CNS facilities 
The types and components of aeronautical navigation facilities are as follows. 
Communication Facility 
 VHF/UHF Radios: Enables short- and long-range voice communication. 
 VCCS: Integrates multiple communication channels for seamless interaction. 
 AFTN: Transmits flight plans, weather updates, and NOTAMs globally. 
 ATIS: Provides automated weather, runway, and operational updates. 
Navigation Facility 
 ILS: Precision approach and landing guidance. 
 VOR/DME: Navigation and distance measurement. 
 GNSS: Satellite-based global positioning. 
Surveillance Facility 
 PSR: Detects non-transponder aircraft. 
 SSR: Tracks aircraft with transponders. 
 ADS-B: Real-time GPS-based aircraft position. 
 MLAT: Triangulates precise positions of aircraft and vehicles. 
 A-SMGCS: Monitors ground movements and manages airfield lighting.

 Corrective maintenance 
The maintenance manager must report the following situations to main stakeholders according to the 
reporting procedures below and, if necessary, immediately notify and disseminate information to 
related institutions and departments, such as the control tower, using the IOP 
A. When a critical part of the equipment requires replacement. 
B. When a facility malfunction prevents normal operation. 
C. When performance verification is canceled, or the inspection cycle for performance 
verification has been exceeded, necessitating the suspension of facility operations. 
D. When inspections, maintenance, or construction activities result in the suspension of 
facility operations. 
E. When regular inspections require the temporary suspension of facility operations. 
F. When notified by a management inspector or flight inspection crew during an inspection 
or flight test to suspend the operation of the facility. 
G. Any other circumstances causing operational disruptions to the facility. 
 
 
Spare Parts 

=== PDF PAGE 254 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 253 
A. To ensure the efficient operation of navigation facilities, the necessary spare parts must be 
secured. 
B. An appropriate inventory level of spare parts for each facility must be determined, and 
necessary actions, such as procurement requests, must be taken accordingly. 
C. Spare parts should be stored separately in a designated location to ensure timely use, and 
utmost effort must be made to manage them effectively. 
 
Measuring Equipment 
A. Measuring equipment must be secured and maintained to accurately measure the 
performance of navigation facilities. 
B. Measuring equipment must be calibrated and inspected according to the calibration cycles 
recommended by the manufacturer, taking into account factors such as purpose, frequency 
of use, and stability, to maintain its performance and accuracy. 
C. At each primary storage location for measuring equipment, calibration certificates (or 
manufacturer calibration/repair reports) within the valid period, along with user manuals 
and cautionary notes (e.g., manufacturer manuals), must be provided to ensure 
performance reliability. 
 
Performance Inspection 
A. Purpose 
 The performance verification inspection ensures that each navigation facility reliably 
provides information suitable for its intended use. 
B. Responsibility 
 The navigation facility manager must designate a responsible person for performance 
verification inspection for each facility. These designated individuals are required to 
directly carry out the performance verification inspections. 
C. Procedure 
 The performance verification inspection responsible person must: 
 Grant a "pass" rating only to facilities that meet the performance verification criteria upon 
completion of the inspection. 
 Record the completion of the performance verification in the maintenance log.

 Maintenance of AGL facilities 
The Aeronautical Ground Lighting (AGL) system at Long Thanh Airport is designed to ensure the 
safe and efficient operation of runways, taxiways, and approach paths. Effective management and 
regular maintenance are essential for maintaining reliability, extending system life, and complying 
with international standards (e.g., ICAO Annex 14, FAA guidelines). 
 
A. Inset Lights: 
 Types: Unidirectional, Bidirectional, Omnidirectional. 
 Sizes: 8 -inch lights for touchdown zones (TDZ), centerlines, and rapid exit taxiway 
indicator lights (RETIL); 12-inch lights for approach, runway thresholds, and edges. 
 Lifespan: Depends on light source (e.g., LED: up to 100,000 hours). 
B. Elevated Lights: 
 Used for approach, PAPI, and obstacles. 
 Installed with breakable couplings or mounted on base plates. 
C. Control Systems: 
 ALCMS (Airfield Lighting Control and Monitoring System) for centralized monitoring. 
 A-SMGCS (Advanced Surface Movement Guidance Control System) for surface 
guidance. 
 
Personnel Management 
A. Qualified Personnel: Assign skilled electrical technicians and linemen with experience in 
high-voltage and series circuits for maintenance tasks. 
B. Training Programs: Implement training sessions to maintain skills and update staff on 
new technologies. 
C. On-call Availability: Ensure maintenance personnel are on -site or on-call during airport 
operational hours. 
 Routine Inspection 
The primary goal of daily inspections of the Aeronautical Ground Lighting System (AGL) is to ensure 
the safe and reliable operation of lighting systems that guide aircraft during takeoff, landing, and 
taxiing. These inspections help identify and address potential issues before they affect operations.

A. Ensure Aircraft Safety: The lighting system is essential for ensuring safe aircraft takeoff, 
landing, and approach. 
B. Maintain System Functionality: Ensure all lighting components function properly without 
any disruptions or failures. 
C. Regulatory Compliance: Adhere to regulations set by organizations such as ICAO 
(International Civil Aviation Organization) and FAA (Federal Aviation Administration) to 
maintain safety and operational standards. 
Lighting System Check 
A. Runway and Taxiway Lighting: Inspect runway, taxiway, and apron lighting to ensure all 
lights are functioning correctly. This includes checking the brightness and ensuring no 
lights are dimmed or turned off. 
B. Visual Inspection: Check all light segments for any visible issues such as cracks, dirt, or 
obstruction that could impair the light output. 
C. Power Check: Ensure all lights are receiving the required electrical power and there are no 
disruptions in the power supply. 
Power Supply and Protection System Check 
A. Circuit Breakers and Switches : Check the condition of circuit breakers, switches, and 
other power -related equipment to ensure they are in good condition and can be easily 
operated in case of emergencies. 
B. UPS (Uninterruptible Power Supply) : Verify that the UPS system is functioning 
correctly, ensuring that backup power will be available in case of a power failure. 
C. Backup Power : Confirm that backup generators are operational and ready to provide 
power to the lighting system if needed. 
Lighting Equipment and Wiring Check 
A. Connection Check: Inspect all wiring and connections to ensure there are no signs of 
damage, wear, or corrosion that could impact system performance. 
B. Socket and Connector Inspection : Inspect light sockets, connectors, and terminals for 
signs of wear or improper connections. Clean or replace as necessary. 
C. Overheating Check: Look for signs of overheating in lighting components, wiring, and 
connections, which could indicate potential fire hazards. 
Lighting System Control Equipment Check

A. Control Panel Inspection : Check the control panel to verify that all systems are

B. Timer and Switch Test : Test the timers and switches that control the lighting system to

 Preventive Maintenance 
Regular inspections to ensure operational readiness. 
 
A. Scheduled Inspections 
 Description: Regularly inspect key components, including lamps, Constant Current 
Regulators (CCRs), and cables, to ensure their external and operational condition meets 
standards. 
 Key Tasks: 
o Lamps: Verify luminance, color, and damage; clean, remove moisture, or replace 
components as needed. 
o CCRs: Inspect fault detection functions, operational parameters, voltage, and current; 
recalibrate or replace parts if anomalies are detected. 
o Cables: Check for damage, cuts, or corrosion; inspect connections, measure 
insulation resistance, and identify leakage. 
 
B.  Scheduled Replacements 
 Description: Replace lamps based on their operational lifecycle or when luminance drops 
below acceptable levels. 
 Key Tasks: 
o Perform batch replacements or selective replacements based on performance metrics. 
o Maintain sufficient spare parts for prompt replacement operations. 
C. Scheduled Cleaning 
 Description: Prevent luminance degradation by regularly cleaning lamp surfaces. 

=== PDF PAGE 259 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 258 
 Key Tasks: 
o Remove external contaminants like dust and watermarks. 
o Use specialized cleaning agents and tools to avoid lamp damage. 
D. Scheduled Measurements 
 Description: Periodically measure lamp luminance using appropriate tools to ensure 
compliance with standards. 
 Key Tasks: 
o Record measurement results and replace or service lamps if necessary. 
o Utilize photometers or similar equipment for early detection of luminance reduction.

 ICT systems 
General 
These management and maintenance guidelines provide a clear strategic direction for ICT 
maintenance and management at Long Thanh International Airport. By focusing on stability, 
efficiency, reliability, and continuous improvement, the airport will enhance its service quality and 
strengthen its competitive edge. 
 
A. These management and maintenance guidelines to guarantee uninterrupted 24/7 operation 
of all ICT infrastructure (networks, servers, storage, and communication equipment) that 
support the entire airport ecosystem —aircraft operations, passenger services, saf ety and 
security, and logistics. By maintaining system stability, the airport minimizes delays, 
passenger inconvenience, and security incidents. 
 
B. In the event of unforeseen system failures or performance degradation, a prompt response 
and swift return to normal operations is essential. This involves leveraging Preventive and 
Maintenance, real-time monitoring and alert systems . The objective is to restore normal 
operations in the shortest possible time frame. 
 
C. The Management and Maintenance CONOPS for the ICT System will strategically address 
aspects such as routine inspection, preventive maintenance and corrective maintenance for 
each system and Data Center, work management, training and education.  
 
List of Systems and Scope 
The Management and Maintenance CONOPS for the ICT System was prepared only for Long 
Thanh International Airport Phase 1 ICT System and the design details may be changed during the 
construction process, so it should be revised and applied according to the situa tion before 
completion.

 Data Center management 
The Data Centers at Long Thanh International Airport are designed to meet Tier -3 standards, as 
proposed by ADPi, in compliance with the TIA-942B standard. These Data Centers ensure a highly 
reliable and secure environment, offering redundant systems for me chanical cooling, electrical 
power, and network communication connections.  The IT infrastructure is supported by two 
mirrored Data Centers, ensuring full redundancy and operational continuity.  
 
A. Data Center 1: 
 Located in a dedicated facility managed separately from the Passenger Terminal Building 
(PTB). 
 Serves as the primary hub for critical IT operations, with independent cooling and power   
systems. 
B. Data Center 2: 
 Situated within the Passenger Terminal Building (PTB). 
 Functions as a backup and disaster recovery site, mirroring Data Center 1 to provide 
seamless failover capabilities. 
By deploying a twin Data Center strategy, LTIA ensures that its IT systems remain operational even 
in the event of localized failures or maintenance activities, enhancing the reliability and security of 
airport operations. 

=== PDF PAGE 266 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 265

 Routine Inspection 
Routine inspection refers to routine checks conducted at scheduled intervals to assess the condition 
of equipment, systems, and facilities, identifying signs of wear, damage, or abnormalities at an early 
stage. During inspections, technicians or responsible personnel examine components (primarily using 
visual assessments and simple tests) to quickly detect potential issues. 
The objective of routine inspections in this section is to detect minor issues in Long Thanh Airport's 
ICT system early, allowing necessary actions to be taken before they escalate into major failures. 
Typically, inspections do not involve immediate repairs but rather determine whether maintenance or 
repairs are needed, leading to appropriate follow-up actions. 
The inspection frequency is set based on the asset’s criticality, regulatory requirements, and safety 
standards, and can be scheduled on a daily, weekly, or monthly basis. 
 
Characteristics of Routine Inspection 
A. Routine inspection is conducted from a preventive perspective (performed while the 
equipment is in normal working condition). 
B. The primary objective is to detect early signs of abnormalities, wear, or non -compliance 
(diagnostic in nature). 
C. It is performed regularly according to predetermined schedules (daily, weekly, monthly, 
etc.), typically involving visual checks, anomaly measurements, etc. 
D. It mainly focuses on inspection, measurement, and observation, and does not usually 
involve component replacement or repairs (identified issues are addressed through separate 
maintenance actions). 
E. Since it is conducted according to a set plan, it can be scheduled outside of operating hours 
or during low -load periods, making it predictable and manageable with relatively short 
downtime. 
F. Although it requires continuous investment in manpower, time, and material costs, it helps 
reduce long -term costs by preventing major failures or extended downtime while 
contributing to asset lifespan extension.  

=== PDF PAGE 274 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 273 
G. By detecting and managing component wear and performance degradation early, it 
increases Mean Time Between Failures (MTBF) and extends equipment life. 
H. It is systematically planned, recorded, and analyzed using tools such as inspection 
schedules, checklists, and CMMS (Computerized Maintenance Management System). 
 
Routine Inspection Procedures 
A. Inspection Planning and Scheduling 
  Define inspection scope based on asset type, criticality, and failure history. 
 Establish a schedule (daily, weekly, or monthly) and assign personnel. 
 Utilize CMMS to track inspection tasks and logs. 
 Minimize operational disruptions by scheduling during low-traffic periods. 
 
B. Pre-Inspection Preparation 
 Review previous reports and identify recurring issues. 
 Gather tools, checklists, and measurement devices. 
 Ensure safety measures and proper authorization for access. 
 
C.  Inspection Execution 
 Conduct visual inspections to detect wear, damage, or abnormalities. 
 Perform functional tests using diagnostic tools. 
 Record findings in checklists and document with photos if needed. 
 
D. Reporting and Documentation 
 Log findings in CMMS, categorizing them by severity. 
 Generate reports summarizing observations and measurements. 
 Escalate urgent issues to maintenance teams for immediate action. 
 
E. Follow-Up Actions and Maintenance Coordination 
 Review results with stakeholders and schedule maintenance tasks. 
 Update maintenance records and analyze trends for preventive planning. 
 

=== PDF PAGE 275 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 274 
F. Continuous Improvement 
 Regularly assess and optimize inspection methods. 
 Integrate predictive maintenance using data analytics. 
 Provide ongoing training to enhance inspection efficiency.

 Preventive Maintenance 
Preventive maintenance refers to planned maintenance activities conducted in advance to prevent 
failures or malfunctions before they occur. This strategy involves performing maintenance, 
component replacements, cleaning, lubrication, or firmware updates before a failure happens, based 
on time or usage metrics (e.g., operating hours, cycles, mileage), while the equipment is still 
functioning normally. The goal is to reduce unexpected failures, enhance equipment reliability,  and 
extend its lifespan. For example, replacing air filters on a quarterly basis in a facility or replenishing 
lubricants in a machine at regular intervals are typical preventive maintenance tasks. According to 
ICAO, preventive maintenance is defined as " maintenance performed at predetermined intervals or 
according to prescribed criteria to reduce the probability of failure or performance degradation." 
Routine inspections can also be considered a part of preventive maintenance, with the ultimate goal 
being "failure prevention in advance." 
Characteristics of Preventive Maintenance 
A. Preventive maintenance is conducted from a proactive perspective (performed while the 
equipment is in normal working condition). 
B. Its primary objective is to prevent failures in advance and maintain or improve equipment 
performance, reliability, and lifespan. 
C. Maintenance is performed according to a predetermined schedule, either time-based (e.g., 
every six months) or usage-based (e.g., after 1,000 operating hours), while the equipment 
is still operational. 
D. Service tasks are conducted before failures occur, including lubrication, cleaning, 
component replacement, and calibration of measuring instruments. 
E. Since it follows a pre -planned schedule, maintenance can be performed outside of 
operating hours or during low -load periods, making it predictable and manageable with 
relatively short downtime. 

=== PDF PAGE 285 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 284 
F. Although preventive maintenance requires continuous investment in manpower, time, and 
materials, it helps reduce long -term costs by preventing major failures and extended 
downtime, contributing to asset lifespan extension.  
G. By detecting and managing component wear and performance degradation early, it 
increases Mean Time Between Failures (MTBF) and extends equipment life. 
H. It is systematically planned, recorded, and analyzed using tools such as maintenance 
schedules, checklists, and CMMS (Computerized Maintenance Management System). 
 
Preventive Maintenance Procedures 
A. Maintenance Planning and Scheduling 
 Identify critical equipment and establish time -based or usage -based maintenance 
schedules. 
 Utilize CMMS (Computerized Maintenance Management System) for automated 
scheduling and record management. 
 Schedule maintenance during low-traffic periods to minimize operational disruptions. 
 
B. Pre-Maintenance Preparation 
 Review manuals and past maintenance data to prepare necessary tools and spare parts. 
 Ensure maintenance personnel follow safety protocols and obtain required authorizations. 
 
C. Execution of Maintenance Tasks 
 Visual Inspection: Check for wear, corrosion, and abnormalities. 
 Lubrication & Cleaning: Maintain performance through proper lubrication and dust 
removal. 
 Component Replacement & Updates: Replace parts and update firmware/software as 
scheduled. 
 Calibration & Testing: Verify proper functionality through testing procedures. 
 
D.  Documentation and Reporting 
 Record all maintenance activities in CMMS and generate reports. 
 Escalate critical issues to the engineering team for immediate action. 
 

=== PDF PAGE 286 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 285 
E. Follow-Up and Optimization 
 Monitor post-maintenance performance and continuously improve schedules. 
 Utilize data analytics to enhance predictive maintenance and extend equipment lifespan.

 Corrective Maintenance 
Corrective maintenance refers to post -failure (reactive) maintenance carried out to restore 
functionality after a malfunction or defect has occurred. It is defined as "actions performed after a 
failure is detected to restore the equipment to its original functional state." It is also known as "reactive 
maintenance" or "breakdown maintenance." 
In this approach, no preventive actions are taken until an actual issue —such as malfunction, failure, 
power outage, or leakage—occurs. Once the problem arises, repairs or component replacements are 
performed to return the equipment to normal operation. 
Corrective maintenance is often unplanned and occurs in unexpected situations, which can lead to 
prolonged operational downtime. Therefore, strict adherence to r outine inspections and preventive 
maintenance is essential, and maintenance personnel must be properly trained to conduct immediate 
recovery actions when inevitable failures occur. 
 
Characteristics of Corrective Maintenance 
A. Corrective maintenance is conducted from a reactive perspective (performed after a failure 
or issue has occurred). 
B. Its primary objective is to quickly restore normal functionality after a failure and prevent 
further damage. 
C. It is performed only when a failure occurs—it has no fixed schedule and is unpredictable. 
D. Involves actual repairs, component replacements, emergency recovery, and root cause 
elimination. 

=== PDF PAGE 296 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 295 
E. Since failures occur unpredictably, sudden equipment shutdowns can halt operations, 
leading to significant time, cost, and productivity losses. 
F. As costs are incurred only when a failure happens, it may appear to be a low-cost approach 
in the short term. However, if a severe failure occurs, sudden increases in repair expenses 
(e.g., overtime, emergency parts) and production losses can drastically raise total costs. 
G. Relying solely on corrective maintenance can lead to frequent failures, ultimately 
shortening equipment lifespan and increasing the risk of unexpected severe damage. 
H. Since corrective maintenance is reactive, immediate response capability (e.g., spare parts 
availability and rapid deployment systems) is more critical than planning. 
 
Corrective Maintenance Procedure 
A. Failure Detection and Reporting 
 Failure Detection Methods 
o Monitoring system (automatic alerts) 
o On-site operations staff (immediate reporting to the operations center) 
o Users (internal staff or external customers) reporting issues 
 Failure Reporting System 
o Notification via emergency hotline (phone, radio), email, or ticket system 
o Clearly specify the failure type, time, location, and impact when reporting 
 
B. Root Cause Analysis and Diagnosis 
 Log Analysis and Equipment Inspection 
o Quickly identify the failure point through system logs, event records, and network 
diagnostics 
 Collaboration with Experts/Suppliers 
o  Immediate escalation to vendor technical support in case of critical failures 
o Reference maintenance manuals and past similar failure records 
 
C. Recovery and System Normalization 
 Recovery Process 

=== PDF PAGE 297 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 296 
o Equipment replacement (spare parts), software patching/rebooting, network 
rerouting, etc. 
o Verification of normal operation after recovery (testing procedures) 
 Operational Notification 
o Notify the operations department upon completion of normalization 
o Provide public announcements or notifications to passengers/staff if necessary 
 
D. Follow-up Actions and Preventive Measures 
 Root Cause Analysis 
o Identify the fundamental cause of the failure and establish preventive measures 
 Failure Report Preparation 
o Document failure details, corrective actions taken, and areas for improvement 
 Process Improvement 
o Update maintenance manuals, conduct staff retraining, and modify system 
configurations as needed 
 
Spare Parts Management 
A. Spare Parts Inventory and Procurement Strategy 
 Failure Detection Methods 
 Establish appropriate stock levels (minimum and maximum quantities) 
 Stock replenishment policy: Immediate reordering after use, periodic inspections 
 Shelf life/aging management: First-In-First-Out (FIFO) method for consumables such as 
batteries 
 Vendor SLA agreements for emergency procurement (ensuring expedited delivery) 
 Explore shared inventory agreements with nearby airports or partner organizations for 
high-cost parts 
 
B. Replacement Procedures 
 Remove faulty parts and install spare parts after taking necessary safety precautions 
 Conduct testing and confirm normal operation  

=== PDF PAGE 298 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 297 
 Record spare parts usage (date of use, part number, etc.), and send defective parts for 
repair (AS) or disposal 
 Stock replenishment: If spare parts inventory is low, immediately place an order or 
retrieve additional stock from nearby storage facilities 
 
 Failure Reporting System 
A. Failure Reporting Procedure 
 Failure detection through operational staff, monitoring systems, or user reports → Failure 
registration 
 Record basic information such as failure type, occurrence time, impact scope, and 
responsible personnel 
 For critical failures (Level A), immediate verbal reporting is required, followed by formal 
documentation 
B. Responsible Departments and Personnel 
 Pre-assign responsible departments based on incident type (Network Team, Server Team, 
Facilities Team, etc.) 
 Introduce the failure Owner concept: A designated individual tracks and oversees the 
resolution process until completion 
 For Level A failures, senior managers or department heads take command, oversee 
situation dissemination, and allocate resources 
 
C. Failure Logging and Record Management 
 Register all incidents in the ticket system or incident logbook 
 Collect and store supporting data such as system logs, event logs, and network 
switch/router logs 
 Regularly review failure history and analyze recurrence frequency 
 
D.  Feedback Process for Recurring failures 
 Conduct Post-Mortem Review Meetings after failure resolution 
 Perform Root Cause Analysis (RCA), evaluate response actions, and derive improvement 
measures 

=== PDF PAGE 299 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 298 
 Enhance education and processes: Update maintenance manuals and provide retraining 
for staff 
 Store all improvements in the Knowledge Base for future reference and training 
 
Failure Severity Levels 
A. Assign severity levels in accordance with the failure severity levels specified in the list. 
B. In cases where it is difficult to apply the specified list 
 Level A:  Service or system downtime affecting the majority of the system or key 
buildings (80% or more). 
 Level B: Service or system downtime affecting a significant portion of the system or key 
buildings (30% or more). 
 Level C:  Service or system downtime affecting a partial portion of the system or key 
buildings (10% or more). 
 Level D:  Service or system downtime affecting a small portion of the system or key 
buildings (less than 10%).

3.7 Technical Equipment system  
The Technical Equipment System at Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA) consists of critical 
systems that support various operational functions and ensure the airport runs efficiently and safely. 
This system includes equipment used for technical services  such as baggage handling, security 
systems, elevator-escalator-moving walks, aircraft stand equipment system, aircraft fuel and GSE 
fuel filing, sewage treatment system, BMS systems and other specialized airport technology. 
Each system is designed to meet high-performance standards and is essential for maintaining seamless 
airport operations, passenger safety, and operational efficiency. Routine maintenance, monitoring, 
and updates to these systems are necessary to ensure they function at optimal levels, comply with 
regulatory requirements, and minimize downtime. 
 


=== PDF PAGE 309 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 308 
In order to ensure the optimal performance and longevity of the equipment systems at Long Thanh 
International Airport (LTIA), a structured approach to maintenance is essential. This approach 
involves three key types of activities: Inspection, Preventive Maintenance , and Corrective 
Maintenance. Each of these activities plays a vital role in the continuous operation of airport systems, 
ensuring safety, efficiency, and compliance with operational standards. 
 
Inspection 
Inspection refers to the systematic evaluation of equipment and systems to assess their condition 
and compliance with safety and operational standards. Inspections are typically conducted by 
external experts or third-party inspectors and are often required to meet legal or regulatory 
standards. This process focuses on identifying any potential issues, ensuring that equipment is 
operating within its prescribed parameters, and ensuring compliance with local regulations. 
A. Purpose: To evaluate the condition of equipment and verify that it is functioning within 
the required safety and regulatory standards. 
B. Key Activities:  
 Testing operational performance to ensure compliance with established standards. 
 Reviewing documentation and operational logs to verify that equipment is being 
maintained properly. 
 Assessing compliance with legal and environmental regulations. 
 
Flowchart 
The workflow is structured as follows: 
 


=== PDF PAGE 310 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 309 
 
A. Preparation Phase 
 Focus: Planning and readiness for emergency maintenance to ensure swift response and 
minimal downtime. 
 Roles: 
o System Operator : Monitor system data for anomalies, maintain an emergency 
contact list, and verify the readiness of emergency shutdown procedures. 
o Maintenance Personnel: Ensure the availability of critical spare parts, tools, and 
resources required for emergency repairs. 
o Contractors: Coordinate with the internal team to understand emergency protocols, 
prepare specialized equipment, and confirm availability for immediate mobilization. 
B. Execution Phase 
 Focus: Immediate implementation of emergency maintenance tasks to restore operational 
conditions. 
 Roles: 
o System Operator: Quickly isolate the affected area, provide access to the system, 
and support real-time troubleshooting. 
o Maintenance Personnel:  Identify and repair faulty components, such as 
compressors, fans, or pumps, and implement containment measures for leaks or 
failures. 
o Contractors: Perform complex repairs, ensure compliance with safety standards, 
and collaborate with the in-house team for seamless execution. 
C. Reporting Phase 
 Focus: Documenting and communicating the details of the emergency incident and 
actions taken. 
 Roles: 
o System Operator: Provide a log of system performance during the emergency and 
document deviations from normal operations. 
o Maintenance Personnel: Record the repairs and replacements conducted, including 
the downtime and resources utilized. 

=== PDF PAGE 311 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 310 
o Contractors: Submit a detailed report of specialized tasks performed, including root 
cause analysis and future prevention recommendations. 
D. Follow-Up and Continuous Improvement Phase 
 Focus: Reviewing the emergency response, addressing process gaps, and enhancing 
system resilience. 
 Roles: 
o System Operators: Analyze system data trends post -repair and update operational 
protocols to prevent similar incidents. 
o Maintenance Personnel:  Evaluate the response efficiency, refine emergency 
maintenance procedures, and plan for periodic drills. 
o Contractors: Provide insights on challenges faced during emergency tasks and 
propose long-term solutions for system robustness. 
 
Preventive Maintenance 
Preventive Maintenance  is the proactive maintenance of equipment designed to prevent 
malfunctions and extend the service life of assets. It includes regular checks, lubrication, cleaning, 
part replacements, and other maintenance tasks that aim to keep equipment running efficiently 
and avoid unexpected failures. This is typically carried out by in-house maintenance teams as part 
of a scheduled, ongoing effort to ensure the system remains in peak working condition. 
A. Purpose:  
To prevent unexpected equipment failures, reduce downtime, and extend the lifespan of 
systems by performing scheduled maintenance tasks. 
B. Key Activities:  
 Regular cleaning and lubrication of equipment. 
 Replacing worn-out or outdated parts before they fail. 
 Routine calibration and adjustments to keep systems operating efficiently. 
 Checking system performance and addressing minor issues before they become major 
problems. 
 
Flowchart 
The workflow is structured as follows:

Corrective Maintenance 
Corrective Maintenance  is performed after a failure or breakdown has occurred. This type of 
maintenance addresses issues that were not caught during the inspection or preventive maintenance 
stages, often requiring repairs or replacements of faulty components. Corrective Maintenance is 
typically carried out by in-house technicians or external contractors depending on the complexity 
of the issue. 
A. Purpose: To restore the equipment to normal operating conditions after a failure, ensuring 
that operations can continue without significant delays. 
B. Key Activities:  

=== PDF PAGE 314 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 313 
 Diagnosing and identifying the root cause of the failure. 
 Repairing or replacing malfunctioning parts or systems. 
 Testing and verifying equipment after repairs to ensure it is fully operational. 
 Updating maintenance logs and reporting the incident for future analysis and prevention.

 Baggage Handling System 
This section provides guidelines for the operation, inspection, maintenance, and emergency . This 
section outlines the maintenance and operational protocols for the Baggage Handling System (BHS) 
at Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA). The guidelines emphasize compliance with international 
standards, routine and corrective maintenance, emergen cy management, and the application of 
Preventive maintenance to ensure seamless baggage handling operations.

Routine inspections are conducted to ensure the operational readiness and safety of the BHS. These

 Security systems 
The security system at Long Thanh Airport prioritizes the safety of passengers and staff while 
protecting critical facilities and assets within the airport. It ensures operational continuity and 
compliance with international aviation security regulations and legal requirements. 
 
General 
These management and maintenance guidelines provide a clear strategic direction for Security 
system maintenance and management at LTIA. The focus will be on stability, efficiency, reliability, 
and continuous improvement to improve service quality and increase competitiveness. 
A. These management and maintenance guidelines ensure 24/7 uninterrupted operation of all 
security systems that support full airport operations. By maintaining system stability, the 
airport minimizes unlawful Interference, passenger inconvenience, and aircraft delays. 
B. Since unexpected system failures or performance degradation can disrupt the airport's 
complete security operations, this section aims to prevent security system failures and 
return to normal operations with rapid response in the event of a failure. 
C. The Management and Maintenance CONOPS for the Security facility will strategically 
address aspects such as operational status management, backup management, failure 
management, preventive maintenance for each system. 
 
Systems and Scope 
  The Management and Maintenance CONOPS for the ICT System was prepared only for  LTIA 
Phase 1 Security System and the design details may be changed during the construction process, so 
it should be revised and applied according to the situation before completion. 
A. Security Screening Equipment (SSE) 
B. Closed Circuit Television (CCTV) System 
C. Security Access Control System (SACS) 
Regulation and Policy 
The maintenance of security facilities is strictly conducted in accordance with “Circular 
No.13/2019/TT-BGTVT”. All security equipment and facilities must undergo regular inspections 
and maintenance as per manufacturers' guidelines, while complying with legal requirements for 

=== PDF PAGE 320 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 319 
data storage, application of advanced technologies, and proper record-keeping and reporting. These 
measures are essential to ensure the continuous safety and operational efficiency of the airport. 
 
 Routine Inspection 
Routine inspections are critical for ensuring that security systems at Long Thanh International 
Airport (LTIA) function optimally and maintain high operational standards. These inspections are 
typically performed at regular intervals as part of the preventive maintenance schedule. They allow 
for the early identification of potential issues before they escalate into failures, helping to ensure 
continuous airport security and minimize downtime. 
A. Security Screening Equipment (SSE) 
 Daily Inspections: 
o Operational Test: Use the manufacturer’s Operational Test Kit (OTK) to verify that 
each piece of screening equipment is functioning correctly at the start of each 
operational day. 
o Visual Check: Check the exterior of all screening equipment (X-ray machines, metal 
detectors, body scanners, etc.) for any visible damage or issues that may affect 
performance. 
 Weekly Inspections: 
o Calibration and Sensitivity Check : Check the calibration of machines like X -ray 
machines and metal detectors to ensure they are detecting prohibited items effectively. 
o Verification of Spares: Ensure that essential spare parts are stocked and ready for 
immediate use in case of equipment failure. 
 Monthly/Quarterly Inspections: 
o Advanced Functional Tests: Conduct a more detailed inspection of the functionality 
of each screening device, including sensitivity control for detection accuracy. 
o Cleanliness and Safety Check: Ensure that all machines are kept clean and free from 
debris that may obstruct performance or cause mechanical issues. 
 Semi-Annual/Annual Inspections: 
o Critical Component Check : Inspect all critical components of the equipment that 
may affect long-term stability (e.g., X-ray tubes, sensors) to ensure they are operating 
within acceptable standards. 

=== PDF PAGE 321 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 320 
o Performance Review : Perform an in -depth review of the equipment's long -term 
performance, including identifying any parts that may require replacement due to 
wear and tear. 
B. Closed Circuit Television (CCTV) System:  
Routine inspections of the CCTV system ensure that all cameras and monitoring equipment 
are functioning properly, providing continuous surveillance of the airport. 
 Daily Inspections: 
o Camera Status Check: Verify that all fixed and PTZ (Pan -Tilt-Zoom) cameras are 
operational, with no noticeable gaps in the coverage area. 
o Recording Check: Ensure that video feeds are being recorded properly, and that the 
storage systems are functioning as expected. 
o Visual Check: Inspect cameras for cleanliness and remove any obstructions that may 
impair their field of view. 
 Weekly Inspections: 
o Network Health Check : Verify that the CCTV network, including servers and 
switches, is functioning without any performance degradation. 
o Review Video Footage : Perform random reviews of recorded video to ensure that 
video quality is sufficient and there are no technical glitches. 
 Monthly/Quarterly Inspections: 
o Inspection of Server Rooms : Check the condition of servers and ensure that they 
are functioning within normal operating parameters. 
o System Redundancy Check: Verify the functionality of failover systems to ensure 
uninterrupted operation in case of primary system failure. 
 Semi-Annual/Annual Inspections: 
o Full System Test: Conduct a thorough inspection of the entire CCTV infrastructure, 
including all cameras, recording systems, and network components. Ensure that all 
systems are fully integrated and operational. 
o Environmental Check: Assess environmental factors (e.g., server room temperature, 
humidity) that could affect system performance, making adjustments as needed. 
C. Security Access Control System (SACS):

ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 321 
Routine inspections for the Access Control System ensure that all components involved in 
regulating and monitoring access to restricted airport areas are functioning properly. 
 Daily Inspections: 
o Card Reader Status Check: Verify that card readers, biometric scanners, and PIN-
based systems are functioning correctly. 
o Access Log Review : Ensure that access logs are being recorded properly and that 
there are no unauthorized access attempts. 
o Visual Inspection : Check all access points for any visible issues or damage to 
hardware such as readers or control units. 
 Weekly Inspections: 
o Verification of Communication Between Components : Ensure that all 
components (e.g., card readers, control units) are communicating properly with the 
central access control system. 
o System Backup Check : Verify that the backup power systems (e.g., UPS) are 
operational to prevent disruptions in case of power failure. 
 Monthly/Quarterly Inspections: 
o Database and Software Check: Ensure that the access control database is up to date, 
and review software versions for any needed updates. 
o Battery Health Check: Inspect the health of battery systems that power control units 
and card readers, ensuring they are in good condition and capable of providing 
backup power if needed. 
 Semi-Annual/Annual Inspections: 
o In-Depth Functional Testing : Perform more comprehensive tests on the entire 
system, including the integration between card readers, biometric systems, and 
control units. 
o Physical Security Check: Ensure that all physical security measures are in place and 
that there are no vulnerabilities in the access control system.

 Preventive Maintenance 
Preventive maintenance is a proactive approach to ensuring that the security systems at Long Thanh 
International Airport (LTIA) remain operational and reliable. The goal is to prevent failures before 
they occur, minimizing downtime and ensuring that security operations are not interrupted. 
A. Security Screening Equipment (SSE ): Preventive maintenance for SSE involves routine 
checks and maintenance of the various security screening technologies. This includes: 
 Scheduled Inspections: Regular checks on X-ray machines, explosive detection systems, 
and other screening equipment to ensure they are functioning correctly. 
 Operational Testing: Daily operational checks using the Operational Test Kit (OTK) 
provided by the manufacturer to verify proper functionality. 
 Calibration and Sensitivity Control: Ensuring that equipment like X-ray machines and 
metal detectors are calibrated for maximum effectiveness in detecting threats. 
 Spare Parts Management: Maintaining a stock of spare parts based on usage data and 
ensuring that replacement parts are available when needed. Spare parts are secured in a 
safe and organized manner to ensure quick access during an emergency. 
B. Closed Circuit Television (CCTV) System : Preventive maintenance for CCTV systems 
includes: 
 Scheduled Inspections: Regular checks on cameras, servers, and network equipment to 
ensure the CCTV system is functioning properly. 
 System Health Monitoring : Continuous monitoring of the CCTV system through the 
Video Management System (VMS) to identify potential issues before they affect 
operations. 
 Spare Parts Management : Regular stock management of essential components like 
cameras and servers to ensure replacements are readily available. 
 Testing Backup and Redundancy Systems : Ensuring the redundancy systems (e.g., 
failover mechanisms) are operational to guarantee continuous surveillance. 
C. Security Access Control System (SACS) 
 Routine Inspections: Regular checks on card readers, biometric systems, and control 
units to ensure proper functioning. 
 Software and Firmware Updates: Regular updates to system software and firmware to 
maintain security standards. 

=== PDF PAGE 324 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 323 
 Battery and Power Backup Checks: Ensuring that power backups, such as UPS systems, 
are functioning to prevent any interruption in access control due to power failures. 
 
 
 Corrective Maintenance 
Corrective maintenance is performed when a system failure or malfunction occurs. The goal is to 
restore the system to normal operation as quickly as possible to minimize disruptions and maintain 
security. 
A. Security Screening Equipment (SSE) 
 Failure Response Procedures: In the event of an equipment malfunction, the following 
actions are taken: 
o Immediate Notification : The failure is reported to the Aviation Security Center 
(ASC) for further investigation. 
o Equipment Repair : The maintenance team is dispatched to repair the 
malfunctioning equipment based on the severity of the failure. 
o Priority Handling : Critical failures (e.g., multiple X -ray failures) are addressed 
immediately to prevent a significant impact on operations. 
 SSE Failure Classification Criteria 
Security screening equipment operates independently due to its operational 
characteristics, making widespread simultaneous malfunctions rare. While such issues 
could potentially arise from power supply problems, all equipment is connected to an 
Uninterruptible Power Supply (UPS), which minimizes the risk of disruptions. 
Description Failure Severity Remark 
Multiple X-Ray Failure A or B  
Single X-Ray Failure C  
Single Equipment Failure (not X-Ray) D Use other Lanes 
equipment 
 
o Failure Severity Levels 
o Grade A:  Failure affecting overall security screening operations (more than half of 
the total screening lanes). 

=== PDF PAGE 325 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 324 
o Grade B:  Failure affecting only part of security screening operations (more than two 
lanes, less than half of the total screening lanes). 
o Grade C:  Failure affecting only part of security screening operations (one lane) 
o Grade D:  Failure affecting only part of security screening operations (less than one 
lane) 
 Failure response procedures 
o Immediately report the following to ASC: Time of occurrence, fault equipment list, 
Impact 
o A, B grades:  Place the technical team on site immediately to determine the cause. 
With the collaboration of APOC, operate emergency response procedures. 
o C, D, Grade: Place maintenance team on site for equipment repair. 
B. Closed Circuit Television (CCTV) System 
 Failure Response Procedures : When a CCTV system failure occurs, it is handled as 
follows: 
o Immediate Diagnosis: The failure is immediately reported to the CCTV operator 
who will assess the situation and determine the severity. 
o System Restoration: A maintenance team is sent to repair the faulty components, 
such as cameras, servers, or switches. 
o Network Restoration: In cases of network malfunctions, the technical team works 
to restore CCTV coverage by either repairing the faulty switches or rerouting the 
network. 
 Failure management 
In the event of a CCTV system problem, security monitoring at the terminal may be 
interrupted, so a quick and effective response procedure to maintain airport security 
level is essential. The objectives are as follows: 
 Quick normalization of CCTV system 
 Secure Airport Security with Non-Disruptive Security Monitoring 
 
 CCTV System Failure Classification Criteria 
Because the CCTV system consists of a multi -layered network with concentrated 
switches, network problems can cause failure in a wide area. In the event of a failure in

in a CCTV system, routine inspections of servers and network equipment, the

of utmost importance.

 Failure management 
In the event of an ACS failure, a prompt and effective response procedure is crucial to 
maintaining the required level of airport security. The objectives are as follows: 
o Quick normalization of ACS system 
o Secure Airport Security with Non-Disruptive Security Access Control 
 ACS System Failure Classification Criteria 
The ACS system controls access through CUs installed in each area and is capable of 
standalone operation even in the event of server or network failures. Additionally, it is 
powered by a UPS to safeguard against power outages, making widespread disruptions 
unlikely. However, since a single CU controls multiple access points simultaneously, 
security control issues may arise in certain areas. Therefore, in the ACS system, regular 
inspections of CUs, availability of spare parts, and the establishment of emerge ncy 
recovery procedures are essential.

o A, B grades:  This should be a UPS power issue, please request the electrical team to

 Spares management 
Having a spare is an important factor in operation. It is almost impossible to repair 
products such as servers. CU and Card Reader on their own by maintenance team, so it 
is best to have a certain amount of spare at all times and replace them immediately in 
the event of a failure. 
If the company that can supply parts exclusively, it is recommended that a system be 
established that can be procured at all times by signing an annual procurement contract. 
Alternatively, regular maintenance contracts are effective on some specific systems. 
When calculating the number of spare parts, it is important to grade them according to 
the number of installations and ensure a certain percentage, taking into account the 
importance of the parts and the status of replacements based on actual operational data. 
In addition to securing the spare quantity, it is important to keep the items safe and 
manage them to be used immediately in case of an emergency.

monitoring, historical performance data, and advanced diagnostics to identify potential failures before 
they occur. This proactive approach helps minimize downtime, reduce repa ir costs, and improve 
passenger safety by addressing performance anomalies before they escalate into major malfunctions. 
Preventive maintenance strategies use sensor -based monitoring, software -driven diagnostics, and 
trend analysis to optimize inspection schedules, component replacements, and energy efficiency.

B. Handrail & Safety System: Repairing desynchronized or tension-lost handrails that may 
cause passenger accidents. 
C. Braking System : Replacing worn brake pads and adjusting automatic stopping 
mechanisms to ensure safe deceleration. 
D. Sensors & Monitoring: Resetting emergency stop sensors, recalibrating motion detectors, 
and repairing load balancing systems. 
E. Electrical & Power Supply: Resolving electrical malfunctions, inspecting control panels 
for overheating, and ensuring power supply consistency.

 Aircraft stand equipment system 
This section covers the inspection, maintenance, and emergency response procedures for the aircraft-
stand equipment at Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)—namely the VDGS, PBB, PCA, GPU, 
and IASS. These assets enable precise aircraft docking, passenger  boarding, and the safe, 
uninterrupted supply of power, air, and data at each stand. A structured maintenance regime 
maximizes availability, prevents operational delays, and supports overall airside safety. 
 
 Routine Inspection 
Routine inspections are vital to ensure the operational efficiency and safety of the aircraft stand 
equipment system at Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA). These inspections help to identify 
potential issues early, prevent failures, and maintain compliance with performance and safety  
standards. By conducting regular inspections, the airport can detect problems before they cause 
system failures or result in costly repairs. 
Description 
Routine inspections of the aircraft stand equipment systems are carried out regularly across various 
components, such as VDGS (Visual Docking Guidance System), PBB (Passenger Boarding Bridge), 
PCA (Pre -Conditioned Air), GPU (Ground Power Unit), and IASS (I ntegrated Aircraft Stand 
System). These inspections are essential to ensure that all equipment functions properly and that any 
potential issues are identified and resolved before they disrupt airport operations. Proactive checks 
contribute to a safer, more  efficient environment by reducing system downtime and maintaining 
seamless operation across the airport’s aircraft stand systems.

 Preventive Maintenance 
Preventive maintenance utilizes data collected from the system, including sensor readings, historical 
performance data, and system trends, to predict and address potential failures before they occur. By 
continuously monitoring the system's condition, maintenance activities can be scheduled in advance, 
allowing for timely interventions to prevent disruptions. This proactive approach reduces unexpected 
downtime, improves system reliability, and extends the lifespan of critical components by ensuring 
maintenance is performed only when required, rather than relying on reactive repairs. 
 
Description

Preventive maintenance involves monitoring key metrics and system behaviors over time to identify 
early signs of wear or inefficiencies. By analyzing trends and comparing them to historical 
performance data, potential failures can be forecasted and address ed proactively. This data -driven 
strategy helps to prevent unplanned outages, optimize system operations, and reduce the frequency 
and cost of repairs. The goal is to maintain a high level of operational efficiency while minimizing 
the risk of system breakdowns, ensuring long-term reliability of the infrastructure.
