 Corrective Maintenance 
Corrective maintenance is triggered by unexpected malfunctions. The objective is rapid root -cause 
analysis, repair or replacement, and restoration of full functionality with minimal stand downtime. 
 
Description 
Corrective maintenance is initiated in response to unexpected failures or malfunctions in plumbing 
systems. When components fail or deviate from normal operating conditions, corrective maintenance 
ensures that the system is quickly restored to full functionality. The focus of corrective maintenance 
is to repair or replace defective parts, minimize system downtime, and avoid the escalation of issues. 
By addressing malfunctions swiftly, corrective maintenance helps maintain operational continuity 
and improves the longevity of plumbing infrastructure, ensuring reliable performance over time.

 Aircraft fuel filling 
This section provides guidelines for the operation, inspection, maintenance, and emergency 
management of the refueling facility at Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA). The facility plays 
a critical role in ensuring uninterrupted fuel supply to support aviation operations. 
Compliance with Standards:  
A. EI/JIG Standards: 
 EI 1581: Ensures the performance and reliability of filter separators in removing water 
and particulate contaminants from aviation fuel. 
 JIG 2: Governs the operational safety and maintenance of hydrant systems and refueling 
operations. 
 JIG 4: Provides guidelines for quality assurance and testing of aviation fuel during 
storage and handling. 
B. API Standards:  
 API 610: Applies to centrifugal pumps, ensuring reliability and safety in handling Jet A-
1 fuel under high pressure. 
 API 650: Ensures the structural integrity and safety of fuel storage tanks, focusing on 
durability and operational efficiency. 
C. ISO Standards:  
 ISO 1825: Specifies the quality and durability of refueling hoses, ensuring resistance to 
high pressure and fuel compatibility. 
 ISO 15589-1: Governs cathodic protection systems to prevent corrosion in buried and 
submerged structures. 
D. Manufacturer Guidelines:  
 Follow specific maintenance and operational practices recommended by the equipment 
manufacturers, including pump drivers, valves, and monitoring devices, to optimize 
performance and longevity. 
 
 Routine Inspection 
Routine inspection focuses on regularly scheduled checks to ensure the proper functioning of 
equipment and systems. This proactive approach helps identify potential issues early, ensuring 
operational continuity and reducing the likelihood of unexpected failures. Routine inspections form

B. Hydrant Valves: Disassemble and inspect internal components for cracks or misalignment.

C. Filter Separators: Replace clogged filters and verify flow rate restoration using real-time 
monitoring systems. 
 
Transfer & Recovery Facility 
A. Tank Shift Pumps : Replace faulty motor components and test flow restoration using 
performance benchmarks. 
B. Drain Recovery Tanks: Apply temporary patches for structural damage until permanent 
repairs can be made. Verify drainage system functionality post-repair. 
C. Filter Separators: Conduct a complete disassembly to identify and remove contaminants. 
Replace separators as necessary. 
 
Auxiliary Facility 
A. Hydrant Valve Test Facility : Test all operational components after repairs. Replace 
damaged loading arms or couplers immediately.

 Sewage Treatment Plant (STP) 
The Sewage Treatment Plant (STP) at Long Thanh International Airport requires a structured and 
proactive maintenance strategy to ensure continuous and reliable operation of its various treatment 
systems. This section outlines routine inspection tasks, prev entive maintenance schedules, and 
corrective actions categorized by the main functional systems of the STP. Each task is tailored to the 
specific design features and technologies adopted at the facility. 
 
 Routine Inspection 
Primary Treatment System: 
Facility Inspection Items Frequency 
Screening Units Bar spacing check, debris removal, motor & rake function test. Daily 
Grit Removal Units Grit buildup observation, aeration diffuser check, grit washing. Daily / Weekly 
A. Screening Units:  
The coarse and fine mechanical screens must be inspected daily to ensure proper removal of 
large and small debris. Operators should check the bar spacing (coarse: ~20 mm, fine: 0.75 
mm), confirm automatic rakes are functioning, and monitor for any unusual motor loads or 
jamming. Regular inspection helps prevent downstream clogging and mechanical failures. 
B. Grit Removal Units:  
Inspect the aerated grit tank daily for excessive sand, silt, or gravel buildup. The aeration 
system at the tank's base should be checked for even bubble distribution to aid in the separation 
of organics from inorganics. The grit washer should be inspected  weekly to ensure it is 
effectively removing organic residue from collected grit, minimizing odor and improving 
disposal quality. 
 
Secondary Treatment System: 
Facility Inspection Items Frequency 
Biological 
Reactors 
MLSS measurement, airflow rate, foam/scum presence, 
internal mixing Daily 
Biofilm Carriers Media integrity, clogging, biofilm buildup pattern Weekly 
Anoxic and 
Aerobic Zones 
DO level (aerobic), nitrate level (anoxic), internal 
recirculation Daily 
A. Biological Reactors:  

=== PDF PAGE 364 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 363 
Operators should check each bioreactor daily for consistent biological activity. This includes 
measuring MLSS concentration (target: ~2,000 mg/L), ensuring proper aeration in aerobic 
chambers, observing scum or foam accumulation, and verifying mixing effic iency. These 
checks are critical to maintaining balanced biological treatment and avoiding process 
imbalances. 
B. Biofilm Carriers:  
The fixed media in aerobic zones must be inspected weekly for signs of physical damage, 
uneven microbial growth, or clogging. Ensuring open flow paths and healthy biofilm growth 
(target thickness: 20 –30 mm) helps maintain high pollutant removal efficiency and reduces 
energy consumption. 
C. Anoxic and Aerobic Zones:  
Daily monitoring includes measuring dissolved oxygen (DO) levels in aerobic zones (target 
range: 2–4 mg/L) and nitrate concentration in anoxic zones to confirm proper nitrification -
denitrification cycles. Recirculation pumps and control valves should be ch ecked for stable 
flow to support efficient nutrient removal. 
 
Tertiary Treatment System: 
Facility Inspection Items Frequency 
Disinfection 
System

operation, leaks Daily

Contact Tank 
Retention time, zigzag flow path blockage, biofilm/algae

Fine Filtration 
Units 
Filter mesh condition, backwash cycle status, pressure 
differential Daily 
A. Disinfection System:  
Operators must check the sodium hypochlorite dosing system daily to ensure accurate 
chemical injection. Residual chlorine levels should be measured at the outlet of the contact 
tank to confirm compliance with QCVN 40:2011/BTNMT Column A standards. Pump 
operation, dosing accuracy, and potential leaks must also be monitored closely. 
B. Chlorine Contact Tank:  

=== PDF PAGE 365 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 364 
Weekly inspection involves ensuring that the zigzag flow channels are clear of blockages and 
biofilm growth. Verifying the proper retention time (approximately 30 minutes) is crucial for 
effective disinfection. Algae buildup on the tank walls should be removed as necessary. 
C. Fine Filtration Units:  
Daily checks include observing the automatic backwash cycle, ensuring that differential 
pressure across the filter does not exceed design limits, and confirming that the 50 -micron 
mesh remains intact. These filters are critical to maintaining final effluen t clarity and 
protecting downstream reuse applications.

A. Aerated Sludge Storage Tank:  
Operators should inspect the aeration system daily to ensure continuous sludge mixing and 
prevent sedimentation or anaerobic conditions. Sludge level and any scum or odor issues 
should be logged and addressed immediately. 
B. Belt Press Dewatering Units:  
Dewatering performance should be monitored daily by checking sludge dryness, belt 
alignment, and polymer feed effectiveness. Any leaks or buildup of solids around the 
equipment must be cleaned to avoid system malfunction. 
C. Polymer Dosing System:  
Every 2 to 3 days, staff should verify polymer preparation consistency, chemical reservoir 
levels, and the functionality of dosing pumps to ensure optimal flocculation during dewatering.

A. PLC & SCADA System:  
Every day, operators must confirm the SCADA interface is functioning correctly, alarm

B. Sensors (Flow, Level, DO, etc.):

includes checking cable connections and ensuring no buildup or fouling affects sensor 
readings. 
C. Data Loggers:

backed up properly. Synchronization with SCADA is crucial for consistent reporting and 
traceability. 
D. Remote Access System 
IT staff should verify weekly that remote access functions are secure and responsive, and that 
authorized personnel can monitor and operate the system off-site without interruptions.

A. Aerated Sludge Storage Tank:

D. Sludge Transport 
Before each off-site sludge transport, the containers should be checked for structural integrity 
and seal condition. Loading systems should be tested for safe operation, and any deterioration 
in containment should be repaired or replaced immediately to prevent environmental release.

 BMS system 
The Building Management System (BMS) will be implemented to monitor and/or control the HVAC 
(Heating, Ventilation, and Air Conditioning), fire safety, and plumbing systems in the Long Thanh 
International Airport Passenger Terminal Building (PTB). The desig n work of the BMS system is 
divided into two main packages: High -Level BMS (HBMS) and Low -Level BMS (LBMS), each 
corresponding to the work of ELV (Extra Low Voltage) consultants and MEP (Mechanical, Electrical, 
Plumbing) consultants. This document covers t he overall BMS system, encompassing both HBMS 
and LBMS as outlined in the provided materials.  
A. Main Features of the BMS System: 
 Monitoring and Display of All Connected Equipment 
 Facility Alarm Handling and Alarm Management 
 Graphical Interface for All Subsystems 
 Data Collection Capabilities 
 Enable Report Management and Interaction with Standard Office Software Packages 
B. HBMS (High-Level BMS): 
 Centralized BMS for the entire airport is the goal of HBMS. 
 The HBMS server application will be installed in a virtual platform (provided by the DCH 
contractor). 
 HBMS is used to monitor all LBMS via the IP network. 
 Central monitoring of HBMS will take place at the Airport Operations Center (APOC) 
and the Airport Crisis Management Center (ACMC). 
 HBMS contractors must ensure that the system performs high -level monitoring, control, 
and management of the LBMS through detailed design and studies. 
 The HBMS should be composed of open, non-proprietary communication protocols (e.g., 
BACnet/IP, LonTalk, Obix, OPC, SNMP, or Modbus/IP). 
 HBMS architecture includes redundant virtual servers located at DC01 and DC02, storage 
space assigned to DCH San, and HBMS workstations located within the airport. 
C. LBMS (Low-Level BMS): 
 LBMS falls under the scope of the MEP consultant. 
 LBMS is an advanced automation and alarm system utilizing DDC (Direct Digital 
Controller) and PLC (Programmable Logic Controller) technology. 

=== PDF PAGE 375 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 374 
D. Interface Between HBMS and LBMS: 
 The scope of the interface between HBMS and LBMS is limited to the Data Outlet 
provided by the SCS (Structured Cabling System).  
 The LBMS provider is responsible for identifying and listing the DI/DO (Digital 
Input/Output) points for automation and control purposes.  
 The HBMS contractor is responsible for working with the SCS and DNE contractors to 
ensure the proper placement of data outlets. 
 
 Routine Inspection 
Routine inspections focus on maintaining the operational readiness and reliability of the refueling 
facility. These inspections are conducted regularly to identify potential issues and ensure compliance 
with performance and safety standards. 
 
Description 
Routine inspections for the Building Management System (BMS) are focused on ensuring that the 
system operates within the established performance parameters and complies with legal or regulatory 
standards. These inspections typically include verifying the c orrect operation of sensors, alarms, 
communication systems, and data logging functions. They aim to identify any anomalies or 
performance deviations that could affect the overall functioning of the BMS or compromise its ability 
to monitor critical infrastructure. Routine inspections are also essential to meet regulatory and safety 
compliance, ensuring that all monitoring systems are operational and meet the required legal 
standards.

 Preventive Maintenance 
Preventive maintenance focuses on proactively monitoring the condition of refueling facility 
components to detect potential issues before they lead to failures. This approach leverages real -time 
data and advanced diagnostic tools to optimize maintenance schedules and enhance operational 
reliability. 
 
Description 
Preventive maintenance for the BMS is focused on proactively preventing equipment failures or 
system breakdowns by performing regular tasks designed to maintain optimal system performance. 
Unlike routine inspections, which are primarily aimed at verifying compliance and performance, 
preventive maintenance focuses on anticipating potential failures before they occur. This includes 
tasks like cleaning, lubricating, recalibrating sensors, and updating system software. The goal is to 
reduce the likelihood of unexpected system malfunctions, improve system reliability, and extend the 
lifespan of key components in the BMS.

 Corrective Maintenance 
Corrective maintenance is performed reactively to address failures or malfunctions within the 
refueling facility. The goal is to restore the affected equipment or system to operational condition as 
quickly and efficiently as possible while minimizing downtime and ensuring safety. 
 
Description 
Corrective maintenance for the Building Management System (BMS) is performed reactively to 
address failures, malfunctions, or issues within the system that affect its monitoring, control, or 
reporting capabilities. The goal is to restore the affected equipment or system to its operational state 
as quickly and efficiently as possible while minimizing downtime and ensuring safety. This 
maintenance requires immediate action to resolve critical issues, whether due to sensor failures, 
communication disruptions, or data logging problems.

3.8 Concept of IT Application for Maintenance Management 
The Facility Maintenance Function  within the Airport Management System (AMS)  at Long 
Thanh International Airport (LTIA) plays a vital role in optimizing the performance of airport 
facilities and systems. By systematizing maintenance procedures, AMS helps minimize operational 
costs while ensuring the highest level of efficiency. The propose d system will allow LTIA to 
streamline its maintenance workflows, optimize resources, and enhance asset performance. 
 
Fig 51 Airport Management System (AMS) Concept for maintenance 
 
A. Systematic Implementation of Maintenance Procedures 
AMS will systematize maintenance procedures, including preventive maintenance , 
corrective maintenance, asset management, and inspection management. This will ensure 
optimized performance and minimize costs through efficient operations, early identification 
of potential issues, and proactive problem-solving. 
B. Optimized Facility Operation through System Management 
AMS will provide information of all facilities, ensuring that preventive inspections and 
maintenance plans are automatically generated. This will help maximize the operational 


=== PDF PAGE 379 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 378 
efficiency of LTIA’s infrastructure by reducing downtime and addressing potential failures 
before they disrupt airport operations. 
C. Minimization of Operating Costs 
By enabling efficient asset lifecycle management and tracking maintenance histories, AMS 
will assist in minimizing operating costs. This maintenance functions will allow the system to 
anticipate when equipment will need repairs or replacement, reducing bot h unplanned 
maintenance and resource wastage. 
 
Facility Maintenance Function Based on Incheon Airport’s Standards 
Incheon Airport already adheres to many global standards in facility management, and LTIA’s 
AMS can take inspiration from these best practices. The following elements will be adapted for 
LTIA: 
D. Maintenance Management: Incheon Airport is known for its regular inspections and 
preventive maintenance processes. LTIA’s AMS will automate these processes, ensuring 
efficiency and reducing human error. 
E. Cost Optimization and Efficiency: Incheon has implemented preventive maintenance, 
corrective maintenance and inspection to reduce operational costs. LTIA will integrate 
similar capabilities in AMS, improving resource allocation and cutting costs. 
F. System Integration and Digitalization: Incheon Airport integrates smart systems that allow 
for real-time monitoring and efficient facility management. LTIA’s AMS will incorporate 
similar digitalization processes, centralizing facility data and improving op erational 
decisions.

 Defect Management for Critical Facilities 
 
Defect Management for Critical Facilities 
 
Objective 
This process ensures that defects or issues in critical facilities are identified, documented, and 
addressed promptly to maintain the overall quality and safety of LTIA's infrastructure. 
 Scope 
A. Registration and management of defects in critical facilities 
B. Work order creation for defect repair 
C. Logging of repair results and system updates 
Process Overview 
A. Facility Maintenance Team 
 The team registers defects related to critical facilities in the Defect Management Master 
and updates the system with relevant details. 
B. Defect Inspection and Logging 
 A Defect Inspection is conducted to assess the severity and nature of the issue. Results 
are logged into the system for further action. 
C. Defect Repair Work Order 
 Based on the inspection, a Work Order is issued for necessary repairs to be conducted, 
including materials and labor required for the job. 
D. Repair Work Completion 
 The repair work is completed, and the results are logged in the system. The work is then 
marked as completed and signed off by the relevant supervisor. 
Key Management Areas 
A. Defect registration and classification 
B. Inspection results and repair details 
C. Work order for defect resolution and completion status

3.9 Information flow  
 
Information flow 
 
 Asset Registration and Inspection Planning 
To register all assets within the facility/system and plan regular inspections to monitor their 
condition and performance. 
A. Process: 
 Asset Registration: All assets (e.g., buildings, mechanical equipment, electrical systems, 
drainage systems, etc.) are registered in the Asset Management System (AMS), including 
details such as location, type, condition, installation year, and maintenance history. 
 Inspection Schedule: Regular inspection schedules are developed for each asset based on 
its criticality, usage, and regulatory requirements. These schedules are recorded in the 
AMS. 
 Inspection Planning: Inspection teams are assigned specific inspection tasks according to 
the schedule, with checklists tailored to each system to assess performance, safety, and 
potential risks. 


=== PDF PAGE 388 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 387 
 Inspection Execution 
To carry out regular inspections of facilities and systems to detect issues before they lead to major 
problems. 
A. Process: 
 Inspection Teams: Inspection teams (internal or external) conduct inspections based on 
detailed checklists focusing on safety, structural integrity, and operational efficiency. 
 Real-Time Data Entry: Inspection results, including identified issues or abnormalities, 
are recorded in real-time using mobile devices or digital forms, directly updated in the 
AMS for tracking. 
 Inspection Reports: After completing inspections, detailed reports are generated and 
sent to relevant departments (e.g., maintenance, operations, engineering) for follow -up 
actions. 
 Maintenance Request and Assignment 
To generate maintenance tasks based on inspection findings and assign them to appropriate teams 
for resolution. 
A. Process: 
 Work Orders: If issues are detected during inspections (e.g., leaks, broken equipment, 
or system failures), work orders are automatically created in the AMS and assigned to 
the maintenance team for corrective actions. 
 Prioritization: Work orders are prioritized according to severity (e.g., safety hazards or 
operational disruption) and the criticality of the system components (e.g., essential 
machinery, electrical systems). 
 Resource Allocation: The AMS assigns necessary resources such as spare parts, tools, 
manpower, and equipment to the relevant maintenance teams. 
 Maintenance Execution and Documentation 
To execute maintenance tasks efficiently and document all activities for future reference. 
A. Process: 
 Maintenance Execution : The maintenance team  or contractors perform necessary 
repairs, replacements, or cleaning tasks. This can include activities such as system repairs, 
part replacements, system calibration, or surface repairs. 

=== PDF PAGE 389 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 388 
 Real-Time Tracking: Maintenance progress is tracked in AMS, including materials used, 
time spent, and completion status. 
 Post-Maintenance Testing: After repairs, systems undergo post-maintenance testing 
to ensure proper functionality. Test results are recorded in the AMS to verify that the 
systems are restored to acceptable operational conditions. 
 Reporting and Feedback 
To evaluate the effectiveness of maintenance activities and collect feedback for continuous 
improvement. 
A. Process: 
 Work Completion Reports : After completing maintenance tasks, the maintenance 
team submits work completion reports  in the AMS, detailing the work performed, 
materials used, and time spent. 
 Feedback Collection: Feedback is collected from both the maintenance team and the 
asset owner (e.g., facility management) on the quality of work, response time, and system 
performance post-repair. 
 Report Sharing: Maintenance reports and feedback are shared with management and 
relevant departments for review and evaluation of the work's effectiveness. 
  
 Follow-Up and Continuous Improvement 
To continuously improve the maintenance process and enhance the performance of facility systems. 
A. Process: 
 Performance Review: Maintenance reports and inspection data are reviewed periodically 
to assess the effectiveness of the maintenance activities and identify any recurring issues 
(e.g., frequent repairs, recurring system failures). 
 Root Cause Analysis: If recurring issues are identified, root cause analysis (RCA) is 
conducted to determine the underlying causes (e.g., poor material quality, inadequate 
installation, design flaws). 
 Process Improvements: Based on the findings, improvements are made to the 
maintenance process. For example, material upgrades, changes in maintenance schedules, 
or better monitoring technologies may be implemented.

3.10 List of Procedures   
 Standard Operation Procedures (SOP) 
 These are routine procedures ensuring consistent and efficient operations under normal conditions: 
Definition 
• Standard Operation Procedures (SOP) are structured, routine procedures designed to ensure 
the consistent and safe operation, inspection, and maintenance of airport facilities and systems. 
SOPs outline step -by-step tasks, responsible parties, required tools, safety precautions, and 
reporting protocols for regular operations under normal conditions. 
Description 
• SOPs serve as the foundation of effective maintenance management by providing 
standardized guidelines for daily, weekly, and periodic maintenance tasks across all facility 
systems—such as electrical, HVAC, BHS, and ICT. These procedures promote operational  
efficiency, compliance with regulations, and minimize human error. SOPs are developed in 
alignment with international standards and local regulations and are continuously reviewed 
and updated based on operational performance, audit results, and technological changes. 
 
A. Building Maintenance 
 Inspection Procedures: 
o Exterior & Interior: Conduct biannual inspections for cracks, leaks, rust, and wear on 
external and internal walls, windows, and ceilings. 
o Roof: Inspect for water damage, cracked tiles, or signs of wear, and clean gutters 
quarterly. 
o Foundation: Perform structural integrity tests and look for signs of shifting or 
cracking annually. 
 Maintenance Procedures: 

=== PDF PAGE 391 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 390 
o Crack Filling: Use appropriate materials to fill cracks in walls, ceilings, and flooring. 
o Painting & Coating: Reapply paint to prevent weather damage every 2-3 years. 
o Cleaning & Sealing: Clean exterior surfaces and reseal windows and doors as needed. 
 
B. Civil Works 
 Inspection Procedures: 
o Pavements: Inspect runways, taxiways, and aprons for cracks, erosion, or 
displacements at least quarterly. 
o Stormwater Drains : Inspect drainage systems monthly for blockages or debris 
buildup. 
o Signage & Markings: Check for visibility and wear on airfield and ground markings 
quarterly. 
 Maintenance Procedures: 
o Crack Sealing: Seal cracks on runways or taxiways using epoxy or other durable 
materials. 
o Repairs: Patch damaged sections of road or pavement. 
o Cleaning Drains: Regularly remove debris and inspect pipe integrity. 
 
C. Electrical System 
 Inspection Procedures: 
o Electrical Panels: Inspect panels for overheating, tripped breakers, or worn wiring 
every 6 months. 
o Wiring & Cables: Inspect and test wiring integrity for visible wear or signs of wear 
and tear annually. 
o Emergency Power Supply : Test backup generators /UPS and batteries monthly to 
ensure they are ready in case of emergency. 
 Maintenance Procedures: 
o Replace Worn Parts: Replace worn cables or connectors immediately. 
o Circuit Testing : Perform voltage and current checks to ensure all circuits are 
functioning within acceptable parameters. 

=== PDF PAGE 392 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 391 
o Generator Service: Conduct an annual servicing of backup generators, ensuring fuel 
quality and functionality. 
 
D. HVAC System 
 Inspection Procedures: 
o Filters: Inspect filters for dirt and blockages every month. 
o Coils & Fans: Inspect coils and fans for corrosion or dust buildup annually. 
o Air Quality: Check CO2 levels and airflow across the terminal quarterly. 
 Maintenance Procedures: 
o Replace Filters: Replace filters quarterly and clean the ducts semi-annually. 
o Clean Coils & Fans: Clean or replace HVAC coils annually and ensure fan blades 
are clear of debris. 
o Refill Refrigerant: Refill refrigerant and check the HVAC system for any leaks. 
 
E. Plumbing System 
 Inspection Procedures: 
o Water Supply Lines:  Inspect visible supply pipes for leaks, corrosion, or damage 
monthly. 
o Drainage Lines: Check for blockages, slow drainage, or odors in drainage systems 
monthly. 
o Valves and Pumps:  Inspect isolation valves, backflow preventers, and booster 
pumps quarterly. 
o Water Meters & Pressure Gauges: Verify readings and functionality quarterly. 
 Maintenance Procedures: 
o Clean Strainers & Aerators:  Clean faucet aerators and strainers in water fixtures 
quarterly to maintain pressure and hygiene. 
o Flush Systems: Flush domestic water lines and reclaim water systems semi-annually 
to remove sediment and biofilm. 
o Descale & Disinfect Tanks:  Descale and disinfect water storage tanks annually to 
prevent microbial growth.

G. Navigation Facilities 
 Inspection Procedures:

 Irregular Operation Procedures (IOP) 
These address disruptions requiring prompt and temporary measures to minimize impact: 
 
Definition 
• Irregular Operation Procedures (IOP) refer to the predefined processes applied when 
abnormal conditions arise in airport facilities or systems that do not pose an immediate threat 
to safety but may disrupt normal operations. These include minor malfunctions, early signs 
of equipment degradation, or unplanned maintenance needs. 
Description 
• IOPs are implemented to proactively address signs of irregularities before they escalate into 
emergencies. Such procedures involve the detection of anomalies through monitoring 
systems, classification of severity, temporary operational adjustments, and pro mpt 
coordination between maintenance teams and the APOC. The goal is to maintain operational 
continuity while resolving issues efficiently and preventing service disruptions. 
 
A. Building Maintenance 
 Inspect and temporarily seal cracks or leaks . If significant damage is found, use 
temporary barriers to protect the affected area and schedule repairs. 
 
B. Civil Works 

=== PDF PAGE 400 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 399 
 Redirect traffic from damaged or cracked pavement . Use temporary road signs and 
cones, and initiate patching or sealing to restore safe traffic flow. 
 
C. Electrical System 
 Switch to backup power sources  if there is a partial power failure, and isolate the 
affected area to prevent further disruption. Notify the maintenance team for quick 
diagnostics. 
 
D. HVAC System 
 Adjust HVAC system settings  temporarily if temperature or airflow deviates from 
desired levels, and inspect filters and vents for blockages. 
 
E. Plumbing System 
 Adjust control valves or booster pump settings temporarily in response to localized water 
pressure drops, and inspect for partially closed valves or filter blockages. 
 
F. Fire Fighting System 
 Reset fire alarm system  and verify its functionality if a false alarm is triggered, and 
inspect the sensors or equipment for faults. 
 
G. Navigation Facilities 
 Activate backup navigation aids in case of partial system failure, and notify air traffic 
control to adjust operations accordingly. 
 
H. AGL (Airfield Ground Lighting) Facilities 
 Activate backup lighting if airfield lights malfunction, and use manual systems to direct 
traffic until the issue is resolved. 
 
I. ICT Systems 
 Switch over the backup system  to avoid system slowdowns, and to handle critical 
operations until performance is restored. 

=== PDF PAGE 401 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 400 
 
J. Baggage Handling System (BHS) 
 Use manual overrides to clear baggage jams, and divert baggage to alternate routes while 
resolving the issue in the system. 
 
K. Security Systems 
 Redirect surveillance to operational cameras in case of CCTV failure, and use manual 
checks for security until the system is restored. 
 
L. Elevator, Escalator, and Moving Sidewalk 
 Redirect passengers to alternative routes if escalators or elevators malfunction, and use 
manual overrides to resume operations temporarily. 
 
M. Aircraft Stand Equipment System 
1. VDGS (Visual Docking Guidance System) 
 Redirect aircraft to manual guidance if VDGS displays incorrect information, and use 
ground crew assistance to guide the aircraft to the stand. 
2. PBB (Passenger Boarding Bridge) 
 Redirect passengers to an alternative boarding point if the PBB cannot fully extend, and 
use mobile staircases for boarding. 
3. PCA (Pre-Conditioned Air) 
 Redirect airflow manually or adjust temperature settings if PCA fails to regulate air 
properly, and use auxiliary cooling systems. 
4. PWS (Potable Water Supply) 
 Redirect aircraft to an alternative stand with a functional water supply if there is a 
disruption in potable water delivery, and provide bottled water to passengers. 
5. IASS (Integrated Aircraft Stand System) 
 Switch to manual operation for affected systems, and redirect aircraft to a standby system 
until the issue is resolved. 
6. GPU (Ground Power Unit) 
 Redirect aircraft to a different GPU or use an alternative power source, and notify ground 
operations of the power issue. 

=== PDF PAGE 402 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 401 
 
N. Aircraft Fuel Filling 
 Switch to alternate fuel dispensers  if a fuel dispensing system fails, and resolve the 
issue promptly to continue operations. 
 
O. GSE Fueling Station 
 Respond to a dispensing pump or nozzle failure by switching to the standby unit if 
available, isolating the faulty component, and notifying maintenance to ensure 
uninterrupted fueling operations. 
 
P. Sewage Treatment Plant (STP) 
 Activate backup systems (e.g., backup pumps or filtration units) if flow disruptions or 
blockages occur, and restore normal operation quickly. 
 
Q. BMS (Building Management System) 
 Switch to manual operation  for systems affected by BMS malfunction (HVAC, 
lighting), and alert technical support for system restoration. 
 
 Emergency Operation Procedures (EOP) 
These outline actions for critical situations requiring immediate resolution to restore safety and 
operations: 
 
Definition 
• Emergency Operation Procedures (EOP) are immediate response protocols activated when a 
critical failure or incident in airport facilities or systems poses a significant threat to human 
safety, system integrity, or overall airport operations. This includes events such as electrical 
fires, major system outages, flooding, or hazardous material leaks. 
Description 
• EOPs ensure rapid and coordinated actions to contain and resolve emergencies. These 
procedures include immediate isolation of affected systems, activation of emergency response

Long Thanh International Airport

teams, engagement with the Airport Crisis Management Center (ACMC), and coordination 
with external agencies such as fire services or utility providers. The procedures aim to mitigate 
risk, restore functionality, and safeguard passengers, staff, and airport infrastructure.

Cracks, Ceiling Damage)

 Key Performance Index (KPI)  
The key performance indicators (KPIs) proposed for Long Thanh International Airport aim to ensure 
the effective maintenance and sustainable operation of its facilities. These include maintaining critical 
system availability at 95% or higher to support seamless airport operations. Response time for 
resolving maintenance issues, particularly for critical failures, should remain within acceptable limits, 
such as under 30 minutes. Compliance with both Vietnamese regulations and international safety and 
operational standards is essential, with a target of maintaining a 100% compliance rate. Energy 
efficiency should also be prioritized by monitoring energy consumption and implementing initiatives 
to reduce overall usage through optimized equipment and operational practices. Lastly, customer 
satisfaction will be measured through regular stakeholder feedback, ensuring continuous 
improvements in the quality and responsiveness of maintenance services. These KPIs collectively 
contribute to Long Thanh Airport’s commitmen t to operational excellence, safety, and long -term 
sustainability. 
4.1 Building maintenance 
 Maintenance Efficiency 
Maintenance efficiency focuses on ensuring that all scheduled tasks are completed on time and within 
the allocated resources. By optimizing response times and task execution, this category aims to 
minimize disruptions to airport operations and maximize the reliability of architectural facilities. 
 
A. Percentage of Scheduled Maintenance Completed on Time 
 Formula: (Number of scheduled tasks completed on time / Total scheduled tasks) × 100 
 Target: 100% 
 
B. Average Response Time for Emergency Repairs 
 Formula: Total time taken to respond to emergency issues / Number of emergency issues 
 Target: < 1 Day 
 

=== PDF PAGE 409 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 408 
 Structural Integrity 
This category emphasizes the importance of maintaining the structural and non-structural components 
of the airport to ensure safety, durability, and aesthetic quality. Regular inspections and timely repairs 
help prevent major defects and ensure that all facilities meet regulatory and operational standards. 
 
A. Percentage of Structural Inspections with No Major Defects 
 Formula: (Number of inspections with no major defects / Total inspections) × 100 
 Target: 90% 
 
 Safety and Compliance 
Safety and compliance metrics ensure that all maintenance activities adhere to established safety 
protocols and regulatory standards. This category aims to minimize risks to passengers, staff, and 
stakeholders while maintaining a safe and legally compliant environment. 
 
A. Compliance Rate with Safety and Regulatory Standards 
 Formula:(Number of compliant inspections / Total inspections) × 100 
 Target: 100% 
 
4.2 Water Supply & Drainage System 
A. System Availability (%) 
 Formula: (Operating time of the system / Total scheduled operation time) × 100 
 Purpose: Ensure consistent water delivery and drainage functionality to support airport 
operations. 
 Target: Execute ≥95%

B. Failure Reporting and Response Compliance Rate 
This category maintains the operational stability of the airport ICT system by ensuring 
rapid reporting and compliance with recovery times during system failures. 
 Failure Initial Reporting Compliance Rate 
 Formula: (Faults reported within 30 minutes / Total faults) × 100%. 
 Target: 100% of faults reported within 30 minutes of occurrence. 
 
C. Failure Recovery Time Compliance Rate 
 Target: Meet recovery time targets for ≥95% of faults by severity level: 
o Level A (Critical): ≤1 hour. 
o Level B (High): ≤2 hours. 
o Level C (Medium): ≤3 hours. 
 Measurement: (Faults recovered within target time / Total faults) × 100%. 
D. System and Equipment Availability Rate 
This category maintains and improves the availability of airport ICT systems to provide 
stable services without interruptions. 
 System Availability Rate 
 Target: Maintain ≥95% availability for all ICT systems. 
 Formula: (Operational uptime / Total operational hours) × 100%. 
4.10 Baggage Handling System 
A. Preventive Maintenance and Inspection Compliance 
 Formula: (Number of actual inspections and maintenance completed / Total number of 
scheduled inspections and maintenance) × 100% 
 Target: 100% 
 Purpose: To ensure that all preventive maintenance and inspections are carried out as 
scheduled, minimizing the risk of system failures and ensuring the baggage handling 
system operates at its optimal efficiency. 
B. Equipment Availability Rate 

=== PDF PAGE 414 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 413 
 Formula: (Total operating time - Downtime) / Total operating time × 100% 
 Target: Execute ≥95% 
o Exceptions include pre-agreed work or scheduled maintenance activities, or failures 
due to external factors. 
 Purpose: To measure and maximize the availability and uptime of the baggage handling 
system, ensuring efficient and continuous operation with minimal downtime, which is 
crucial for airport operations. 
4.11 Security System 
A. Security Screening Equipment Availability Rate 
 Formula: (Total operating time - Downtime) / Total operating time × 100% 
* Calculate the rate for each unit of equipment (e.g., X-RAY, ETD) and then calculate average value. 
 Target: Execute ≥95% 
o Exceptions include pre-agreed work or scheduled maintenance activities, or failures 
due to external factors. 
 Purpose: To measure and maximize the availability and uptime of the Security Screening 
system, ensuring efficient and continuous operation with minimal downtime, which is 
crucial for airport operations. 
B. System Availability Rate 
 Formula: (Total operational time - Downtime due to failures) / Total operational time × 
100% 
 Target: Execute ≥95% 
o Exceptions include pre-agreed work or scheduled maintenance activities. 
 Purpose: To measure the operational availability of the system, ensuring that the  
Surveillance and Access Control System operates with minimal downtime. This KPI 
reflects the effectiveness of preventive maintenance and the promptness of addressing 
failures. 
4.12 Elevator, Escalator, and Moving Sidewalk 
A. Equipment Availability Rate

Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 414 
 Formula: Operating time / (Operating time + Downtime) × 100% 
 Target: Execute ≥95% 
o Exceptions include pre-agreed work or scheduled maintenance activities. 
 Purpose: To ensure maximum availability and operational efficiency of the elevator, 
escalator, and moving sidewalk systems, minimizing unplanned downtime.  
B. Personal Injury Incidents 
 Formula: Number of major personal injury incidents caused by malfunctioning or 
maintenance-related failures of the elevator, escalator, or moving sidewalk systems. 
 Target: 0 incidents 
o Incidents caused by passenger negligence or fault are excluded. 
 Purpose: To ensure the effective maintenance and safety of the elevator, escalator, and 
moving sidewalk systems by minimizing personal injury incidents caused by equipment 
malfunction or maintenance issues.

 Appendix 
5.1 Glossary of Terms 
Term Definition 
ACB (Air Circuit 
Breaker) 
An electrical device that protects circuits from overcurrent by interrupting 
the flow during faults. 
ACMC The backup facility to APOC, activated during crises to manage airport 
operations. 
AFTN A system for global communication between airports and air traffic control 
for flight processing. 
ANPR A system using optical character recognition to read vehicle license plates. 
APOC The central hub for real -time monitoring and coordination of airport 
operations. 
Apron A designated area where aircraft are parked, loaded, unloaded, or refueled. 
BHS A system for handling baggage from check-in to claim, ensuring tracking and 
security. 
BMS A centralized system managing building functions like HVAC, lighting, and 
security. 
Chiller A mechanical system that cools air or water for terminal or equipment 
temperature control. 
CMMS Software for managing maintenance schedules, operations, and assets. 
CMS Software for centralized control and scheduling of multimedia content. 
Cooling Towers Systems used to expel heat from HVAC or refrigeration systems using water 
circulation. 
DHCP A network protocol that automatically assigns IP addresses to devices. 
DNS A system that translates domain names into IP addresses. 
Drainage System A system of pipes and channels to collect and transport stormwater, 
preventing airport flooding. 
DRM A technology to protect digital content from unauthorized use. 
DR Center A backup IT facility for continuing operations during disasters. 
EPG On-screen TV guide showing program schedules. 
EMS A platform for managing infrastructure or network components. 
EPMS A system managing software patches and updates (may have meant "PMS"). 
EVIDS Digital signage for displaying flight, wayfinding, or promotional info in 
terminals. 
Fire Hydrant System A water network installed to help control fires on airport premises. 

=== PDF PAGE 420 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 419 
F/W (Firewall) A network security device controlling traffic based on rules. 
GSE Ground equipment used to support aircraft operations on the ground. 
GPU A system that supplies power to parked aircraft, replacing APU use. 
HA A system design approach ensuring continuous operation and minimal 
downtime. 
HVAC A system that controls heating, ventilation, and air conditioning for indoor 
comfort. 
IASS An integrated system supporting fault detection and equipment 
synchronization at aircraft stands. 
IB A middleware system facilitating communication between applications. 
IPS A real-time security system that analyzes and blocks threats. 
IP PBX A private branch exchange system using IP networks to manage calls. 
IR Camera A camera that captures images using infrared light in low/no-light 
environments. 
LPR Technology capturing and processing vehicle license plate numbers. 
MATV A system for centralized distribution of TV signals. 
MTC A manual toll collection system with human operators. 
MSO A central facility for mobile call switching and routing. 
MTBF A metric estimating average time between hardware or system failures. 
MTS End-user equipment in mobile telecommunications. 
NAC A network security solution controlling device access. 
NFC A short-range wireless tech for exchanging data between nearby devices. 
NMS A system for monitoring and managing network performance and faults. 
OTDR A device testing the integrity of fiber optic cables. 
Parking Service Road A road providing vehicle access to ground service parking areas. 
PAPI A visual aid that helps pilots maintain the correct approach path during 
landing. 
PCA A system that supplies conditioned air to parked aircraft to maintain cabin 
comfort. 
PLC An industrial computer used for automating electromechanical processes. 
PM User access level and permission management within a system. 
PMS A system managing software updates and patches. 
PST A traditional circuit-switched telephone network. 
PWS A system providing potable water to aircraft via fixed points. 
QoS A network function that prioritizes traffic types for performance. 
RFID A wireless tech using radio waves to identify and track tagged objects. 
Runway A paved area used for takeoff and landing of aircraft. 

=== PDF PAGE 421 ===
Long Thanh International Airport 
 
ConOps 7. Facility/system Management and Maintenance 
 
Long Thanh International Airport (LTIA)  P a g e  | 420 
SAC Systems regulating physical or logical access to restricted areas. 
SAN A high-speed network for block-level storage access. 
Smoke Exhaust System A system to ventilate smoke and heat during a fire emergency. 
SSO A user authentication method enabling access to multiple systems with one 
credential set. 
STB A device decoding digital TV signals for display. 
STP A facility that treats sewage to meet environmental discharge standards. 
Substation A facility that steps up or down voltage to ensure power supply stability. 
Taxiway A path for aircraft to move between runways, aprons, and terminals. 
TMS Systems monitoring and controlling vehicle or data traffic. 
Transformer A device that increases or decreases voltage in power transmission. 
TRS A radio system sharing frequency channels among users for efficiency. 
UPS A backup power system for critical operations during power disruptions. 
UTILITY (UTM) A unified system combining firewall, antivirus, and other security functions. 
VDGS A visual guidance system helping aircraft dock safely and precisely. 
VMS Electronic traffic signs displaying variable information. 
VoD A system allowing users to access video content on demand. 
VoIP A method for delivering voice communication over IP networks. 
WiFi A wireless LAN tech based on IEEE 802.11 standards.
